Functional Specifications Document (FSD)

This document is the official implementation specification for the Jeshan Labs internal portal platform. It covers all 28 application modules across 6 sections, database design for 100+ tables, API contracts, user stories, and 25 mandatory development guidelines that must be followed throughout the project.

1.1

Executive Summary & Project Overview

Strategic context, purpose, and high-level scope

The Jeshan Labs Internal Company Portal is a mission-critical, enterprise-grade web application commissioned by Jeshan Labs Private Limited — a multinational-scale Information Technology organisation — to consolidate all internal operational workflows, HR processes, project execution activities, and administrative functions into a single, secure, and highly responsive digital workspace.

As Jeshan Labs grows in headcount and geographic reach, the proliferation of disconnected spreadsheets, email chains, and isolated tools creates operational inefficiencies, data inconsistencies, and compliance risks. This portal directly addresses those challenges by providing a unified platform accessible to every employee, from individual contributors to C-level stakeholders, through a role-aware interface that surfaces only the capabilities relevant to each person's function.

JL
Portal Code
Jeshan Labs Portal
5K+
Target Users
500 – 5,000 Employees
18+
App Modules
Integrated Workspaces
14
Defined Roles
RBAC-Governed Access
Strategic Objectives
Operational Unification
Replace fragmented tools with a single source of truth for HR, Projects, Finance, IT Services, and Administration.
Security & Compliance
Enforce row-level security, audit trails, and RBAC to meet enterprise data-governance and regulatory requirements.
Employee Experience
Deliver intuitive, mobile-responsive interfaces that reduce friction for employees performing day-to-day tasks.
Scalability & Maintainability
Build on modern, modular architecture that supports future feature additions and horizontal scaling without redesign.
Scope Boundaries
In Scope
  • All 18+ application modules described in Section 1.4
  • Web browser access (Chrome, Firefox, Edge, Safari)
  • Mobile-responsive PWA behaviour
  • REST API backend with role-based data isolation
  • CI/CD pipeline and containerised deployment
  • Real-time notifications via WebSocket
  • Email and in-portal notification delivery
  • Audit logging for all mutating operations
Out of Scope (v1.0)
  • Native mobile applications (iOS / Android)
  • ERP system integrations (SAP, Oracle) beyond API hooks
  • Public-facing customer portals
  • AI/ML predictive analytics features
  • Third-party payroll processor integrations
  • Video conferencing embedded capability
  • Biometric or hardware token authentication
1.2

System Architecture Overview

Technology stack, layers, and infrastructure components

The portal adopts a three-tier, microservice-ready architecture with a React SPA on the presentation tier, a NestJS REST API on the application tier, and a PostgreSQL + Redis persistence tier. All communication is encrypted via TLS 1.3, and the entire stack is containerised for reproducible deployment across environments.

Presentation Tier — Frontend
Technology Version Role in Architecture
React 18.x Component-based UI framework; concurrent rendering for improved UX responsiveness
TypeScript 5.x Static typing for all frontend code; eliminates runtime type errors and improves IDE support
Redux Toolkit 2.x Global state management; slices for auth, notifications, user context, and UI state
React Router v6 6.x Client-side routing with nested routes, lazy-loaded route bundles, and protected route guards
Material UI (MUI) v5 Design system and component library; custom theme aligned to Jeshan Labs brand guidelines
React Query (TanStack) 5.x Server-state management; caching, background refetching, optimistic updates, and pagination
Socket.io Client 4.x WebSocket connectivity for real-time notifications, sprint board updates, and chat feeds
i18next 23.x Internationalisation; all visible strings externalised to JSON translation files per locale
Jest + React Testing Library 29.x Unit and integration tests; component rendering tests, hook tests, and utility function coverage
Cypress 13.x End-to-end automation testing; critical user journeys, regression suites, and accessibility checks
Application Tier — Backend
Technology Version Role in Architecture
Node.js 20 LTS JavaScript runtime; non-blocking I/O ideal for high-concurrency portal workloads
NestJS 10.x Modular MVC framework; controllers, services, guards, interceptors, and pipes for every domain module
TypeScript 5.x Strongly-typed backend code; DTOs, entities, and interfaces eliminate implicit-any bugs
JWT + CSRF Tokens RFC 7519 Stateless authentication via short-lived access tokens (15 min) and long-lived refresh tokens (7 days); CSRF double-submit cookie pattern
bcrypt cost 12 Password hashing with adaptive cost factor; protects credentials at rest against brute-force attacks
Winston Logger 3.x Structured JSON logging with log levels (error, warn, info, debug); daily rotating log files and centralised output
Bull Queue 4.x Redis-backed job queue for async background tasks: email dispatch, payroll processing, report generation, notification fan-out
TypeORM 0.3.x ORM for parameterised queries, entity definitions, migrations, and repository pattern; prevents SQL injection
Data & Persistence Tier
ComponentPurpose
PostgreSQL 15+ Primary relational store; Row-Level Security policies, JSONB columns for flexible attributes, full-text search
Redis 7.x In-memory caching (master data, session state), Bull queue storage, rate-limit counters, WebSocket adapter
PgBouncer Connection pooling between application servers and PostgreSQL; reduces connection overhead under peak load
S3-Compatible Storage Object storage for file uploads (documents, avatars, invoices, attachments); signed URL access control
Infrastructure & DevOps
ComponentPurpose
Docker + Compose Containerise all services (frontend, backend, DB, Redis) for environment parity from dev to production
GitHub Actions CI/CD pipeline: lint → unit tests → build → integration tests → Docker build → deploy to staging/production
Nginx Reverse proxy, SSL termination, static file serving, gzip, rate-limiting headers, CORS policy enforcement
Environment Vaults Secrets injected at runtime via environment variables; no secrets committed to source control

1.3

User Roles Catalog

14 RBAC roles with access scope and key permissions

Access control is governed by Role-Based Access Control (RBAC). Every user is assigned exactly one primary role; additional contextual permissions (e.g. project-scoped manager rights) are applied through the RBAC/Permissions module. The table below defines each role's identity code, functional scope, and key permissions.

# Role Name Role Code Description & Access Scope Key Permissions
01 SASuper Admin SUPER_ADMIN Full system-wide access. Manages all tenants, configurations, and user accounts. Bypasses module-level restrictions. Typically assigned to the technical owner or CTO.
  • Create / delete any user or role
  • View all audit logs (system-wide)
  • Modify global system settings
  • Override any workflow approval
  • Access all modules without restriction
02 IAIT Admin IT_ADMIN Manages system infrastructure, user account provisioning, IT service tickets, and asset inventory. Cannot access HR confidential data or financial records.
  • Create / suspend / unlock user accounts
  • Assign roles and permissions
  • Manage IT service ticket queue
  • View and update asset registry
  • Deploy portal version updates
03 HRMHR Manager HR_MANAGER Oversees all HR operations including recruitment pipeline, onboarding, performance reviews, leave policies, payroll submission, and HR reporting. Full access to employee records.
  • View and edit all employee profiles
  • Approve / reject leave requests
  • Initiate payroll processing run
  • Manage recruitment job requisitions
  • Configure performance review cycles
04 HREHR Executive HR_EXECUTIVE Executes day-to-day HR operations under direction of HR Manager. Creates employee records, assists onboarding, processes leave applications, and generates standard HR reports.
  • Create and update employee profiles
  • Process leave applications
  • Conduct onboarding task tracking
  • Schedule interviews for requisitions
  • Generate HR standard reports
05 EMPEmployee EMPLOYEE Standard access for all full-time and part-time employees. Can view own records, submit requests, access assigned projects and tasks, and use self-service features.
  • View own profile and payslips
  • Submit leave requests
  • View and update assigned tasks
  • Access training materials
  • Submit IT service requests
06 MGRManager MANAGER Team leads and functional managers. Approves leave for direct reports, assigns tasks, views team performance data, and generates team-level operational reports.
  • Approve leave for direct reports
  • View team's tasks and goals
  • Assign tasks to team members
  • Conduct and submit performance reviews
  • View team attendance and time reports
07 PMProject Manager PROJECT_MANAGER Owns project delivery. Full control over project configuration, sprint planning, backlog management, team assignments, risk tracking, milestone definition, and project-level reporting.
  • Create and configure projects
  • Manage sprints, backlog, defects
  • Assign team members to projects
  • Define milestones and risks
  • Generate project analytics reports
08 FMFinance Manager FINANCE_MANAGER Controls all financial data including invoices, billing, payroll configuration, and budget reporting. Approves financial transactions and generates compliance-grade financial reports.
  • Create, approve, and void invoices
  • Configure payroll parameters
  • Approve payroll disbursement
  • View full financial audit trail
  • Generate financial reports
09 FEFinance Executive FINANCE_EXECUTIVE Executes financial operations including invoice creation, billing reconciliation, and payroll preparation. Cannot approve or finalise financial transactions independently.
  • Create and draft invoices
  • Record payments and receipts
  • Prepare payroll for manager approval
  • Run standard billing reports
  • Manage vendor/client billing records
10 RECRecruiter RECRUITER Manages the full recruitment lifecycle from job requisition to offer letter. Can access candidate records, schedule interviews, track pipeline stages, and communicate with applicants.
  • Manage job requisitions (JL-JRQ)
  • View and update candidate records
  • Schedule and record interview feedback
  • Generate offer letters via templates
  • Access recruitment pipeline reports
11 TRNTrainer TRAINER Creates and manages training courses, learning materials, assessments, and certification programs within the LMS module. Can track learner progress across assigned courses.
  • Create and publish training courses
  • Upload materials and assessments
  • Assign courses to employees
  • View and export learner progress
  • Issue training certificates
12 AMAsset Manager ASSET_MANAGER Maintains the corporate asset inventory including hardware, software licences, and physical assets. Processes asset allocation, deallocation, maintenance, and disposal requests.
  • Add, edit, and retire assets
  • Allocate and deallocate assets to employees
  • Log maintenance records
  • Generate asset utilisation reports
  • Process asset request tickets
13 AUDAuditor AUDITOR Read-only access to audit logs, system activity records, financial transactions, and compliance reports. Cannot create, modify, or delete any data. Primarily for internal/external compliance reviews.
  • Read-only access to all audit logs
  • View financial transaction history
  • Export compliance and audit reports
  • View system activity timelines
  • No write or delete permissions
14 GSTGuest GUEST Highly restricted access for contractors, interns, or external collaborators. Can only view publicly shared resources and collaborate on explicitly granted project boards.
  • View shared project boards (if granted)
  • Access public company announcements
  • Submit IT help desk tickets
  • View own profile only
  • No HR, Finance, or admin access
1.4

Application Modules Overview

All 18+ integrated workspaces with descriptions and role access

The portal is structured as a collection of domain-specific modules, each accessible via the main navigation menu based on the logged-in user's role. Modules share common services (authentication, notifications, audit, master data) but are independently maintainable and deployable. The table below summarises each module.

Core Employee Modules
M-01 Employee Dashboard

Personalised landing page for every employee. Displays today's tasks, leave balances, announcements, pending approvals, project status widgets, upcoming meetings, and quick-action shortcuts. Rendered with role-aware widget visibility.

Access: All Roles
M-02 Employee Directory

Searchable company-wide employee directory with profiles, contact information, organisational hierarchy view, reporting chain, and direct messaging links. Sensitive fields (salary, bank details) are masked from non-HR roles.

Access: All Roles (field-level masking)
M-03 Leave Management

End-to-end leave lifecycle: application submission, multi-level approval workflows, leave balance tracking (earned, casual, sick, comp-off), holiday calendar, leave history, and manager/HR reporting. Integrated with payroll deduction logic.

Access: Employee, Manager, HR
M-04 Performance Review & Goals

Structured OKR and KPI management. Employees set quarterly / annual goals; managers conduct mid-cycle check-ins and final reviews with numeric ratings and qualitative feedback. 360-degree peer review capability. Generates performance history timelines.

Access: Employee, Manager, HR Manager
M-05 Notifications Centre

Centralised notification feed driven by system events (task assignments, approvals, mentions, deadlines). Supports real-time WebSocket push, email digest, and in-portal notification bell. Users configure notification preferences per event type.

Access: All Roles
HR & Talent Modules
M-06 Recruitment (JL-JRQ)

Full ATS capability. HR/Recruiter raises Job Requisitions (sequenced as JL-JRQ-00001+), tracks candidate pipeline through stages (Applied → Screening → Interview → Offer → Hired/Rejected), captures interview feedback, and triggers onboarding on hire.

Access: HR Manager, HR Executive, Recruiter
M-07 Onboarding

Guided onboarding workflow for new hires. Generates onboarding task checklists across departments (IT setup, HR document collection, buddy assignment, training enrolment). Tracks completion percentage with deadline alerts for responsible parties.

Access: HR Executive, IT Admin, Manager, Employee (own)
M-08 Training LMS

Learning Management System for internal training programmes. Trainers create courses (video, PDF, quiz), assign to employees or roles, and track completion. Employees earn certificates on passing assessments. Supports mandatory and optional training types.

Access: Trainer, Employee, Manager, HR
Projects Suite Modules
M-09 Projects Dashboard

Portfolio view of all projects the logged-in user is associated with. Displays health indicators, milestone status, overdue tasks count, burn-down progress, and team utilisation. Drill-down links to individual project workspaces.

Access: PM, Manager, Employee (own projects)
M-10 Backlogs

Product and sprint backlog management with user story creation, prioritisation, story-point estimation, and backlog grooming features. Supports epic → story → sub-task hierarchy with drag-and-drop ordering.

Access: PM, Employee (own projects)
M-11 Defect Tracker

Centralised defect / bug management linked to project modules. Captures severity, priority, reproduction steps, screenshots, and fix notes. Supports triage workflows, status transitions (Open → In Progress → Fixed → Closed), and defect aging reports.

Access: PM, Employee, QA roles
M-12 Tasks

Granular task management within projects. Assignees can update status, log time, add comments and attachments, and set due dates. Supports recurring tasks and sub-tasks. Task completion triggers completion notifications to reporter and manager.

Access: PM, Manager, Employee (assigned tasks)
M-13 Team Management

Project team roster with role assignments (Developer, QA, Designer, BA, Architect). Tracks allocation percentage per team member, identifies over-allocation, and provides a team capacity view for sprint planning.

Access: PM, Manager, HR Manager
M-14 Milestones

Project milestone tracker with planned vs. actual dates, milestone dependency mapping, and visual Gantt-style timeline. Milestone completion triggers stakeholder notifications. Overdue milestones are flagged with automated escalation alerts.

Access: PM, Manager, Stakeholders
M-15 Sprints & Sprint Board

Scrum sprint management: create sprints, pull backlog items, run sprint ceremonies (planning, review, retrospective notes). The interactive Sprint Board provides a Kanban-style view (To Do / In Progress / QA / Done) with real-time drag-and-drop updates via WebSocket.

Access: PM, Employee (project member)
M-16 Risks

Risk register per project with likelihood, impact, and severity scoring. Supports risk categorisation (Technical, Resource, Scope, External), mitigation action tracking, and risk trend charts. High-severity risks auto-notify the PM and sponsor.

Access: PM, Manager, Super Admin
M-17 Project Reports

On-demand and scheduled project reports: velocity charts, burn-down/burn-up, defect density, milestone completion rate, team productivity, and resource utilisation. Exportable as PDF and Excel. Report subscriptions deliver via email on schedule.

Access: PM, Manager, Super Admin
Finance & Operations Modules
M-18 Invoices & Billing

Client invoice generation with line items, tax calculations, and payment terms. Tracks invoice status (Draft → Sent → Paid → Overdue). Supports recurring billing, payment reminders, and integration with client records. Full audit trail per invoice.

Access: Finance Manager, Finance Executive
M-19 Payroll

Monthly payroll computation based on salary components (basic, HRA, allowances, deductions, tax). Imports leave deductions and bonus data. Generates payslips (PDF) for employee self-service. Approval workflow before disbursement. Payroll history archive.

Access: Finance Manager, HR Manager, Employee (own payslip)
M-20 Asset Management

Corporate asset registry (laptops, phones, peripherals, software licences, furniture). Tracks allocation lifecycle (Unassigned → Assigned → Under Repair → Disposed). Generates asset utilisation and depreciation reports. Employee can raise asset requests.

Access: Asset Manager, IT Admin, Employee (requests)
Administration & Platform Modules
M-21 IT Services / Help Desk

Employee-facing IT support ticketing system. Employees raise incidents or service requests (hardware issue, software access, VPN, email setup). IT Admin manages the queue with priority SLA tracking. Ticket status updates trigger notifications.

Access: All Roles (raise), IT Admin (manage)
M-22 Workflow Automation

Configurable workflow engine for automating approval chains (leave, payroll, purchase requests). Visual workflow builder for routing rules. Supports parallel approvals, escalation timers, conditional branching, and email action notifications at each step.

Access: Super Admin, IT Admin, HR Manager
M-23 Analytics Dashboard

Executive-level business intelligence dashboard aggregating KPIs across all modules: headcount trends, project delivery metrics, revenue vs. billing, leave utilisation, training completion rates, and IT ticket SLA adherence. Supports date range filters and drill-down.

Access: Super Admin, HR/Finance/Project Managers
M-24 User Management

Centralised user lifecycle management: create, activate, deactivate, unlock, and delete user accounts. Manages password reset workflows, MFA configuration, login history, and session management. Bulk import via CSV for onboarding batches.

Access: Super Admin, IT Admin
M-25 RBAC / Permissions

Fine-grained permission management layered atop the base roles. Configures module-level, feature-level, and record-level permissions. Supports permission inheritance, temporary access grants, and permission audit history. All changes require Super Admin or IT Admin approval.

Access: Super Admin, IT Admin
M-26 Master Data

Centralised master data management for all lookup tables: departments, designations, locations, cost centres, skill taxonomies, leave types, asset categories, project types, and more. Seeded at deployment; maintainable through the admin panel without code changes.

Access: Super Admin, IT Admin
M-27 Library

Internal knowledge library for sharing company policies, SOPs, onboarding guides, technical documentation, and HR handbooks. Organises documents in tagged folders with version history. Employees can search, bookmark, and download documents.

Access: All Roles (read); HR/Admin (manage)
M-28 Audit Logs

Immutable audit trail for all Create, Update, and Delete operations portal-wide. Records actor, timestamp, IP address, action type, changed fields (before/after), and affected entity. Queryable with date range, module, user, and action-type filters. Exportable for compliance.

Access: Super Admin, IT Admin, Auditor
1.5

25 Mandatory Development Guidelines

Non-negotiable standards governing all development work on the portal

Mandatory Compliance. Every pull request must demonstrate adherence to the applicable guidelines below. Code reviews will explicitly verify these standards. Violations are blockers — PRs will not be merged until resolved.

Every screen, component, and layout in the portal must render correctly and remain fully functional across all viewport sizes — starting from 320px (small mobile) through 768px (tablet), 1024px (laptop), up to 1920px+ (large desktop). No horizontal scroll bars on any viewport below 1280px.

Implementation approach: Use Bootstrap 5's responsive grid system exclusively. Define breakpoint-aware CSS using MUI's sx prop or custom styled-components media queries. Tables that cannot be abbreviated must convert to card layouts on small viewports. Navigation must collapse into a hamburger/drawer menu on mobile. Touch targets must meet WCAG minimum 44×44px.

Testing requirement: Each Cypress E2E suite must include viewport tests at 320×568, 768×1024, and 1440×900 resolutions. Lighthouse mobile score must remain ≥ 85.

All route-level components must be wrapped with React lazy() and Suspense to enable code-splitting. Heavy components (charts, rich-text editors, PDF viewers, large data tables) must also be lazy-loaded and not included in the initial bundle.

Performance measures: Images must use loading="lazy" and be served in next-gen formats (WebP/AVIF) from object storage. Lists of more than 50 items must use virtual scrolling (react-window or similar). React.memo must be applied to all pure presentational components. useMemo and useCallback used judiciously to avoid unnecessary re-renders on state updates.

Targets: Initial bundle size < 200 KB gzipped. First Contentful Paint < 1.5 s on a simulated 4G connection. Core Web Vitals LCP < 2.5 s, FID < 100 ms, CLS < 0.1.

All static reference data (departments, designations, leave types, asset categories, project types, skill lists, nationality lists, currency codes, etc.) must be fetched from the backend once at application startup, after successful authentication, and stored in Redux state or React Query's global cache.

Rationale: Fetching reference data repeatedly on every page load wastes bandwidth and creates redundant API calls. A shared Redux slice (masterDataSlice) or React Query cache key (['masterData']) ensures all components read from a single in-memory copy. This data must have a TTL of the user's session — if the session is refreshed, master data is also refreshed.

Implementation: An AppInitialiser component fetches master data in parallel using Promise.all and renders a loading skeleton until ready. Backend endpoint GET /master-data/bootstrap returns a single aggregated payload.

Every application module must maintain its own constants/ folder containing typed TypeScript constant files. Constants are organised by domain: leaveConstants.ts, projectConstants.ts, roleConstants.ts, etc.

Categories of constants: (1) API endpoint paths, (2) status/state enumerations, (3) number sequence prefixes (JL-EMP, JL-PRJ, etc.), (4) pagination default sizes, (5) validation thresholds, (6) UI colour tokens for status badges. All constants must be exported as const objects or TypeScript enum — never as bare string literals scattered in components.

Backend: Backend must mirror this pattern in a shared common/constants/ NestJS module, with enums shared between DTO validation decorators and business logic.

Zero hardcoded user-visible strings are permitted in any React component. Every label, heading, placeholder, validation message, button text, tooltip, and notification message must be referenced via the useTranslation hook from i18next. The default locale is en-IN; the file structure is public/locales/en-IN/<module>.json.

Translation key convention: Keys use dot-notation scoped by module and element, e.g. leave.form.startDate.label, leave.errors.endBeforeStart, common.buttons.save. All new keys must be added to the EN translation file before a PR is raised. Missing keys render the raw key string — automated ESLint rules must flag direct string usage in JSX.

Future readiness: The i18n structure must accommodate at least Hindi (hi-IN) and Tamil (ta-IN) locale files without code changes — only JSON additions.

The codebase must be organised into clearly bounded modules both on the frontend and backend. The frontend follows a feature-folder structure: each feature has its own components/, hooks/, services/, types/, constants/, and pages/ sub-directories.

Reuse policy: Any UI pattern appearing in more than two places must be extracted to src/components/common/. Common hooks (pagination, debounce, form helpers, API error handling) must reside in src/hooks/. Backend NestJS follows module boundaries — each domain is a NestJS module with its own controller, service, DTOs, and entities. Shared guards, interceptors, and decorators live in common/. No copy-paste duplication is permitted — DRY violations are PR blockers.

Tooling: ESLint with no-duplicate-code and custom rules. SonarQube code smell threshold: 0 critical, <5 major per module.

No business data, configuration value, or reference list may be hardcoded inside component source code or backend service logic. Every value that could change between environments (base URLs, feature flags, tax rates, leave quota values, role lists, status labels) must come from: (a) environment variables for infrastructure config, (b) the master data tables in the database for domain reference data, or (c) the application constants files for code-level enumerations.

Examples of violations (blocked): const roles = ['admin', 'hr', 'employee'] inside a component; a hardcoded tax rate of 0.18 in a payroll function; a hardcoded department list in a dropdown. All of these must read from the Redux master data store or a dedicated API endpoint.

Enforcement: Code reviewers check for inline arrays of business labels, numeric magic numbers without named constants, and inline API URLs. ESLint no-magic-numbers rule is enabled.

All data lists — employee directory, task lists, audit logs, candidate pipeline, notification feeds, asset registry — must implement infinite scroll / scroll-to-load pagination. No numbered page navigation buttons unless the business case explicitly requires it (e.g. report grids with known page counts).

Implementation: Use React Query's useInfiniteQuery hook. Backend API endpoints for lists must accept cursor (keyset pagination) or page + limit query parameters and return a nextCursor or hasMore flag. Default page size is 25 records. The Intersection Observer API triggers the next fetch when the user scrolls within 200px of the list bottom. A loading spinner is rendered below the list during fetch; a "You've reached the end" message is shown when hasMore is false.

Performance note: Virtual scrolling must be combined with infinite scroll for lists expected to exceed 500 items to avoid DOM node accumulation.

Unit Tests (Jest + React Testing Library): Minimum 80% code coverage across all frontend modules. Tests must cover: component rendering with different prop combinations, hook logic, Redux slice actions and reducers, API service functions (mocked), utility/helper functions, and validation schema logic. Backend NestJS services must have Jest tests covering all business rules, edge cases, and error paths. Coverage must be reported in CI and gate PRs below threshold.

E2E Automation (Cypress): All critical user journeys must have Cypress specs: login / logout / session expiry, leave application full flow, creating and completing a task, raising an IT ticket, recruitment pipeline stage progression, invoice creation and approval, payroll run. Cypress tests run in CI against a seeded test environment. Flaky tests must be resolved within 2 business days of detection.

Test file placement: Unit tests co-located as ComponentName.test.tsx. E2E specs in cypress/e2e/<module>/. Test utilities and fixtures in cypress/support/ and src/test-utils/.

Data access must be enforced at two levels: (1) PostgreSQL Row-Level Security (RLS) policies that restrict which rows a database role can read/write, and (2) NestJS Guard and Service-layer checks that verify the requesting user's role before any data is returned.

Principle of Least Privilege: Each query must scope its result set to only the data the requesting user is entitled to see. An Employee querying tasks must only receive tasks assigned to or raised by them. A Manager must only see their direct reports' leave requests. HR Executives must not see salary data that belongs to Finance-only scope.

Implementation pattern: All repository queries receive a RequestContext object containing userId, role, and departmentId. WHERE clauses are dynamically extended based on role. Direct database access bypassing the API layer is prohibited. RLS policies are migration-managed and reviewed at each release.

All HTTP requests that mutate state (POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE) must include a valid CSRF token. The portal uses the Double Submit Cookie pattern: the backend sets a csrf_token cookie on login; the frontend reads this cookie value and sends it as an X-CSRF-Token request header on every mutating request. The backend validates header-vs-cookie match before processing.

Mandatory login: Every protected route is guarded by a React ProtectedRoute component that checks for a valid JWT in the Redux auth slice. An expired or absent token redirects to the login page with the original intended URL stored for post-login redirect. The backend applies a global NestJS JwtAuthGuard to all controllers except the auth endpoints (/auth/login, /auth/refresh, /health).

Session inactivity: A 30-minute inactivity timer triggers automatic logout. The refresh token endpoint renews the access token silently while the user is active. On expiry, a "Session expired" modal prompts re-authentication.

All database interactions in NestJS must go through TypeORM repository classes — never raw SQL strings inside service or controller layers. Each domain entity has a dedicated repository class extending TypeORM's Repository<Entity>. Custom query methods are defined on the repository, not in the service.

Base repository: A BaseRepository<T> abstract class provides common operations: findById, findAll(options), create, update, softDelete, and hardDelete. All entities support soft delete via deleted_at timestamp. Hard delete is restricted to Super Admin via a separate endpoint requiring elevated authentication confirmation.

Transaction management: Multi-step operations (e.g. creating an employee + generating JL-EMP sequence + creating onboarding tasks) must be wrapped in a TypeORM QueryRunner transaction. Partial failures roll back atomically.

Every data mutation (Create, Update, Delete) on any entity must automatically produce an immutable audit log entry. This is implemented as a NestJS TypeORM subscriber (AuditSubscriber) that intercepts afterInsert, afterUpdate, and afterSoftRemove events and writes a record to the audit_logs table asynchronously via the Bull queue (to not block the main request).

Audit log fields: id, entity_name, entity_id, action (CREATE / UPDATE / DELETE), actor_user_id, actor_role, ip_address, user_agent, changed_fields (JSON diff of before/after values), timestamp. Sensitive fields (password_hash, bank_account_number) are excluded from the diff payload.

Immutability: The audit_logs table grants INSERT permission only to the audit service database role. UPDATE and DELETE are revoked at the database level. Audit log records are never soft-deleted.

The portal follows Semantic Versioning (SemVer): MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH. The current version string is stored in both package.json and an app_versions database table. Each production deployment creates a new version record with: version number, release date, release notes summary, deployed-by user ID, and environment tag.

Version display: The portal footer and the About modal display the current version. The backend GET /health endpoint returns version in its response. Version is also emitted in log headers for quick identification during incident investigation.

API versioning: The REST API base path is prefixed with /api/v1/. Breaking changes require a new version prefix (/api/v2/) while old versions are maintained for a minimum 90-day deprecation window.

Consistent naming is mandated across the entire codebase:

ContextConventionExample
React ComponentsPascalCaseEmployeeProfileCard, LeaveApprovalModal
Functions & MethodscamelCasefetchEmployeeById, calculateLeaveDays
Constants & EnumsSCREAMING_SNAKE_CASEMAX_LOGIN_ATTEMPTS, LEAVE_STATUS.APPROVED
DB Tables & Columnssnake_caseemployee_profiles, leave_requests, created_at
CSS / SCSS Classeskebab-case.leave-card, .modal-header
Files (components)PascalCase.tsxEmployeeForm.tsx, TaskCard.tsx
Files (hooks/utils)camelCase.tsuseLeaveBalance.ts, formatDate.ts
NestJS Module Folderkebab-caseemployee-profile/, leave-management/

ESLint and Prettier enforce these conventions. Deviation from naming rules triggers a CI lint failure that blocks the PR merge.

Every entity that requires a business-readable identifier must use a JL-prefixed sequential number. The sequence is managed via a dedicated sequence_generators database table with a row per entity type, and a NestJS SequenceService that atomically increments and returns the next value within a database transaction (using SELECT ... FOR UPDATE to prevent duplicate generation under concurrency).

EntityFormatExample
EmployeeJL-EMP-#####JL-EMP-00001
ProjectJL-PRJ-#####JL-PRJ-00001
Job RequisitionJL-JRQ-#####JL-JRQ-00001
InvoiceJL-INV-#####JL-INV-00001
IT TicketJL-TKT-#####JL-TKT-00001
AssetJL-AST-#####JL-AST-00001
Leave RequestJL-LVE-#####JL-LVE-00001
Payroll RunJL-PAY-#####JL-PAY-00001

These identifiers are immutable once generated. They are displayed in all list views, detail pages, notifications, and email communications to enable quick reference across teams.

Frontend: All forms must use React Hook Form with a Yup or Zod schema. Required fields are visually marked with a red asterisk. Submit buttons are disabled until all required fields are valid. Inline error messages appear below each invalid field on blur. Toast notifications are not a substitute for inline validation — both are used in combination.

Backend: All incoming request bodies are validated using NestJS class-validator DTOs. The global ValidationPipe is configured with whitelist: true (strips unknown properties) and forbidNonWhitelisted: true (throws on unknown). Any request failing validation returns a structured 400 Bad Request response with per-field error messages matching the frontend i18n keys.

Validation rules include: required fields, min/max length, regex patterns (email, phone, JL codes), date range logic (end date > start date), numeric range constraints, cross-field conditional rules (e.g. if leave type = "Medical", attachment is mandatory), and file type/size restrictions for uploads (max 10 MB, allowed types: PDF, JPG, PNG, DOCX).

Frontend: All unhandled errors caught by React Error Boundaries and API response errors must be logged using a centralised Logger utility (wrapping console.error in development and a remote logging service in production). The logger emits structured JSON: { level, message, module, userId, timestamp, stack }. console.log calls must be removed before production deployment; ESLint's no-console rule enforces this at the warn level, auto-fixed to use the Logger utility.

Backend: NestJS uses Winston Logger with a global exception filter that intercepts all unhandled exceptions and logs them with full stack traces. HTTP 5xx errors are logged at error level. HTTP 4xx client errors are logged at warn level. Request/response lifecycle is logged at debug level in development. Log files rotate daily and are retained for 90 days.

Alerting: ERROR-level log entries must trigger a notification to the IT Admin Slack channel (or equivalent) within 5 minutes of occurrence via a log monitoring integration.

Any time a user is assigned a task, ticket, approval action, review cycle, training course, or any item requiring their attention, a notification must be triggered automatically. Notifications are not optional and not deferred — they are part of the business logic of the assignment operation, handled via a NestJS NotificationsService called within the same service layer as the assignment.

Notification channels: (1) In-portal: A real-time WebSocket event is emitted to the user's socket room, incrementing the notification bell counter and prepending the notification to their feed. (2) Email: A Bull queue job dispatches a formatted HTML email via the configured SMTP service within 60 seconds of the event. Email content is derived from i18n templates.

Notification types include: Task assigned, task completed (to reporter), leave request submitted (to manager), leave approved/rejected (to employee), IT ticket status changed, sprint item moved to Done, payroll approved, invoice overdue, risk status changed, onboarding task due tomorrow.

All multi-step approval processes must be modelled as configurable workflow definitions rather than hardcoded approval logic. The Workflow Automation module (M-22) provides the engine. Each workflow definition specifies: trigger event, sequence of approval steps, approval roles per step, escalation timeout, conditional branching rules, and completion/rejection actions.

Pre-configured workflows include: Leave approval (Employee → Manager → HR), Payroll approval (Finance Executive → Finance Manager), Asset request (Employee → IT Admin → Asset Manager), Onboarding task sign-off (per department), Invoice approval (Finance Executive → Finance Manager → CEO above threshold), Project creation (PM → Super Admin for budget approval).

State machine: Each in-flight workflow instance has a state (Pending, In-Review, Escalated, Approved, Rejected, Cancelled). State transitions are persisted with actor and timestamp. All state changes produce audit log entries and notifications per G-19.

Every fresh deployment must run a seed script that populates all master data tables with the default reference values required for the portal to function. Seeds are written as TypeORM data-source seed files and run automatically at the end of the database migration pipeline. Seeds must be idempotent — running them multiple times must not create duplicate records (use ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING or upsert strategy).

Seed data includes: Roles and permissions, departments (Engineering, HR, Finance, IT, Sales, Operations), default leave types (Annual, Sick, Casual, Maternity, Paternity, Comp-Off), asset categories (Laptop, Mobile, Monitor, Furniture, Software Licence), project types (Internal, Client-Facing, R&D), ticket categories (Hardware, Software, Network, Access), currency list, country list, skill taxonomy, and sequence generator initial values (starting at 1).

Environment: Different seed profiles for development (includes demo data with ~50 employees and sample projects) and production (reference data only, no PII demo records).

Each application module must maintain its own scoped stylesheet (ModuleName.module.css using CSS Modules, or a module-specific styled-components theme file). Global styles are limited to: CSS reset, root-level CSS custom properties (design tokens), typography base, and MUI theme overrides.

No global class pollution: Class names in module stylesheets are locally scoped via CSS Modules to prevent unintended cascade. A class defined in LeaveManagement.module.css as .cardHeader compiles to a unique scoped name and cannot accidentally affect the Payroll module's .cardHeader.

Theme tokens: Colours, spacing, border-radius, and font sizes are defined in the MUI theme (src/theme/theme.ts) and CSS custom properties in src/styles/tokens.css. Components must consume tokens — hardcoded hex colour values in stylesheets are prohibited (ESLint rule enforced).

Backend: Business logic must reside exclusively in NestJS Service classes (*.service.ts). Controllers are thin orchestrators — they parse the HTTP request, invoke the service, and return the HTTP response. They contain no conditional business logic. Repositories are thin data-access layers — they contain no business rules. This strict three-layer separation ensures each layer is independently testable.

Frontend: Components must not contain business rules or data transformation logic. Business logic on the frontend is handled in custom hooks (useLeaveCalculation, usePayrollSummary) or service utility modules (src/services/leaveService.ts). React components are responsible only for rendering state and delegating user interactions to hooks.

Domain service examples: Payroll calculation logic, leave balance deduction rules, sprint velocity computation, invoice late-fee calculation, and risk severity scoring all reside in dedicated service functions — never in render functions, event handlers, or repository methods.

Every database table that references another table must declare a formal foreign key constraint. Orphaned records (e.g. a task record pointing to a deleted project) are not permitted. Referential integrity is enforced at the database level, not just at the application level.

Cascade rules: ON DELETE behaviour must be explicitly defined for each FK: CASCADE is used where child records are meaningless without the parent (e.g. sprint items deleted when sprint is deleted), RESTRICT where deletion of parent should be blocked if children exist (e.g. cannot delete a department that has active employees), SET NULL where the child record remains valid but the reference becomes optional (e.g. task reporter set to NULL if the user is deactivated).

Migration review: Every migration that adds a new FK is reviewed in code review. Missing FK constraints on new tables are CI-blocking via a custom TypeORM migration linting rule. All FK columns are indexed to optimise JOIN performance.

Every "Create" form in the portal must be designed to capture the complete set of fields the entity can hold — both the minimal required set (mandatory) and the enrichment fields (optional). Skimping on optional fields at creation time leads to incomplete records and creates update overhead later.

Form design standard: Creation forms must display all fields, clearly labelled with mandatory (*) and optional indicators. Optional fields may be collapsed into an "Additional Details" accordion if they create visual overload, but must be accessible in the same form. Fields must not be deferred to a "second step" unless the UX explicitly supports a multi-step wizard pattern that presents all fields across the steps.

FSD requirement: Each module's FSD section must list every field for every creation form, specifying data type, validation rule, whether mandatory or optional, default value if any, and source (free text / dropdown from master data / computed). This ensures developers and QA both have a single source of truth for form completeness.

1.6

Non-Functional Requirements

Quality attributes governing system behaviour beyond functional features

NFR Category Attribute / Metric Target / Acceptance Criteria Measurement Method
Performance API Response Time (p95) ≤ 300 ms for all READ endpoints under normal load (≤ 200 concurrent users). ≤ 600 ms for complex aggregation endpoints. Load testing with k6 or Gatling; p95 latency captured in CI load-test stage.
Page Load Time (FCP) First Contentful Paint ≤ 1.5 s on a simulated 4G network (Chrome DevTools throttled). LCP ≤ 2.5 s. Google Lighthouse CI run on every PR targeting main branch.
Database Query Time No query exceeding 100 ms at p99 on standard list endpoints. Complex reporting queries capped at 2 s with background job offloading above threshold. PostgreSQL pg_stat_statements monitoring; slow query log threshold 100 ms.
WebSocket Event Latency Real-time notification delivery ≤ 500 ms from event trigger to client receipt under 500 concurrent WebSocket connections. Custom latency measurement in Cypress E2E test suite; Socket.io metrics endpoint.
Scalability Concurrent Users Support 500 concurrent authenticated users in phase 1. Architecture must horizontally scale (additional Docker containers / Kubernetes pods) to reach 5,000 concurrent users in phase 2 without architectural rework. Horizontal scale test in staging environment with traffic simulation; PgBouncer connection pool metrics.
Data Volume Database must perform within SLA targets with up to 5 million records per core entity table (employees, tasks, audit_logs). Partition large tables (audit_logs, notifications) by year. Synthetic data load test: seed 5 M rows, run all critical API endpoints, verify p95 latency SLA.
Redis Cache Master data cache hit rate ≥ 95% after app warm-up. Redis memory usage must not exceed 80% of allocated heap; eviction policy is allkeys-lru. Redis INFO command; application-level cache hit/miss counters exposed on /metrics.
Availability Uptime SLA 99.5% monthly uptime (≤ 3.6 hours unplanned downtime per month). Planned maintenance windows limited to off-peak hours (Saturday 23:00–02:00 IST), announced 48 hours in advance via portal notification. Uptime monitoring (UptimeRobot or equivalent) with public status page; monthly availability report.
Disaster Recovery RTO/RPO Recovery Time Objective (RTO) ≤ 4 hours. Recovery Point Objective (RPO) ≤ 1 hour. Daily automated database backups; WAL streaming for point-in-time recovery. Annual DR drill; backup restoration tested quarterly; WAL recovery time measured.
Graceful Degradation If Redis is unavailable, the application falls back to direct database reads without crashing. If the notification service is unavailable, the main transaction still completes with a queued retry for the notification. Chaos testing with tc netem / Toxiproxy injecting Redis and notification service failures.
Security Vulnerability Scanning Zero Critical and High CVEs in production Docker images. Dependency audits (npm audit --audit-level=high) run in CI; findings block merge until resolved. OWASP Top 10 addressed by architecture as detailed in Section 1.7. Snyk or Trivy scanning in CI/CD pipeline; weekly automated dependency audit reports.
Penetration Testing Annual third-party penetration test before each major version release. All High-severity findings must be resolved within 14 days; Medium within 30 days. External pen test report; JIRA tickets for each finding with severity and resolution date tracked.
Authentication Brute Force Account locked after 5 consecutive failed login attempts within 15 minutes. Rate limit: 10 login requests per minute per IP. CAPTCHA triggered after 3 failures. Automated test simulating brute-force; rate-limit headers inspected in Cypress tests.
Maintainability Code Quality SonarQube quality gate: 0 Critical bugs, 0 Critical vulnerabilities, Technical Debt Ratio ≤ 5%, Code Duplication ≤ 3%. Cyclomatic complexity per function ≤ 10. SonarQube scan in CI; quality gate status shown in PR checks. Gate failure blocks merge.
Test Coverage Minimum 80% line coverage on frontend and backend. Coverage report generated in CI on every push; delta coverage must not drop below 80%. Jest --coverage flag; Codecov badge in repository README; PR comment with coverage delta.
Accessibility WCAG Compliance All pages must meet WCAG 2.1 Level AA criteria. This includes: sufficient colour contrast (4.5:1 text, 3:1 UI components), keyboard navigability, skip-to-main-content link, semantic HTML5 landmark elements, ARIA labels on all interactive elements lacking visible text labels. Axe-core accessibility audit in Cypress; Lighthouse accessibility score ≥ 90 on every PR.
Screen Reader Support Core workflows (login, leave application, task creation, dashboard navigation) must be fully operable using NVDA + Chrome and VoiceOver + Safari without visual reference. Manual screen reader testing at least once per sprint for new feature areas; findings logged in accessibility backlog.
1.7

Security Architecture

Defence-in-depth controls protecting the portal at every layer

The portal applies a Defence-in-Depth security model — security controls are implemented at multiple independent layers so that a failure of one control does not expose the system. The following sub-sections describe each security domain, the specific controls implemented, and where they are enforced in the architecture.

Role-Based Access Control (RBAC)

Every API endpoint is protected by a NestJS RolesGuard that evaluates the JWT's role claim against the endpoint's required roles via the @Roles() decorator. Unauthorised role attempts return 403 Forbidden with no information leakage.

Fine-grained permissions beyond role-level are enforced by a PermissionsGuard checking the user_permissions table for feature-level overrides. PostgreSQL Row-Level Security policies enforce data isolation at the database level as a second independent layer.

Layers: NestJS GuardsJWT ClaimsPostgreSQL RLS
JWT + Refresh Token Strategy

Access tokens (JWT, signed RS256) expire in 15 minutes. Refresh tokens are opaque, stored in an HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Strict cookie, and expire in 7 days. Refresh tokens are stored hashed in the database; rotation on every use (Refresh Token Rotation).

On detecting a refresh token reuse attack (same token used after rotation), the backend immediately invalidates the entire refresh token family for that user, forcing re-authentication. This prevents token theft exploitation.

Layers: RS256 SigningHttpOnly CookieToken Rotation
CSRF Protection

Double Submit Cookie pattern. The backend sets a csrf_token cookie (non-HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Strict) on successful login. The frontend Axios interceptor reads this cookie and attaches its value as the X-CSRF-Token request header on all state-mutating requests.

The backend's global CSRF middleware validates header-cookie match before routing the request to the controller. A mismatch returns 403. Safe methods (GET, HEAD, OPTIONS) are exempt. CSRF token is rotated on each login.

Layers: Cookie + HeaderSameSite=Strict
HTTPS-Only Transport

All traffic to and from the portal is encrypted via TLS 1.3 (minimum TLS 1.2 permitted). Nginx terminates SSL and enforces HTTP → HTTPS 301 redirects. Strict-Transport-Security (HSTS) header with max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains; preload is set on all responses.

TLS certificates are managed via Let's Encrypt with automated renewal (Certbot). No self-signed certificates are permitted in production. Internal service-to-service communication within the Docker network does not require TLS as it is isolated, but follows the principle of encrypted secrets in environment variables.

Layers: TLS 1.3HSTS301 Redirect
Rate Limiting & DDoS Mitigation

NestJS Throttler module applies rate limits: 100 requests per minute per IP on public endpoints, 300 requests per minute per authenticated user on protected endpoints. Login endpoint is further restricted to 10 requests per minute per IP. Rate limit exceeded returns HTTP 429 with Retry-After header.

Nginx upstream rate limiting (limit_req_zone) provides a first line of defence at the proxy level. Redis stores rate-limit counters for distributed rate limiting across multiple backend instances. Slow-request detection configured at 10 s threshold.

Layers: Nginx limit_reqNestJS ThrottlerRedis counters
Input Sanitisation & SQL Injection Prevention

All user-supplied input is sanitised server-side using the class-sanitizer library in conjunction with class-validator DTOs. HTML is stripped from all free-text fields using sanitize-html with a strict allowlist. NestJS ValidationPipe with whitelist: true strips properties not declared in the DTO.

TypeORM's parameterised query builder prevents SQL injection by construction — raw SQL is not used. TypeORM's createQueryBuilder uses positional parameters (:param) for all dynamic values. All repository methods undergo quarterly code review specifically for injection vulnerabilities.

Layers: class-sanitizerTypeORM paramsWhitelist DTO
XSS Prevention

React's JSX rendering auto-escapes all dynamic string values injected into the DOM. dangerouslySetInnerHTML is prohibited across the codebase — an ESLint rule flags any usage as an error. Rich-text content (e.g. library documents) is sanitised with DOMPurify before rendering, allowing only a strict subset of HTML tags.

The Content-Security-Policy (CSP) header prevents inline script execution and restricts script sources to the application's own origin. This is a second-layer defence: even if an XSS payload is injected, the CSP prevents it from executing or exfiltrating data to external origins.

Layers: React JSX escapingDOMPurifyCSP Header
AES-256 Encryption for Sensitive Fields

PII and sensitive financial data stored in PostgreSQL is encrypted at the column level using AES-256-GCM via a TypeORM Value Transformer. Fields encrypted at rest include: national ID numbers (Aadhaar, PAN), bank account numbers, salary details, offer letter salary figures, and personal contact numbers beyond work email.

The encryption key is a 256-bit key stored in the environment vault (not the database). Key rotation is performed annually or on security incident. Decryption occurs only within the NestJS service layer — encrypted values are never returned raw from the repository. The database storage engine also uses full-disk encryption (LUKS / cloud-provider managed encryption).

Layers: AES-256-GCM columnsDisk encryptionVault key storage
HTTP Security Headers

All responses from Nginx include the following security headers. Headers are validated in CI using the OWASP Secure Headers Project checker tool.

Header Value / Policy Purpose
Strict-Transport-Security max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains; preload Forces HTTPS for 1 year; prevents SSL stripping attacks
Content-Security-Policy default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: https://cdn.*; font-src 'self'; connect-src 'self' wss:; frame-ancestors 'none' Prevents XSS, clickjacking via frames, and data exfiltration to external origins
X-Frame-Options DENY Prevents embedding in iframes; blocks UI redress / clickjacking attacks
X-Content-Type-Options nosniff Prevents browser MIME-type sniffing; stops content-type confusion attacks
Referrer-Policy strict-origin-when-cross-origin Limits referrer information sent to third parties; protects URL-embedded data
Permissions-Policy camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=(), payment=() Blocks access to browser APIs not required by the portal; reduces attack surface
X-XSS-Protection 0 (disabled; rely on CSP) Disabled intentionally per OWASP guidance — legacy header can introduce vulnerabilities; CSP handles XSS
Cache-Control (API) no-store, no-cache, must-revalidate, private Prevents sensitive API response caching in browser or intermediate proxies
Secrets Management
Environment Variables

All runtime secrets (DB credentials, JWT signing keys, SMTP passwords, S3 keys, Redis auth) are injected via environment variables at container startup. .env files are never committed to source control — .gitignore enforces this with a pre-commit hook that scans for secrets using git-secrets or truffleHog.

A .env.example file with placeholder values documents all required variables. Each deployment environment (dev, staging, production) maintains its own secret store.

Vault Integration (Phase 2)

For production deployments at scale, HashiCorp Vault (or AWS Secrets Manager) integration provides dynamic secret generation, automatic rotation, and fine-grained access policies. The NestJS application authenticates to Vault via a service account token and retrieves secrets at startup, eliminating long-lived static credentials.

Secret access is audited in the Vault audit log; any unexpected access pattern triggers an alert to the IT Admin.

PostgreSQL Row-Level Security (RLS)

RLS provides a transparent, database-enforced data isolation layer independent of application code. Even if an application-level bug bypassed NestJS guards, RLS policies ensure the database only returns rows the connecting role is entitled to see.

Employee Data

Employees see only their own row in employee_profiles. Managers see rows where manager_id = current_user_id. HR roles see all rows within their department. Finance sees salary columns only.

Project Data

Project members see only projects they belong to (via project_members join). PMs see all projects they own. Super Admin sees all projects. Guest role has no access unless explicitly granted.

Financial Records

Invoice and payroll tables have RLS policies restricting access to Finance role database users only. Employees can only see their own payslip row (filtered by employee_id). No cross-employee salary visibility.

Security + Audit Synergy. Every security event — failed login attempt, CSRF token mismatch, access denied (403), RBAC violation, privilege escalation attempt — is written to the security_events log table in addition to the application Winston log. The Audit Logs module (M-28) surfaces these events for the Auditor and IT Admin roles. Patterns of repeated security events trigger automatic alerts to the Super Admin via the notification system (G-19).

2.1 Module 1: Employee Dashboard

2.1.1 Purpose & Overview

The Employee Dashboard serves as the central, personalized landing page for all authenticated users within the Jeshan Labs Private Limited internal company portal. It provides at-a-glance visibility into key HR information, personal data, upcoming events, pending actions, and organizational announcements. The dashboard is role-sensitive — content, widgets, and quick-action buttons adapt dynamically based on the authenticated user role, employment status, and permissions. The goal is to reduce navigation overhead and surface contextually relevant information within a single unified view, improving employee engagement and operational efficiency.

2.1.2 User Roles & Access Matrix

RoleDescriptionPersonal InfoTeam WidgetsApprovalsPost AnnouncementsHR Analytics
EmployeeAll permanent and contract staff (JL-EMP-XXXXX)Own OnlyReadOwn OnlyNoNo
Team LeadSenior employees managing a direct team of 2-15 membersOwn + TeamFullFullNoTeam Only
ManagerDepartment / function head overseeing multiple teamsDept-LevelFullFullDept OnlyDept-Level

2.1.3 Feature Descriptions

Displays a summary card containing: profile photo (with upload/change trigger), full name, Employee ID (JL-EMP-XXXXX), designation, department, reporting manager name, date of joining, employment type (Full-Time / Contract / Intern), and current employment status. An Edit Profile quick link navigates to the full profile management page. Profile completeness percentage is calculated and displayed with a progress bar. Employees are prompted to complete their profiles when completeness falls below 80%.

Displays current month attendance summary: Present days, Absent days, Late arrivals, Half-days, Work-from-home days. A mini calendar highlights attendance status per day using colour codes (Green = Present, Red = Absent, Yellow = Late, Blue = WFH, Gray = Weekend/Holiday). Clock-in / Clock-out action button is available from the dashboard. The widget links to the full Attendance module for detailed logs and regularisation requests.

Displays current leave balances for all leave types: Casual Leave (CL), Sick Leave (SL), Privilege Leave (PL), Compensatory Off (CO), Maternity/Paternity Leave (as applicable). Each leave type shows: Entitled, Availed, Lapsed, and Remaining counts. An Apply Leave shortcut button is present. Pending leave approval requests are listed with status badges.

Displays a prioritised list of items requiring the user attention. For employees: pending document submissions, incomplete onboarding tasks, pending survey responses. For managers/HR: leave approval requests, timesheet approvals, exit clearance approvals, offer approvals, recruitment requisition approvals, performance review submissions due. Each item shows requester name, request type, submission date, and urgency indicator. One-click approve/reject inline actions are available for simple requests.

Displays the latest company announcements in a scrollable card feed. Each announcement contains: title, publish date, category tag (Policy Update / Event / IT Notice / Holiday etc.), author, and a brief excerpt with Read More expansion. HR Executives and Managers with posting rights can create new announcements via a dedicated form. Announcements can be targeted by department or broadcast company-wide. Announcements older than 30 days are archived.

Configurable grid of quick-access icon buttons: Apply Leave, Download Payslip, Raise IT Request, View Holiday Calendar, Submit Timesheet, Employee Directory, HR Helpdesk, Company Policies Library, and up to 3 user-customisable shortcuts. Users can pin/unpin quick links from any module. The panel is role-aware — recruitment links are shown only to Recruiter/HR roles.

A dedicated widget lists today and upcoming (next 7 days) birthdays and work anniversaries of colleagues in the user department and direct reporting chain. Each entry shows: profile photo thumbnail, name, designation, and a one-click option to send a personalised greeting message within the portal. HR receives a consolidated daily digest email every morning. Employees can opt out of birthday visibility in their privacy settings.

A monthly mini-calendar widget displays team-level events: approved leave days (per team member), public holidays, company events, and review cycle deadlines. Hovering/clicking a date reveals a tooltip with event details. Managers see their full team leave in a consolidated view. Employees see only their direct peers approved leave. An Open Full Calendar link navigates to the expanded Team Calendar module. ICS export for Google Calendar / Outlook integration is supported.

2.1.4 User Stories

Story IDRoleUser StoryAcceptance CriteriaPriority

2.1.5 Business Rules

Rule IDRuleDescription
BR-D-001Profile Completeness ThresholdIf an employee profile completeness falls below 80%, a non-dismissible banner prompts the employee to complete their profile. HR is notified after 7 days of inaction.
BR-D-002Widget Visibility — Role-BasedApproval widgets are hidden for roles without approval authority. HR Analytics KPIs are hidden from non-HR / non-admin roles. Quick links adapt to the user module permissions.
BR-D-003Birthday Privacy Opt-OutIf an employee has enabled birthday privacy in their settings, their birthday is NOT shown to peers on the dashboard. HR sees all birthdays regardless of privacy setting.
BR-D-004Announcement TargetingCompany-wide announcements are visible to all active employees. Department-scoped announcements are visible only to employees assigned to that department. Announcements cannot be targeted to individual employees.
BR-D-005Clock-in RestrictionClock-in is only permitted once per calendar day. A second clock-in attempt triggers a validation error with an option to raise an attendance regularisation request.
BR-D-006Pending Approval EscalationIf a leave approval request remains unactioned by a manager for more than 48 hours on a working day, the request is automatically escalated to the manager manager and HR.
BR-D-007Dashboard Data FreshnessLeave balances are fetched in real-time. Attendance summary is refreshed every 15 minutes. Announcements are refreshed every 5 minutes. Team calendar is refreshed on page load.
BR-D-008Inactive Employee AccessEmployees with status Resigned, Terminated, or On Notice Period can still access the dashboard but with limited functionality — no leave applications, no IT requests, read-only profile.

2.2 Module 2: Employee Directory

2.2.1 Purpose & Overview

The Employee Directory is a searchable, filterable catalogue of all active and historical employees of Jeshan Labs Private Limited. It serves as an internal people search tool that enables employees to quickly locate colleagues, understand the organisational hierarchy, and access contact information. The directory integrates with the authentication system to provide role-sensitive profile visibility — certain fields (salary, personal address, health information) are restricted to HR and Management roles. The directory reduces reliance on manual contact lists, supports cross-team collaboration, and serves as the data source for the organisation chart visualisation.

2.2.2 Feature Descriptions

F-DIR-01: Search
Full-text search across first name, last name, employee ID, email, designation, and department. Search results appear instantly (debounced at 300ms). Supports wildcard searches. Search history (last 5 queries) is stored in session for quick repeat access.
F-DIR-02: Filter & Sort
Multi-criteria filters: Department (multi-select), Designation (multi-select), Location/City (multi-select), Employment Type, Status. Sort by: Name (A-Z/Z-A), Date of Joining (recent/oldest), Department. Filters persist across sessions via URL query parameters.
F-DIR-03: Employee Profile View
Clicking any employee card opens a detailed profile page with: profile photo, name, employee ID, designation, department, contact (email/phone — role-gated), reporting manager, date of joining, and employment type. HR-only sections include: salary band, leave balance, performance rating, and documents status.
F-DIR-04: Organisation Chart
An interactive, zoomable hierarchical organisation chart rendered using reporting_manager_id relationships. Supports top-down and left-right view orientations. Each node shows: photo thumbnail, name, designation. Clicking a node expands direct reports. Export as PNG or PDF. Mobile-responsive with pinch-to-zoom.
F-DIR-05: Export
HR roles can export the directory as: CSV (all active employees with configurable column selection), XLSX (formatted with column headers), or PDF (formatted directory booklet). Export respects current filter/search state. Export audit log is maintained. Sensitive fields excluded from non-HR exports by default.
F-DIR-06: Direct Message / Connect
Each employee profile card has a Send Message button that opens the internal portal messaging module pre-addressed to that employee. Integration with Microsoft Teams / Slack (if configured) provides Chat on Teams / Message on Slack deep-link buttons. Email copy-to-clipboard button is available on hover.

2.2.3 User Stories

Story IDRoleUser StoryPriority
US-DIR-001EmployeeAs an Employee, I want to search for a colleague by name or department so that I can quickly find their contact information for work communication.Must Have
US-DIR-002New JoinerAs a New Joiner, I want to browse the organisation chart so that I can understand the company team structure and reporting hierarchy before my first day.Must Have
US-DIR-003HR ExecutiveAs an HR Executive, I want to export a filtered list of all active Engineering department employees to CSV so that I can share the headcount report with the department head.Must Have
US-DIR-004ManagerAs a Manager, I want to filter the directory by my department and sort by date of joining so that I can quickly identify the most senior and most recent team members.Should Have
US-DIR-005EmployeeAs an Employee, I want to view a colleague profile including their designation, team, and manager so that I can route my communication to the right person in a cross-functional project.Must Have
US-DIR-006HR ManagerAs an HR Manager, I want the org chart to update automatically when reporting relationships change so that I do not need to manually maintain a separate chart.Must Have
US-DIR-007EmployeeAs an Employee, I want to click Send Message from any colleague profile card so that I can initiate communication without leaving the portal.Should Have
US-DIR-008System AdminAs a System Admin, I want to configure which profile fields are visible to which roles so that sensitive employee data is not exposed to unauthorised users.Must Have

2.2.4 Business Rules

Rule IDRuleDescription
BR-DIR-001Active Employees Only (Default)By default, the directory shows only employees with status = active. Resigned and terminated employees are hidden unless an HR role applies the Include Past Employees filter.
BR-DIR-002Sensitive Field MaskingThe following fields are visible only to HR roles: personal mobile number, personal email, home address, salary/band, blood group, medical notes. Employees can see their own sensitive fields on their own profile only.
BR-DIR-003Org Chart IntegrityIf an employee reporting_manager_id is NULL (top-level), they appear as a root node. Circular reporting chains are validated at data entry and blocked.
BR-DIR-004Export AuditAll directory exports are logged with: user ID, export timestamp, filter criteria applied, number of records exported, format. Logs are retained for 1 year.
BR-DIR-005Search Minimum CharactersSearch requires a minimum of 2 characters before results are displayed to prevent server overload from single-character queries.
BR-DIR-006Photo DisplayIf an employee has not uploaded a profile photo, a generated avatar using their initials and a deterministic background colour is displayed in the directory.

2.2.5 API Endpoints

MethodEndpointDescriptionAuthRoles

GET/api/v1/employees/exportExport filtered employee list — query params specify filters and format (csv/xlsx/pdf)YesHR Executive, HR Manager, System Admin
GET/api/v1/departmentsList all active departments (used to populate filter dropdowns)YesAll
GET/api/v1/designationsList all active designations (used to populate filter dropdowns)YesAll

2.3 Module 3: Recruitment — Applicant Tracking System (ATS)

2.3.1 Purpose & Overview

The Recruitment module is a full-featured internal Applicant Tracking System (ATS) for Jeshan Labs Private Limited. It manages the complete talent acquisition lifecycle — from raising a job requisition through departmental approval, to publishing job postings, tracking candidate applications across hiring stages, scheduling interviews, collecting structured feedback, generating offer letters, background checks, and ultimately converting a candidate to an employee record in the onboarding module. The ATS eliminates manual email-based recruitment processes, ensures compliance with hiring policies, and provides real-time dashboards and analytics for the Talent Acquisition team.

2.3.2 Hiring Pipeline — Stage Definitions

Applied→ Screening→ Interview Round 1→ Technical Assessment→ HR Interview→ Offer→ Joined or Rejected or Withdrawn
StageActor(s)Key ActionsExit Condition
AppliedSystem (auto)Application received; confirmation email sent to candidate; recruiter notifiedRecruiter reviews and moves to Screening or Rejects
ScreeningRecruiter / HR ExecutiveResume review, phone screen call, initial qualification check against job requirementsShortlisted to Interview; Not suitable to Rejected
Interview Round 1Recruiter + Hiring ManagerSchedule interview, conduct, submit structured feedback formFeedback: Proceed to Technical or Rejected
Technical AssessmentTechnical Interviewer(s)Technical test / live coding / case study, submit technical feedback with scoreScore at or above threshold: HR Interview; Below threshold: Rejected
HR InterviewHR Executive / HR ManagerCulture fit, compensation discussion, background verification initiationApproved: Offer; Not suitable: Rejected
OfferHR Manager / RecruiterGenerate offer letter, obtain management approval, send to candidate, await acceptance/rejection, set joining dateAccepted: Joined (triggers onboarding); Rejected: Close Requisition
JoinedHR / SystemConvert candidate to employee record; trigger onboarding plan creation; send welcome emailTerminal — Onboarding module takes over
RejectedRecruiter / SystemSend rejection email (if configured); record rejection reason; mark application as closedTerminal — candidate remains in pool for future requisitions

2.3.3 User Stories

Story IDRoleUser StoryPriority
US-REC-001Hiring ManagerAs a Hiring Manager, I want to raise a job requisition with department, designation, required experience, and salary range so that HR can formally begin the hiring process with accurate requirements.Must Have
US-REC-002HR ManagerAs an HR Manager, I want to review, approve, or reject job requisitions with comments so that only positions with confirmed budget and business need are published externally.Must Have
US-REC-003RecruiterAs a Recruiter, I want to publish an approved job requisition to multiple platforms (internal portal, LinkedIn, Naukri, Indeed) simultaneously so that I can maximise candidate reach without repeated manual effort.Must Have
US-REC-004RecruiterAs a Recruiter, I want to track all applications in a Kanban-style pipeline view so that I can see the status of every candidate at a glance and identify bottlenecks in the hiring process.Must Have
US-REC-005InterviewerAs an Interviewer, I want to receive a calendar invite with interview details and submit structured feedback through the portal so that feedback is captured systematically and does not rely on informal email chains.Must Have
US-REC-006RecruiterAs a Recruiter, I want to schedule multiple interview rounds for a candidate with different interviewers and track each round status so that I can manage complex interview panels efficiently.Must Have
US-REC-007HR ExecutiveAs an HR Executive, I want to generate an offer letter from a pre-approved template populated with candidate-specific details so that I can produce accurate, consistent offer documents in minutes.Must Have
US-REC-008HR ManagerAs an HR Manager, I want to review and digitally approve the offer letter before it is sent to the candidate so that all offers are authorised at the correct level before commitment.Must Have
US-REC-009RecruiterAs a Recruiter, I want to initiate background verification directly from the portal and track its status so that I can ensure all pre-employment checks are completed before the joining date.Should Have
US-REC-010HR ManagerAs an HR Manager, I want to view a recruitment analytics dashboard showing time-to-fill, cost-per-hire, offer acceptance rate, and source-of-hire breakdown so that I can continuously optimise our talent acquisition strategy.Should Have
US-REC-011CandidateAs a Candidate applying through the internal job board, I want to receive automated status update emails at each stage change so that I stay informed without needing to follow up with the recruiter.Must Have
US-REC-012System AdminAs a System Admin, I want to configure custom hiring stage workflows per department or job type so that the Engineering hiring process can have different stages than Sales hiring.Nice to Have

2.3.4 Business Rules

Rule IDRuleDescription
BR-REC-001Requisition Approval RequiredA job requisition must be approved by the HR Manager before it can be published to any platform. Requisitions in draft or pending_approval status cannot generate postings.
BR-REC-002No. of Positions CapApplications cannot exceed the no_of_positions value on the requisition. When positions are filled, the requisition status automatically changes to closed and no further applications are accepted.
BR-REC-003Duplicate Candidate CheckOn application submission, the system checks for existing candidates with the same email or phone number. If a duplicate is found, the recruiter is alerted and must confirm before creating a new record or merging with the existing candidate profile.
BR-REC-004Interview Feedback MandatoryAn application cannot be moved from an interview stage to the next stage until all assigned interviewers for that round have submitted feedback. A minimum of one feedback record per interview round is mandatory.
BR-REC-005Offer Letter Approval HierarchyOffer letters for CTC above INR 15 LPA require approval from both HR Manager and the Finance Head. Offers below INR 15 LPA require only HR Manager approval. All offers require at least one level of approval before being sent.
BR-REC-006Offer ExpiryOffer letters are valid for a maximum of 10 calendar days from the sent date unless a custom expiry is set. Expired offers automatically change status to expired and the requisition position becomes available again.
BR-REC-007Candidate Data RetentionRejected candidate data is retained for 12 months from the last application date for compliance and re-consideration. After 12 months, personal data is anonymised per data privacy policy.
BR-REC-008Candidate-to-Employee ConversionWhen a candidate application status changes to Joined, the system automatically creates a draft employee record using candidate data. HR must review and complete the record before it becomes active.
BR-REC-009Platform Publishing ControlInternal job postings are only visible to authenticated portal users. External platform publishing requires the posting to be explicitly marked for external publishing and requires the applicable API integration to be configured.

2.3.8 Workflow — State Machines

REQUISITION STATES:
[draft] -- Submit for approval --> [pending_approval]
[pending_approval] -- HR Approves --> [approved]
[pending_approval] -- HR Rejects  --> [draft]  (with comments)
[approved]  -- Publish posting  --> [published]
[published] -- All positions filled --> [closed]  (auto)
[published] -- HR closes manually  --> [closed]
[any state] -- HR cancels          --> [cancelled]

OFFER STATES:
[draft] --> [pending_approval] --> [approved] --> [sent]
[sent]  --> [accepted]  --> triggers Onboarding plan creation
[sent]  --> [rejected]  --> position re-opened on requisition
[sent]  --> [expired]   --> (auto, after expiry_date passes)
[approved] --> [revoked] (before sending — HR decision)
