2.4 Module 4: Onboarding

2.4.1 Purpose & Overview

The Onboarding module manages the structured transition of a new hire from offer-acceptance to full productivity. It automates the creation of personalised onboarding plans from reusable task templates, coordinates pre-joining document collection, IT asset and access provisioning, buddy assignment, orientation scheduling, mandatory compliance training, and probation tracking. All stakeholders — new joiner, HR coordinator, IT team, reporting manager, and buddy — have role-specific dashboards showing their pending onboarding tasks. Completion status is tracked with SLA monitoring to prevent delays in day-one readiness.

2.4.2 Feature Descriptions

F-ONB-01: Onboarding Plan Auto-Creation
When a candidate offer is accepted and the joining date is confirmed, the system automatically creates an onboarding plan by instantiating all active, applicable task templates based on employment type, department, and designation. HR can review and modify the auto-generated plan before it becomes active.
F-ONB-02: Pre-Joining Document Collection
A secure pre-joining portal link is emailed to the new hire before their joining date. They can upload required documents (educational certificates, previous employer relieving letter, identity proofs, bank account details, PF nomination). Document checklist shows upload status, verification status, and HR comments. HR receives notification on each upload.
F-ONB-03: IT Setup Requests
Onboarding plan automatically generates IT setup tasks: laptop/workstation provisioning, email account creation, software licence assignment, VPN access, access card, and project tool access. IT Admin receives a consolidated setup checklist for each new joiner. Each item can be marked complete with optional notes. Dependency ordering ensures email is created before other access provisioning.
F-ONB-04: Buddy Assignment
HR coordinator assigns a buddy — a peer employee in the same department — to each new joiner. The buddy receives an automated introduction email with the new joiner profile and a buddy handbook. Buddy tasks are generated in the onboarding plan: week-1 introduction call, team introduction, first-month check-in. Buddy assignment can be changed by HR up to joining date.
F-ONB-05: Orientation Schedule
HR creates a structured orientation schedule (Day 1 to Day 30 agenda) with sessions: company overview, HR policies walkthrough, IT induction, department introduction, client confidentiality and compliance. Sessions can be individual or group-based. New joiners can view the schedule on their dashboard. Calendar invites are auto-sent for scheduled sessions.
F-ONB-06: Training Assignments
Mandatory onboarding training modules (POSH policy, information security, code of conduct, tool-specific training) are auto-assigned based on department and designation. New joiner completes training via the linked LMS or embedded portal training modules. Completion is tracked and non-completion is flagged to HR and manager before probation review.
F-ONB-07: Checklist Template Management
HR Admin can create, edit, activate, or deactivate onboarding task templates. Templates are tagged by category (document / IT setup / orientation / training / compliance / HR), applicable employment type, and department. Template changes apply to future onboarding plans only. Templates can be cloned and modified for variant use cases.
F-ONB-08: Probation Tracking
Probation records are automatically created for all new hires with a configurable probation period (default 3 months). Manager receives a reminder 30, 15, and 7 days before probation end date to complete a structured review form. HR can extend probation with a documented reason. Confirmed employees date_of_confirmation is auto-set in the employees table upon confirmation decision.

2.4.3 User Stories

Story IDRoleUser StoryPriority
US-ONB-001New JoinerAs a New Joiner, I want to receive a pre-joining portal link so that I can complete document uploads and review my onboarding plan before my first day, arriving prepared.Must Have
US-ONB-002HR ExecutiveAs an HR Executive, I want the system to auto-generate a complete onboarding checklist from templates when a candidate accepts an offer so that I do not need to manually create task lists for every new hire.Must Have
US-ONB-003IT AdminAs an IT Admin, I want a consolidated IT setup checklist for each new joiner arriving in the next 7 days so that I can provision equipment and access before their joining date.Must Have
US-ONB-004BuddyAs a Buddy, I want to receive clear instructions and a task list for supporting the new joiner so that I can fulfil my buddy responsibilities without ambiguity.Should Have
US-ONB-005New JoinerAs a New Joiner, I want to view my Day 1 orientation schedule before joining so that I know where to go, who to meet, and what to expect on my first day.Must Have
US-ONB-006HR ManagerAs an HR Manager, I want to manage onboarding task templates so that the checklist generated for each new hire is always current, accurate, and tailored by department and employment type.Must Have
US-ONB-007ManagerAs a Reporting Manager, I want to receive a probation end date reminder 30 days in advance so that I have sufficient time to conduct a structured review and document my confirmation decision.Must Have
US-ONB-008HR ExecutiveAs an HR Executive, I want to view a real-time onboarding status dashboard for all active new joiners so that I can proactively identify and resolve blockers before joining day.Must Have
US-ONB-009New JoinerAs a New Joiner on Probation, I want to see my remaining probation days and the review date on my dashboard so that I can self-prepare and know when to expect my confirmation discussion.Should Have
US-ONB-010HR ManagerAs an HR Manager, I want to extend an employee probation period with a documented reason so that underperforming employees receive additional time and structured support before a final decision.Should Have

2.4.4 Business Rules

Rule IDRuleDescription
BR-ONB-001Auto-Plan TriggerOnboarding plan creation is automatically triggered when an offer_letter record changes to status = accepted. The plan is created in draft status; HR must activate it. Activation must occur at least 3 business days before the joining date.
BR-ONB-002Mandatory Task CompletionOnboarding plan cannot be marked as completed unless all tasks with is_mandatory = 1 are in status = completed. Non-mandatory tasks may be left open or marked as waived by HR.
BR-ONB-003IT Setup SLAIT setup tasks (category = it_setup) must be completed before the new joiner joining date. If any IT setup task is incomplete 24 hours before joining, HR and IT Manager receive an escalation alert.
BR-ONB-004Probation Period DefaultDefault probation period is 90 days for full-time employees, 30 days for contractual employees, and 60 days for interns. Probation for senior hires (Level at or above 8) is 180 days. All defaults are overridable by HR Manager at plan creation.
BR-ONB-005Probation Extension LimitProbation can be extended a maximum of two times. Total probation period (including extensions) cannot exceed 365 days. Each extension requires documented justification approved by HR Manager.
BR-ONB-006Buddy EligibilityBuddy must be an active employee with status = active, in the same department, with tenure at or above 6 months, and not currently assigned as buddy to more than 2 active new joiners.
BR-ONB-007Pre-joining AccessNew joiners receive read-only pre-joining portal access (for document upload and schedule view only) from the date of offer acceptance until their joining date, without a full employee account being activated.

2.4.7 Workflow — State Machines

ONBOARDING PLAN STATES:
[Offer Accepted] --> Auto-create plan --> [draft]
[draft]    -- HR activates (3+ days before joining) --> [active]
[active]   -- All mandatory tasks done --> [completed]  (auto)
[active]   -- HR cancels (employee withdrew) --> [cancelled]

TASK STATES:
[pending] --> [in_progress] --> [completed]
[pending] / [in_progress] --> [overdue]  (auto, past due_date)
[overdue] --> [completed]  (late completion still allowed)
[pending] --> [waived]  (HR only, non-mandatory tasks)

PROBATION STATES:
[in_progress] -- Manager confirms --> [confirmed]  (updates employees.date_of_confirmation)
[in_progress] -- Manager extends  --> [extended] --> [in_progress] (new period)
[in_progress] -- Manager terminates --> [terminated]

Onboarding plan activatedNew Joiner + Buddy + IT Admin + Manager + HR CoordinatorEmail (role-specific welcome)Immediately on plan activation
Pre-joining link issuedNew Joiner (personal email)EmailImmediately on offer acceptance; reminder 3 days before joining
Document uploaded by new joinerHR CoordinatorIn-app notificationImmediately on upload
IT setup task overdue (24h before joining)IT Admin + IT Manager + HR CoordinatorEmail (escalation)24 hours before joining date if IT tasks incomplete
Onboarding task overdueAssigned employee + HR CoordinatorEmail + in-appDay after due_date at 9:00 AM IST
Probation review due (30 days)Reporting Manager + HR ExecutiveEmail30 days, 15 days, and 7 days before end_date
Probation confirmedEmployee + HR + ManagerEmail + in-appImmediately on decision
Probation extendedEmployee + HR Manager + ManagerEmailImmediately on extension decision
Onboarding plan completed (100%)New Joiner + HR Coordinator + ManagerEmail + in-app celebration notificationOn last mandatory task completion

2.5 Module 5: Performance Management

2.5.1 Purpose & Overview

The Performance Management module enables Jeshan Labs Private Limited to run structured, transparent, and data-driven performance review processes. It supports multiple review cycle types (Annual, Half-Yearly, Quarterly), flexible goal frameworks (OKR and KPI), self-appraisal, manager review, multi-rater 360-degree feedback, HR-driven calibration, and development planning. The module also manages Performance Improvement Plans (PIPs) for employees who require structured support. All reviews are tied to a defined review cycle ensuring consistent timelines and comparative analytics across the organisation.

2.5.2 Rating Scale Definition

RatingLabelDescriptionTypical Population %
5ExceptionalSignificantly exceeds all goals; role model for peers; drives impact beyond own scope~5%
4Exceeds ExpectationsConsistently exceeds most goals; proactive ownership; strong business impact~20%
3Meets ExpectationsAchieves all assigned goals; reliable delivery; meets role requirements fully~50%
2Needs ImprovementPartially meets goals; visible gaps in performance; requires active support~20%
1UnsatisfactoryFails to meet most goals; significant performance gaps; PIP consideration required~5%

2.5.3 User Stories

Story IDRoleUser StoryPriority
US-PER-001EmployeeAs an Employee, I want to set my individual goals at the start of each review cycle with measurable targets so that my performance assessment is objective and aligned to business outcomes.Must Have
US-PER-002ManagerAs a Manager, I want to review and approve my team members goals so that they are aligned to department OKRs before the review cycle begins.Must Have
US-PER-003EmployeeAs an Employee, I want to complete a structured self-appraisal during the review window so that I can document my achievements, challenges, and development areas before my manager review.Must Have
US-PER-004ManagerAs a Manager, I want to review my team self-appraisals and provide ratings with qualitative comments so that I can give fair, well-documented performance feedback to each team member.Must Have
US-PER-005EmployeeAs an Employee, I want to receive feedback from peers and cross-functional colleagues as part of a 360-degree review so that I get a holistic view of my performance beyond my manager perspective.Should Have
US-PER-006HR ManagerAs an HR Manager, I want to run a calibration session where final ratings are reviewed and normalised across teams so that the distribution of ratings is fair and consistent across the organisation.Must Have
US-PER-007HR ExecutiveAs an HR Executive, I want to create and configure review cycles with defined timelines for self-review, manager review, and HR review windows so that all participants know their deadlines.Must Have
US-PER-008ManagerAs a Manager, I want to initiate a Performance Improvement Plan for an employee with rating at or below 2 so that there is a formal, documented improvement process with clear milestones and timelines.Must Have
US-PER-009EmployeeAs an Employee, I want to view my historical performance ratings across all completed review cycles so that I can track my growth trajectory and identify patterns in my performance.Should Have
US-PER-010HR ManagerAs an HR Manager, I want to view a performance analytics dashboard with rating distribution, review completion rates, and top performer lists so that I can make data-driven decisions on promotions and compensation.Should Have
US-PER-011EmployeeAs an Employee, I want to collaboratively create a development plan with my manager after the review is completed so that I have a clear path for skill growth and career progression.Should Have
US-PER-012HR ExecutiveAs an HR Executive, I want to send automated reminders to employees and managers who have not completed their review forms by the deadline so that review cycles close on time.Must Have

2.5.4 Business Rules

Rule IDRuleDescription
BR-PER-001Minimum Tenure for ReviewEmployees must have a minimum tenure of 3 months in the review period to be included in a performance review cycle. Employees who joined less than 3 months before cycle end are excluded and receive a Not Applicable review record.
BR-PER-002Goal Weight SumThe sum of weight_percentage across all active goals for an employee within a single review cycle must equal exactly 100%. The system prevents goal submission if the total differs from 100%.
BR-PER-003Sequential Review StagesManager review window cannot open until the self-review window has closed. HR review window cannot open until all manager reviews are submitted (or the deadline has passed). This sequencing is enforced by the review cycle timeline configuration.
BR-PER-004360 Feedback Anonymity360-degree feedback is anonymous — feedback_provider_id is visible only to HR Manager and System Admin. Employees and their managers see aggregated ratings and anonymised comments. A minimum of 3 feedback providers must respond for 360 results to be visible to the reviewee.
BR-PER-005Final Rating AuthorityThe final_rating on a performance_review record is set by HR during calibration, overriding manager_rating if necessary. The final_rating is what is used for compensation, promotion, and PIP decisions.
BR-PER-006PIP TriggerEmployees with final_rating = 1 in any cycle are automatically flagged for PIP consideration. HR Manager must either initiate a PIP or document a waiver reason within 15 working days of the review cycle closure.
BR-PER-007PIP Concurrent with Active ReviewAn employee on an active PIP is still included in regular review cycles. Their PIP status is visible to their reviewer and HR during the review process.
BR-PER-008Review VisibilityEmployees can view their own completed performance_review record (including final_rating and hr_comments) only after HR officially closes the review cycle. During the cycle, employees can only see their self-submitted content.
BR-PER-009Rating Calibration LockingOnce HR marks a performance_review as completed, no further changes to ratings or comments are permitted by any role. Amendment requests must be submitted as formal HR tickets.

2.5.7 Validation Rules

Field / ActionRuleError Message
Goal weight_percentage sumAll active goals per employee per cycle must sum to exactly 100.00Total goal weights must equal 100%. Current total: [X]%
self_rating / manager_rating / final_ratingInteger value between 1 and 5 inclusiveRating must be a whole number between 1 and 5
Self-review submission timingCannot submit outside the cycle self_review_start to self_review_end windowSelf-review window is not currently open. Window opens on [date].
Manager review submissionEmployee self-review must be submitted before manager can submit; cannot submit outside manager_review windowEmployee has not yet submitted their self-review. Manager review cannot be submitted.
360 feedback — self-exclusionAn employee cannot provide 360 feedback on their own review recordYou cannot submit 360 feedback for your own performance review
360 feedback minimum providersAt least 3 submitted feedback records required before results are shown to revieweeSystem shows Insufficient responses message to reviewee; HR sees full data
PIP durationPIP end_date must be 14 to 180 days after start_datePIP duration must be between 14 and 180 days
Development plan action_itemsAt least 1 action item required; each action item must have description, resource, and target dateEach action item must include a description, learning resource, and target completion date
Review cycle datesself_review_start must be after period_end; manager_review_start must be after self_review_end; hr_review_start must be after manager_review_endReview window dates must follow the sequence: self-review then manager review then HR calibration

2.5.8 Workflow — State Machines

REVIEW CYCLE STATES:
[draft] --> [goal_setting]  (HR activates; employees set goals)
[goal_setting] --> [self_review]  (HR advances; self-review window opens)
[self_review]  --> [manager_review]  (HR advances; manager window opens)
[manager_review] --> [hr_calibration]  (HR advances; calibration begins)
[hr_calibration] --> [completed]  (HR closes; all final_ratings locked)
[any state] --> [cancelled]  (HR Manager only)

INDIVIDUAL REVIEW RECORD STATES:
[not_started] --> [self_review]  (employee opens and begins self-appraisal)
[self_review]  --> [manager_review]  (employee submits self-appraisal)
[manager_review] --> [hr_review]  (manager submits review)
[hr_review]  --> [completed]  (HR sets final_rating and closes)

PIP STATES:
[active] -- Mid-point: progress adequate --> [active] (continues)
[active] -- End date: targets met        --> [successful]
[active] -- End date: targets not met    --> [unsuccessful] -- HR takes action
[active] -- Employee exits during PIP    --> [terminated_during]
[active] -- HR withdraws                 --> [withdrawn]

GOAL STATES:
[draft] --> [active]  (manager approves)
[active] --> [completed]  (achievement recorded; achieved_value set)
[active] --> [cancelled]  (manager/HR cancels with reason)

Trigger EventRecipient(s)ChannelTiming

Section 3: Project Management Suite

Enterprise-grade project lifecycle management — Jira/Azure DevOps equivalent for Jeshan Labs Private Limited

3.0 Module Overview

The Project Management Suite (PMS) is a fully integrated, Jira-equivalent project lifecycle management platform built natively within the Jeshan Labs Private Limited internal portal. It provides end-to-end visibility and control over every software, infrastructure, and research project undertaken by the organisation. The suite is role-aware, real-time, and deeply integrated with the HR, Leave, and Finance modules of the portal.

Scope
  • Project Dashboard & Health Indicators
  • Product & Sprint Backlogs
  • Defect / Bug Tracking
  • Task Board (Kanban)
  • Team & Capacity Management
  • Milestones
  • Sprint Planning & Board
  • Risk Register
  • Project Reports
Key Actors
  • Admin — Full access across all projects
  • Project Manager (PM) — Full access within owned projects
  • Tech Lead — Manage tasks, sprints, backlog in assigned project
  • Developer / QA / BA / Designer — Work items in assigned projects
  • DevOps — Deployment & environment tasks
  • Viewer / Stakeholder — Read-only dashboards & reports
ID Prefixes
ProjectJL-PRJ-XXXXX
EpicJL-EPC-XXXXX
Backlog ItemJL-ITM-XXXXX
DefectJL-DEF-XXXXX
TaskJL-TSK-XXXXX
MilestoneJL-MIL-XXXXX
SprintJL-SPR-XXXXX
RiskJL-RSK-XXXXX
3.1 Sub-Module: Project Dashboard
3.1.1 Functional Description

The Project Dashboard is the central landing surface for the PMS. It presents an at-a-glance, real-time overview of all projects a user is associated with (or all projects for Admins/PMs). The dashboard aggregates data from every sub-module — sprints, tasks, defects, risks, and milestones — to surface actionable intelligence without requiring navigation into individual projects.

Dashboard Widgets
  • My Tasks — Cross-project task list filtered to the logged-in user, sortable by priority/due date
  • Project Cards — One card per project showing name, status badge, RAG indicator, progress bar, PM, team size, and sprint status
  • Sprint Burndown Chart — Ideal line vs. actual remaining story points for the active sprint per project
  • Velocity Chart — Bar chart of completed story points per sprint over the last 6 sprints
  • RAG Status Summary — Aggregate count of Red / Amber / Green projects with drill-down
  • Overdue Items — Tasks and defects past their due dates across all user projects
  • Upcoming Milestones — Next 5 milestones within 30 days
  • Open Risks — High/Critical open risks requiring attention
Supported Views
  • Grid View — 3-column responsive card grid of all projects
  • List View — Tabular listing with sortable columns (status, priority, deadline, PM)
  • My Dashboard — Personalised view showing only projects/tasks relevant to the logged-in user
  • Executive View — High-level summary for stakeholders: RAG, budget vs. actual, milestone health, open risks count
Filters & Controls
  • Filter by: project type, status, priority, PM, date range
  • Search by project name / project code
  • Toggle: Active only / All projects
  • Sort: by name, deadline, priority, RAG status
3.1.2 User Stories
Story ID Role User Story Acceptance Criteria Priority
US-PD-001 PM As a Project Manager, I want to see all my projects on a single dashboard with real-time status indicators so that I can quickly identify which projects need attention. Dashboard loads within 3 seconds; each project card shows RAG status, current sprint, % complete, and next milestone; filtering by status works correctly. Critical
US-PD-002 Developer As a Developer, I want to see "My Tasks" widget listing all tasks assigned to me across projects so that I can manage my workload without switching between projects. Widget shows task ID, title, project, priority, due date, and status; tasks are sortable; overdue tasks are highlighted in red; clicking a task opens the task detail. Critical
US-PD-003 PM As a PM, I want to view a Sprint Burndown Chart for each active project so that I can monitor whether the team is on track to complete the sprint. Chart displays ideal burndown line vs. actual remaining story points; X-axis shows sprint days, Y-axis shows story points; data refreshes every 15 minutes; chart is interactive (hover tooltips). Critical
US-PD-004 Admin As an Admin, I want to view a portfolio-level RAG status summary so that I can report project health to leadership without manually gathering data. Summary card shows total projects in Red, Amber, Green; clicking a RAG status filters the project list; export to PDF is available. High
US-PD-005 PM As a PM, I want to see a Velocity Chart showing story points completed per sprint over the last 6 sprints so that I can predict future delivery capacity. Bar chart renders correctly for up to 6 sprints; average velocity line is overlaid; if fewer than 6 sprints exist, chart shows available data; chart is exportable as PNG. High
US-PD-006 All As any user, I want to switch between Grid View and List View on the project dashboard so that I can view project information in my preferred format. View preference persists in user session; Grid view shows cards with visual indicators; List view shows sortable table; both views show the same data; default view is Grid. Medium
US-PD-007 Stakeholder As a Stakeholder, I want read-only access to the Executive Dashboard view so that I can monitor project health without modifying any data. Executive view hides all edit/create controls; shows RAG, budget utilisation, milestone health, team utilisation; access is restricted by the Stakeholder role. Medium
US-PD-008 All As any user, I want to receive a visual alert on the dashboard for overdue tasks and critical open risks so that urgent items are never missed. Overdue tasks show a red badge with count; critical risks show a warning icon; clicking opens the filtered list; notification count is updated in real time. High

3.1.5 Validation Rules
Project Creation Validations
  • project_name: Required, 3–200 characters, must be unique within the organisation
  • project_code: Required, 3–10 alphanumeric characters, uppercase, unique globally (e.g. JLPAY, JLHR)
  • start_date: Required; must not be in the past for new projects (configurable)
  • end_date: Required; must be after start_date by at least 1 day
  • estimated_hours: If provided, must be > 0 and ≤ 999999.99
  • budget: If provided, must be > 0; two decimal places max
  • project_manager_id: Must reference an active employee with PM or Admin role
  • completion_percentage: Integer 0–100 inclusive
Member & Settings Validations
  • An employee may not be added to the same project twice with an active status (duplicate prevention)
  • joined_date: Must be ≥ project start_date
  • left_date: If provided, must be ≥ joined_date
  • hourly_rate: If provided, must be > 0
  • sprint_duration_weeks: Integer 1–4 inclusive
  • Only one active PM role allowed per project at any time
  • RAG status change requires a mandatory justification note (min 10 characters)
  • notification_preferences JSON must conform to the defined schema
3.1.6 Business Rules
  1. BR-PD-001: A project's rag_status is automatically computed nightly: Red if end_date is overdue OR completion_percentage < expected_percentage by >10%; Amber if behind schedule by 5–10%; Green otherwise. Manual override by PM/Admin is permitted with a reason note, and the auto-compute is suspended for 48 hours.
  2. BR-PD-002: completion_percentage is auto-calculated as: (count of tasks in status 'done' ÷ total tasks) × 100, rounded to nearest integer. Manual override is allowed only by PM/Admin.
  3. BR-PD-003: actual_hours is a running total aggregated from task_time_logs; it is not directly editable.
  4. BR-PD-004: A project may not be deleted (only cancelled). Cancellation sets status to 'cancelled' and deactivates all open sprints and unassigned tasks. Completed projects are immutable except for report generation.
  5. BR-PD-005: The project_id (JL-PRJ-XXXXX) is system-generated on creation, is immutable thereafter, and is the canonical reference across all integrated modules.
  6. BR-PD-006: Burndown chart data is computed in real time from the active sprint's remaining story points. If no sprint is active, the burndown widget displays a "No Active Sprint" placeholder.
  7. BR-PD-007: Velocity is computed as the arithmetic mean of completed story points across completed sprints. A minimum of 2 completed sprints is required to display velocity.
  8. BR-PD-008: A user without project membership may not access any project data, regardless of their system role, unless they hold the Admin role.

3.2 Sub-Module: Backlogs
3.2.1 Functional Description

The Backlog module provides a structured, hierarchical workspace for defining and managing all work items for a project. It supports the full agile product backlog lifecycle from Epic definition down to atomic Sub-tasks. The module supports drag-and-drop re-ordering, Story Point estimation using the Fibonacci sequence, labels, rich-text descriptions with acceptance criteria, and seamless drag-to-sprint planning.

Item Hierarchy
Epic Large body of work spanning multiple sprints; tied to a business objective. ID format: JL-EPC-XXXXX
Story User-facing feature deliverable within one sprint. Contains acceptance criteria.
Task Technical or operational work item within a story.
Sub-task Granular breakdown of a task; atomic unit of work.
Bug Defect found during sprint; linked to a story or standalone.
Improvement Enhancement to existing functionality; not a new feature.
Fibonacci Story Point Scale
1 2 3 5 8 13 21 ?

Items estimated at 13 or 21 points should trigger a PM/Tech Lead review for decomposition into smaller stories. The ? value indicates unestimated items.

Backlog Views
  • Product Backlog — All items not yet in a sprint, ordered by priority
  • Sprint Backlog — Items committed to the current sprint
  • Epic Board — Kanban view grouped by Epic
  • Flat List — All items in a searchable/filterable table
3.2.2 User Stories
Story IDRoleUser StoryAcceptance CriteriaPriority
US-BL-001 PM / BA As a PM/BA, I want to create Epics with descriptions, colours, and target dates so that large initiatives are tracked at a high level. Epic is created with a system-generated JL-EPC-XXXXX ID; colour picker shows 12 preset options; target date must be after start date; epic appears in the Epic Board immediately. Critical
US-BL-002 BA / Developer As a BA, I want to create User Stories with rich-text acceptance criteria and link them to an Epic so that developers have clear, unambiguous requirements. Rich-text editor supports bullet lists, bold, italic, and code blocks; acceptance criteria field is separate from description; story is linked to one Epic; story ID is auto-generated. Critical
US-BL-003 Tech Lead As a Tech Lead, I want to estimate backlog items using Fibonacci story points so that sprint capacity planning is accurate. Estimation widget shows buttons for 1,2,3,5,8,13,21,?; selected value is saved immediately; unestimated items are flagged with a "?" badge; total story points are shown per Epic. Critical
US-BL-004 PM / Tech Lead As a PM, I want to reorder backlog items via drag-and-drop so that priority is communicated visually through position. Drag handle is visible on hover; items snap to new position on drop; order_index is updated in the database; reorder is reflected for all users within 2 seconds; undo of last reorder is possible. High
US-BL-005 PM / Tech Lead As a PM, I want to move backlog items into a sprint via drag-and-drop during sprint planning so that the sprint scope is defined quickly. Sprint planning panel shows the backlog alongside the sprint board; dragging an item from backlog to sprint updates its sprint_id; total sprint story points are updated in real time; items cannot be added to a completed sprint. US-BL-008 PM As a PM, I want to filter the backlog by Epic, assignee, label, status, and story points so that I can quickly find and manage specific items. Filters are combinable; filter state persists in URL for shareable links; "Clear Filters" button resets all; filtered results show item count; filter controls are accessible. Medium

3.2.5 Validation Rules
  • title: Required, 3–500 characters; HTML tags are stripped before storage
  • story_points: Must be one of {1,2,3,5,8,13,21} or NULL. Any other integer is rejected with error "Invalid Fibonacci value"
  • item_type = 'sub_task': parent_item_id is mandatory; nesting depth is limited to 2 levels (story → task → sub_task)
  • epic_id: Must belong to the same project as the backlog item
  • sprint_id: Item cannot be assigned to a sprint in status 'completed' or 'cancelled'
  • Epics: target_date must be ≥ start_date; color must be a valid 6-digit HEX value prefixed with #
  • Labels: max 50 characters per label, max 10 labels per item, max 100 labels per project
  • Attachments: max 10 files per item, max 20 MB per file; allowed types: pdf, png, jpg, jpeg, gif, zip, docx, xlsx
3.2.6 Business Rules
  1. BR-BL-001: Story points ≥ 13 on a single item trigger an automatic advisory notification to the PM and Tech Lead suggesting decomposition.
  2. BR-BL-002: Items in status 'done' within an active sprint cannot be moved back to the product backlog without PM approval.
  3. BR-BL-003: Deleting an Epic cascades to a soft-delete of all child backlog items only if none of them are in an active sprint. If any child is sprint-active, deletion is blocked with an error.
  4. BR-BL-004: The order_index is maintained at the project level; the product backlog is the single ordered sequence. Items assigned to sprints retain their backlog order within the sprint backlog.
  5. BR-BL-005: An item of type 'bug' appearing in the backlog must be linked to a defect record (defects table) or promoted to a defect. The system provides a "Create Defect from Item" action.
  6. BR-BL-006: Sprint capacity warning: if total story points of items planned for a sprint exceed 120% of the last 3-sprint average velocity, a warning is shown during sprint planning (not a hard block).

Trigger EventRecipientsChannelTiming
New backlog item assigned to userAssigneeIn-app + EmailImmediate
Backlog item moved to sprintAssignee, PMIn-appImmediate
Backlog item status changed to 'done'Reporter, PMIn-appImmediate
Item estimated ≥ 13 story pointsPM, Tech LeadIn-appImmediate
Item unestimated for > 72 hoursPM, Tech LeadIn-app + EmailScheduled (daily digest)
Epic target_date within 7 days with <80% stories donePM, Epic ownerIn-app + EmailDaily
3.3 Sub-Module: Defects / Bug Tracking
3.3.1 Functional Description

The Defect Tracking module provides a structured, full-lifecycle bug management system. It captures defects from discovery through to verified closure, including re-open cycles. Each defect record is rich with contextual data — reproduction steps, environment details, browser/OS info, severity, and attachments — to enable developers to resolve issues efficiently without back-and-forth communication.

Defect Lifecycle
New → Assigned → In Progress → Testing → Resolved → Closed
From Testing/Resolved: Reopened → Assigned again
Resolution Types
  • Fixed — Bug was reproduced and fixed
  • Not a Bug — Behaviour is by design; closed with explanation
  • Duplicate — Linked to an existing defect
  • Won't Fix — Accepted risk; deferred indefinitely
  • Deferred — Will be addressed in a future release
Severity vs. Priority Matrix
SeverityDefinitionSLA (Fix target)
CriticalSystem crash / data loss / security breach4 hours
HighMajor functionality broken; no workaround1 business day
MediumFunctionality degraded; workaround exists3 business days
LowMinor UI/UX issue; cosmetic defectNext sprint
Bug Metrics Widget
  • Total open defects (by severity)
  • Defect ageing report (open > N days)
  • Defects by environment
  • Reopen rate (reopened/total resolved)
  • Mean time to resolve (MTTR)
3.3.2 User Stories
Story IDRoleUser StoryAcceptance CriteriaPriority
US-DT-001 QA As a QA Engineer, I want to create a defect with full contextual details including steps to reproduce, expected result, actual result, environment, browser, and OS so that developers can reproduce and fix it without requiring additional communication. All required fields validated on submit; defect ID JL-DEF-XXXXX auto-generated; attachments (up to 10 files) supported; confirmation message shown; defect visible in project defect list immediately. Critical
US-DT-002 PM / Tech Lead As a PM, I want to assign a defect to a developer with a target resolution date so that accountability and SLAs are enforced. Assignee dropdown shows only active project members; assignment triggers email notification to assignee; status changes from 'new' to 'assigned'; due date is set and displayed with SLA countdown. Critical
US-DT-003 Developer As a Developer, I want to update the defect status to "In Progress" and add comments documenting my investigation so that stakeholders are informed of progress. Status transition is validated (cannot jump from 'new' to 'resolved' without intermediate states); comment is saved with timestamp and author; activity log shows all status changes. Critical
US-DT-004 QA As a QA Engineer, I want to reopen a resolved defect if the fix is insufficient, with a mandatory explanation, so that the developer understands why the fix was rejected. Reopen action available only in 'resolved' status; comment is mandatory on reopen (min 20 chars); reopen count is incremented on the defect; reopened defect status goes back to 'assigned'; reporter and PM are notified. Critical
US-DT-005 PM As a PM, I want to view a defect metrics dashboard showing open defects by severity, ageing, and environment so that I can assess quality risk and prioritise fix resources. Metrics update in real time; severity breakdown shown as colour-coded bar chart; ageing report shows defects open > 7, 14, 30 days; filters by sprint, environment, and assignee are available; export to Excel supported. High
US-DT-006 QA / Developer As a team member, I want to link a defect to a backlog item (story or task) so that the context of the defect within the feature scope is clear. Link dialog shows search by item ID/title; multiple backlog items can be linked; link is bidirectional (defect appears in the linked backlog item's detail); unlinking is also possible. High
US-DT-007 PM / QA As a PM, I want to track which version a bug was found in and which version it was fixed in so that release notes and regression test planning are accurate. version_found and version_fixed fields available on defect form; version list is maintained per project; reports can be filtered by version. Resolution type field is mandatory when closing; 'Won't Fix' and 'Deferred' resolutions require a justification note of at least 30 characters; PM/Admin can set these; Developer cannot set 'Won't Fix' unilaterally. Medium

3.3.5 Validation Rules
  • title: Required, 10–500 characters
  • steps_to_reproduce: Required, minimum 30 characters; should contain at least 2 numbered steps
  • expected_result / actual_result: Both required, minimum 10 characters each
  • severity and environment: Both required at creation time
  • Status transitions are enforced by a server-side state machine; invalid transitions return HTTP 422 with detailed error
  • Resolution type is mandatory when transitioning to 'resolved' or 'closed'
  • 'Won't Fix' and 'Deferred' resolutions require comment of min 30 characters
  • Reopen comment: minimum 20 characters
  • Attachments: max 10 per defect, max 20 MB each; allowed types: jpg, jpeg, png, gif, pdf, mp4 (screen recordings), zip
  • due_date: Auto-calculated from severity SLA on assignment; can be manually overridden by PM only
3.3.6 Business Rules
  1. BR-DT-001: A 'Critical' severity defect found in the 'prod' environment triggers an immediate P0 escalation notification to the PM, Tech Lead, and Admin with an SMS alert.
  2. BR-DT-002: The defect state machine is strictly enforced: New → Assigned → In Progress → Testing → Resolved → Closed. The only allowed backward transition is Resolved/Testing → Reopened. All other backward transitions are blocked.
  3. BR-DT-003: A defect cannot be closed by the same developer who resolved it (four-eyes principle for prod and staging environments); QA or PM must close.
  4. BR-DT-004: If a defect's due_date passes without resolution, its priority is automatically escalated one level (e.g., Medium → High) and the PM is notified.
  5. BR-DT-005: Defects marked "Duplicate" must reference the original defect ID; the system validates the referenced defect exists in the same project.
  6. BR-DT-006: A sprint cannot be completed if it contains defects in status 'new', 'assigned', or 'in_progress' with severity 'critical' or 'high'. PM must resolve or defer these defects first.
3.3.7 Notification Triggers
Trigger EventRecipientsChannelTiming
New defect createdPM, Tech LeadIn-app + EmailImmediate

Defect assigned to developerAssigneeIn-app + EmailImmediate
Defect status changedReporter, AssigneeIn-appImmediate

Defect SLA due date breachedAssignee, PMIn-app + EmailImmediate (at breach time)

3.4 Sub-Module: Tasks Management
3.4.1 Functional Description

The Tasks Management module provides both Kanban board and list views for managing all tasks across projects. It supports multi-level task hierarchies, granular time logging, task dependencies (blocking relationships), inline checklists, threaded comments, and a complete audit trail of every change made to a task. Integration with the sprint module ensures tasks respect sprint boundaries while the time log integrates with the HR timesheet module.

Kanban Board Columns
To Do In Progress In Review Testing Done
  • Columns are configurable per project (add/rename/reorder)
  • WIP (Work in Progress) limits can be set per column
  • Cards show assignee avatar, priority colour, story points, due date, and checklist progress
  • Swimlane grouping: by assignee, by epic, by priority, or flat
  • Card quick-edit inline: status, assignee, priority
Time Tracking
  • Log hours against a task with date, hours, and description
  • Multiple logs per task (daily entries by multiple team members)
  • Time remaining = estimated_hours − Σ logged_hours
  • Timer widget: start/pause/stop stopwatch within the task detail page
  • Integrates with monthly timesheet report in HR module
  • PM can view aggregated logged hours across all tasks per member
Dependency Types
  • Blocks — This task must be completed before the target task can begin
  • Is Blocked By — This task cannot begin until the target is done
  • Relates To — Informational link; no scheduling constraint
3.4.2 User Stories
Story IDRoleUser StoryAcceptance CriteriaPriority
US-TM-001 PM / Tech Lead As a Tech Lead, I want to create tasks with type, priority, description, estimated hours, assignee, and due date so that work is clearly defined and tracked. Task ID JL-TSK-XXXXX auto-generated; all required fields validated; task appears on Kanban board immediately under "To Do"; assignee is notified. Critical
US-TM-002 All As a team member, I want to move tasks across Kanban columns using drag-and-drop so that status is updated visually in real time. Drag-and-drop updates status in DB; transition is validated; WIP limit warning shown if column limit exceeded (non-blocking by default); status change appears in task activity log. Critical
US-TM-003 Developer As a Developer, I want to log hours worked on a task with a description of work done so that time is accurately tracked for project costing and timesheet purposes. Time log entry requires date, hours (max 24), and description; logged hours update the task's total; remaining hours are recalculated; entries are deletable by the author within 24 hours. Critical
US-TM-004 Tech Lead As a Tech Lead, I want to set task dependencies (blocks / is blocked by) so that the development team knows the correct execution sequence. Dependency creates a visual link on the task detail page; blocked tasks show a warning badge on the Kanban card; circular dependency detection prevents invalid links; dependency graph is viewable. High
US-TM-005 Developer As a Developer, I want to add a checklist to a task to track sub-steps without creating formal sub-tasks so that minor actionable items are managed inline. Checklist items are added/reordered inline; each item has a checkbox; completion percentage of checklist is shown on the Kanban card; completed checklist items cannot be re-opened without unchecking. High
US-TM-006 All As a team member, I want to add threaded comments with @mentions and attachments to a task so that all discussion related to that task is captured in one place. 3.4.5 Validation Rules
  • title: Required, 3–500 characters
  • estimated_hours: If provided, must be > 0 and ≤ 999.99
  • hours_logged (per entry): Must be > 0 and ≤ 24; total logged across all entries for a task may exceed estimated (warning shown but not blocked)
  • due_date: If provided, must be ≥ start_date (if set) and must fall within the sprint's date range if assigned to a sprint
  • Circular dependencies: Server-side graph traversal validates no cycle is created before saving a dependency
  • A task cannot depend on itself
  • Checklist: max 50 items per task; item_text 1–500 characters
  • Comment: min 1 character, max 10,000 characters
  • Watchers JSON: max 50 watchers per task
  • Max depth for parent_task_id hierarchy: 2 levels (task → sub_task; no sub-sub-tasks)
3.4.6 Business Rules
  1. BR-TM-001: A task in status 'done' cannot have additional time logged unless the PM explicitly reopens it. The completed_at timestamp is set only once and is not updatable.
  2. BR-TM-002: A task that is blocked by an incomplete dependency is displayed with a warning badge on the Kanban board; it can still be moved (soft warning only).
  3. BR-TM-003: Deleting a task with logged time is prohibited. The task must be cancelled (status = 'cancelled') instead; logged hours are retained for reporting.
  4. BR-TM-004: Time log entries sync daily to the HR Timesheet module; employees can see their project hours in their monthly timesheet view.
  5. BR-TM-005: WIP limits are enforced as warnings by default. If a PM sets them as hard limits in project settings, card drops that exceed the limit are rejected with an error message.
  6. BR-TM-006: Every write to the tasks table that changes a tracked field automatically inserts a record into task_history via a DB trigger or application-layer hook.
3.4.7 Notification Triggers
Trigger EventRecipientsChannelTiming
Task assigned to userAssigneeIn-app + EmailImmediate
Task due date approaching (24h)Assignee, WatchersIn-app + EmailScheduled
Task overdue (past due_date, status ≠ done)Assignee, PM, WatchersIn-app + EmailDaily
Task status changed to 'done'Reporter, PM, WatchersIn-appImmediate
Comment with @mentionMentioned usersIn-app + EmailImmediate
Task blocking dependency resolvedAssignee of blocked taskIn-appImmediate
Logged hours exceed estimated hoursAssignee, PMIn-appImmediate
3.5 Sub-Module: Team Management
3.5.1 Functional Description

The Team Management module provides project PMs with full visibility and control over their project's human resources. It covers team roster management, role assignments, workload capacity planning, and availability tracking integrated with the HR Leave module. The workload view highlights over-allocation and under-utilisation to optimise sprint capacity planning.

Team Roster Features
  • View all current and past project members with their roles and join/leave dates
  • Add/remove members from projects (PM/Admin only)
  • Update member roles within a project
  • View member profiles: department, designation, skills, availability
  • Member search by name, role, or department
  • Member availability calendar (leave integration)
Workload & Capacity View
  • Capacity bar per member: allocated hours vs. available hours in current sprint
  • Over-allocation alert: red indicator if member allocated > 100% capacity
  • Cross-project workload: hours allocated across ALL active projects for a member
  • Available capacity: (working days in sprint − leave days) × daily_hours_capacity − assigned task hours
  • Sprint capacity summary: total team available hours vs. total estimated task hours
  • Export capacity report to Excel
3.5.2 User Stories
Story IDRoleUser StoryAcceptance CriteriaPriority
US-TEM-001 PM As a PM, I want to add team members to my project with specific roles so that their access and responsibilities are clearly defined. Member search shows all active employees; role dropdown shows available roles; duplicate membership check prevents re-adding active members; added member receives a notification; membership is immediately visible in the roster. Critical
US-TEM-002 PM As a PM, I want to view the workload of each team member in the current sprint so that I can prevent over-allocation and ensure balanced task distribution. Workload view shows hours allocated vs. hours available per member; over-allocated members (>100%) are flagged red; under-utilised members (<50%) shown in amber; numbers update when tasks are assigned/reassigned. Critical
US-TEM-003 PM As a PM, I want to see each member's approved leave during the sprint so that sprint capacity is adjusted accordingly. Leave data is pulled from the HR Leave module via API; leave days are excluded from available capacity calculation; leave-adjusted capacity is shown per member; planned leaves in upcoming sprints are visible. High
US-TEM-004 PM As a PM, I want to update a team member's role in the project so that their access permissions are aligned with their current responsibilities. Role change is audited; only one PM role per project; changing from PM role requires designating a replacement PM or PM role remains vacant (warning shown); change is effective immediately. High
US-TEM-005 PM As a PM, I want to remove a team member from the project when they roll off so that they no longer have access to project data. Removal sets left_date to today and is_active to false; open tasks assigned to removed member are flagged "Unassigned" and PM is notified; historical data (time logs, comments) is preserved; removed member loses project access immediately. High
US-TEM-006 Admin As an Admin, I want to view a cross-project workload report for any employee so that I can identify resource conflicts across the portfolio. Report shows employee's active project memberships, allocated hours per project, total allocation %, and available capacity; data is current-sprint scoped; exportable to Excel. Medium
US-TEM-007 PM As a PM, I want to set a daily capacity (hours per day) for each team member in my project to reflect part-time allocations so that capacity planning is accurate. Daily capacity field (0.5–8.0 hours, step 0.5) is editable per member in project settings; defaults to 8 hours; capacity is used in workload calculations; change is logged in audit trail. Medium
US-TEM-008 All As a team member, I want to see the project team directory with contact details and roles so that I can quickly identify who to reach out to for specific areas. Team directory is accessible to all project members; shows name, role, email, department, profile picture; displays active members by default; toggle to show all (including past) members. Medium
3.5.3 API Endpoints
MethodEndpointDescriptionRequired Roles

POST/api/v1/projects/{project_id}/teamAdd team member to projectAdmin, PM

3.5.4 Business Rules & Validation
  1. BR-TEM-001: Only active employees (HR module status = 'active') can be added to projects. Resigned/terminated employees are excluded from member search results.
  2. BR-TEM-002: Each project must have exactly one active PM at all times. The system prevents removal of the sole PM without a replacement being designated.
  3. BR-TEM-003: Available capacity = (sprint_working_days − approved_leave_days) × member_daily_capacity. Approved leave is fetched from the HR Leave module in real time.
  4. BR-TEM-004: Over-allocation warning is triggered when a member's total allocated task hours (across all active projects) exceeds 110% of their total available capacity.
  5. BR-TEM-005: Removing a team member does not delete their time logs, comments, or task assignments. Open tasks are de-assigned (assigned_to = NULL) and PM is notified to reassign.
  6. BR-TEM-006: The daily_capacity field per member defaults to 8 hours but can be reduced (e.g., 4 hours for part-time) by the PM. The minimum is 0.5 hours.

Member role changedAffected member, PMIn-appImmediate
Member over-allocated (>110%)PM, affected memberIn-app + EmailImmediate (on task assignment)
Sprint starts with unassigned tasksPMIn-appAt sprint start time
3.6 Sub-Module: Milestones
3.6.1 Functional Description

The Milestones module provides structured tracking of significant project checkpoints — go/no-go gates, release dates, client demo dates, regulatory submission deadlines, and phase completions. Each milestone aggregates linked backlog items and tasks to compute an automatic completion percentage, and its RAG health informs the project dashboard.

Milestone Features
  • Create milestones with target dates and owners
  • Link backlog items, tasks, and defects to milestones
  • Auto-compute completion_percentage from linked items
  • Visual timeline/Gantt-style milestone view
  • Dependencies between milestones (milestone A must complete before milestone B)
  • Status progression: Pending → In Progress → Completed / Missed
  • Actual completion date vs. target date variance tracking
Milestone Status Rules
StatusTrigger Condition
PendingTarget date in future; no linked items completed yet
In ProgressAt least one linked item is in progress or done
CompletedAll linked items done AND PM marks complete
MissedTarget date passed AND status ≠ completed (auto-set by system)
3.6.2 User Stories
Story IDRoleUser StoryAcceptance CriteriaPriority
US-ML-001 PM As a PM, I want to create project milestones with a title, description, target date, and owner so that key delivery checkpoints are formally tracked. Milestone ID JL-MIL-XXXXX auto-generated; target date validated (must be within project start/end range); owner must be an active project member; milestone appears in the timeline view immediately. Critical
US-ML-002 PM As a PM, I want to link backlog items and tasks to a milestone so that its completion is computed automatically from the linked work items. Link dialog supports multi-select of items; completion_percentage = (done items / total linked items) × 100; linking is bidirectional; items can be unlinked; circular milestone dependencies are prevented. Critical
US-ML-003 PM As a PM, I want to view all project milestones in a timeline view showing their target dates, current progress, and status so that I can plan and communicate the delivery roadmap. Timeline renders milestones as markers on a date axis; past milestones shown as completed or missed; progress bar overlaid per milestone; zoom to sprint/month/quarter available. High
US-ML-004 PM As a PM, I want to set dependencies between milestones so that prerequisite milestones must be completed before successor milestones are started. Dependency is saved and shown as an arrow in the timeline; circular dependency detection prevents invalid links; if a predecessor milestone is 'Missed', a warning is shown on the dependent milestone. High
US-ML-005 PM As a PM, I want to manually mark a milestone as 'Completed' with an actual completion date so that the record reflects reality even when linked items may not all be formally closed. Manual completion requires a confirmation dialog; actual_date is recorded; variance (target vs. actual) is computed and displayed; completion event triggers notifications; status becomes immutable after completion (no regression). High
US-ML-006 Stakeholder As a Stakeholder, I want to view a read-only milestone status report showing all milestones with RAG status and completion percentages so that I can assess project delivery health. Report is accessible to Stakeholder role; shows milestone ID, title, target date, actual date (if completed), status badge, completion %, owner; exportable as PDF. Medium
US-ML-007 PM As a PM, I want to receive a proactive alert when a milestone is at risk of being missed (target date within 7 days and completion < 80%) so that I can take corrective action in time. System sends in-app and email alert to milestone owner and PM; alert fires once per milestone per 24-hour window; snooze option available for 48 hours; alert includes current completion % and list of incomplete linked items. High

3.6.5 Business Rules & Validation
  1. BR-ML-001: A milestone's target_date must fall within the project's start_date and end_date. Milestones outside the project date range are rejected.
  2. BR-ML-002: The system auto-transitions a milestone to 'missed' status via a nightly job if target_date has passed and status is not 'completed'.
  3. BR-ML-003: A completed milestone cannot be re-opened. If linked items are later found incomplete, a new milestone must be created to track the remaining work.
  4. BR-ML-004: Circular dependencies between milestones are prevented by a server-side depth-first search before saving any new milestone dependency.
  5. BR-ML-005: Milestone at-risk alert fires when: target_date − today ≤ 7 days AND completion_percentage < 80%.
  6. BR-ML-006: The completion_percentage auto-calculation is: (count of linked items with status 'done' or 'closed') ÷ (total linked items) × 100. Items with no linked items default to 0% unless PM manually sets a value.
3.6.6 Notification Triggers

Milestone target_date within 7 days and completion < 80%Milestone owner, PMIn-app + EmailDaily
Milestone auto-set to 'Missed'Milestone owner, PM, AdminIn-app + EmailNightly job
Milestone marked 'Completed'All project members, PMIn-appImmediate
Dependent milestone predecessor missedOwner of dependent milestone, PMIn-app + EmailImmediate
3.7 Sub-Module: Sprints & Sprint Board
3.7.1 Functional Description

The Sprints module provides full Scrum sprint lifecycle management — from sprint creation and planning through daily execution on the Sprint Board to sprint completion, velocity recording, and retrospective documentation. Only one sprint per project may be in 'active' status at any time. The sprint board is a real-time Kanban view scoped exclusively to items in the active sprint.

Sprint Lifecycle
Planning → Active → Completed
Or: Planning / Active → Cancelled
  • Planning: Sprint created; items can be added/removed; dates set; sprint not yet started
  • Active: Sprint started; sprint board live; team works items; burndown tracked
  • Completed: Sprint ended; velocity recorded; incomplete items returned to backlog; retrospective created
  • Cancelled: Sprint abandoned; all items returned to backlog
Sprint Board Columns
To Do In Progress In Review Testing Done
Sprint Metrics Tracked
  • Total story points committed vs. completed
  • Daily burndown: remaining story points per day
  • Team velocity (completed SPs per sprint)
  • Sprint scope creep: items added mid-sprint
  • Defects raised vs. resolved in sprint
3.7.2 User Stories
Story IDRoleUser StoryAcceptance CriteriaPriority
US-SP-001 PM / Tech Lead As a PM, I want to create a sprint with a name, goal, start date, and end date so that the team has a clearly defined time-boxed iteration. Sprint ID JL-SPR-XXXXX auto-generated; start and end dates validated (end > start; no overlap with other non-cancelled sprints); sprint goal is optional but recommended; sprint appears in planning state; default duration from project settings. Critical
US-SP-002 PM / Tech Lead As a PM, I want to drag backlog items into the sprint during sprint planning so that the sprint scope is defined visually and story point totals are shown in real time. Split-panel view shows product backlog on left and sprint items on right; drag-and-drop assigns sprint_id; running total of story points updates instantly; capacity warning shown if total exceeds 120% of average velocity. Critical
US-SP-003 PM As a PM, I want to start a sprint so that the sprint board becomes live and the burndown chart begins tracking. Only one sprint can be active per project; start action validates that at least one item is in the sprint; start date is set to today if in planning; all sprint items are set to status 'to_do' if not already in progress; team notified. Critical
US-SP-004 All As a team member, I want to use the Sprint Board to move items between columns so that sprint progress is visible to the entire team in real time. Board refreshes in real time (WebSocket or polling <5s); items show assignee, priority, story points, defect count; drag validates WIP limits; completed items are highlighted; board is mobile-responsive. Critical
US-SP-005 PM As a PM, I want to complete a sprint, returning unfinished items to the product backlog, so that the team can begin a new sprint cleanly. Complete action shows a summary: completed items, incomplete items, story points completed; PM selects destination for incomplete items (backlog or next sprint); velocity is recorded on the sprint record; sprint status set to 'completed'. Critical
US-SP-006 PM / Tech Lead As a PM, I want to view the sprint burndown chart showing ideal vs. actual remaining story points per day so that I can identify scope creep or delivery risk early. Chart shows ideal (linear) burn line and actual daily remaining SPs; X-axis = sprint days, Y-axis = story points; scope creep shown as upward spikes; chart is interactive with hover tooltips; data is day-granular. High
US-SP-007 PM / Tech Lead As a PM, I want to document a sprint retrospective (What Went Well, What to Improve, Action Items) after sprint completion so that learnings are captured and acted upon. Retrospective form available only after sprint completion; WYSIWYG lists for each section; action items include owner and due date; facilitator and participants recorded; retrospective is read-only after 7 days of creation. High
US-SP-008 PM As a PM, I want to view sprint velocity over the last 6 sprints to use as a basis for future sprint planning. Velocity chart shows bars per sprint with story points completed; average velocity line overlay; trend (improving/declining) indicated; minimum 2 sprints required for display; data exported as CSV. Medium

PUT/api/v1/sprints/{sprint_id}/retrospectiveUpdate retrospective (within 7 days of creation)Facilitator, PM
3.7.5 Business Rules & Validation
  1. BR-SP-001: Only one sprint per project may have status 'active' at any time. Attempting to start a second sprint while one is active returns HTTP 422.
  2. BR-SP-002: Sprint date ranges within the same project must not overlap. Validation is enforced server-side on create/update.
  3. BR-SP-003: The burndown snapshot job runs nightly at 23:59; it records remaining_story_points = sum of story points of sprint items not in status 'done'. The ideal_remaining is calculated as: total_story_points × (remaining_sprint_days / total_sprint_days).
  4. BR-SP-004: When completing a sprint, items with status 'done' are finalized; items with any other status are returned to the product backlog (sprint_id set to NULL) unless the PM explicitly assigns them to a specific future sprint.
  5. BR-SP-005: Sprint cannot be completed if it contains critical/high severity open defects. PM must resolve or defer these first (as per BR-DT-006).
  6. BR-SP-006: Items added to an active sprint after start are tracked as "scope creep" and flagged in the burndown chart as upward spikes. The count of scope-creep items is recorded on the sprint record.
  7. BR-SP-007: A retrospective can only be created or edited within 14 days of sprint completion. After 14 days it is read-only.
3.7.6 Notification Triggers
Trigger EventRecipientsChannelTiming
Sprint startedAll project membersIn-app + EmailImmediate
Sprint ends in 2 daysPM, All project membersIn-app + EmailScheduled (48h before end)
Sprint completedAll project members, StakeholdersIn-app + EmailImmediate
Sprint cancelledAll project members, PMIn-app + EmailImmediate
Scope creep item added mid-sprintPMIn-appImmediate
Sprint retrospective createdAll sprint participantsIn-appImmediate
Retrospective action item dueAction item ownerIn-app + EmailDay of due date
3.8 Sub-Module: Risk Register
3.8.1 Functional Description

The Risk Register provides a structured, auditable repository of all identified project risks. It follows standard risk management practice with a probability × impact scoring matrix to compute a risk score and auto-assign severity bands. Each risk has a mitigation plan, contingency plan, owner, and a full update history. The register integrates with the project dashboard's risk health widget and generates alerts when risk scores escalate.

Risk Categories
  • Technical — Architecture failures, technology limitations, integration issues
  • Resource — Team attrition, skill gaps, availability
  • Schedule — Scope creep, dependency delays, estimation errors
  • Budget — Cost overruns, currency fluctuation, unplanned expenses
  • External — Regulatory changes, vendor risks, client-side delays
  • Security — Data breaches, compliance violations
Probability × Impact Matrix

risk_score = probability × impact (both on 1–5 scale; score range: 1–25)

Score RangeSeverityColour
1–4LowLow
5–9MediumMedium
10–19HighHigh
20–25CriticalCritical
3.8.2 User Stories
Story IDRoleUser StoryAcceptance CriteriaPriority
US-RI-001 PM / BA As a PM, I want to log a project risk with title, description, category, probability, and impact so that all project risks are formally documented in one place. Risk ID JL-RSK-XXXXX auto-generated; probability and impact are dropdowns (1–5); risk_score = probability × impact; severity auto-set based on score; risk appears in the risk register immediately. Critical
US-RI-002 PM As a PM, I want to document a mitigation plan and contingency plan for each risk so that the team knows how to respond if the risk materialises. Mitigation plan and contingency plan are rich-text fields; both are required for risks with severity 'high' or 'critical'; plans are versioned in risk_updates table; last-updated timestamp is displayed. Critical
US-RI-003 PM As a PM, I want to assign a risk owner and target resolution date so that accountability for risk mitigation is clear. Owner must be an active project member; target_date must be in the future; owner receives in-app and email notification on assignment; owner is notified when target date is 3 days away. Critical
US-RI-004 PM As a PM, I want to view the risk register as a probability × impact heat map so that risk distribution across the project is visually clear. 5×5 matrix rendered with colour-coded cells (green/yellow/red); each cell shows count of risks; clicking a cell filters the risk list to those risks; heat map is printable. High
US-RI-005 PM As a PM, I want to log a risk update (new probability, new impact, status change, notes) so that the evolution of a risk is fully audited. Risk update creates a row in risk_updates; risk_score and severity are recalculated; if severity escalates to 'critical', immediate alert is sent; all updates visible in risk history timeline. High
US-RI-006 PM As a PM, I want to close a risk with a closure reason so that the register only shows active risks requiring attention. Closure requires selecting a status ('mitigated','closed','accepted'); 'accepted' requires a mandatory justification of min 20 chars; closed risks are hidden by default but accessible via "Show Closed" toggle. High
US-RI-007 PM / Stakeholder As a Stakeholder, I want to view an executive risk summary showing counts of open risks by severity so that I can quickly gauge project risk exposure. Risk summary widget shows: Critical (n), High (n), Medium (n), Low (n); embedded in the project dashboard executive view; hyperlinks to filtered risk list; exportable as PDF. Medium
US-RI-008 PM As a PM, I want to clone a risk from another project in my portfolio so that commonly recurring risks (e.g. resource attrition) don't have to be re-documented from scratch. Clone dialog shows projects the user has PM access to; cloned risk gets a new JL-RSK-XXXXX ID, project_id set to target project, and status reset to 'open'; original risk reference is noted in description; clone is auditable. Medium

3.8.5 Business Rules & Validation
  1. BR-RI-001: risk_score is always computed as probability × impact and is never directly editable. Severity is derived from the score band table.
  2. BR-RI-002: Risks with severity 'high' or 'critical' must have a mitigation_plan and contingency_plan defined before their status can be changed from 'open' to anything other than 'accepted'. Acceptance requires justification.
  3. BR-RI-003: When a risk's score increases to 'critical' (≥ 20) via a risk update, an immediate alert is dispatched to the PM, risk owner, and Admin.
  4. BR-RI-004: The project's RAG status is influenced by the risk register: if any 'critical' risk is 'open', the RAG is pushed toward Red unless manually overridden.
  5. BR-RI-005: All risk updates are immutable once saved; they form an audit trail. Corrections require a new update entry with a note referencing the correction.
  6. BR-RI-006: A risk owner must be an active project member. If a risk owner leaves the project, the risk is flagged "Unowned" and PM is notified to reassign.
3.8.6 Notification Triggers
Trigger EventRecipientsChannelTiming
New risk created (High/Critical severity)PM, AdminIn-app + EmailImmediate
Risk escalated to Critical (score ≥ 20)PM, Risk owner, AdminIn-app + Email + SMSImmediate
Risk assigned to ownerRisk ownerIn-app + EmailImmediate
Risk target_date within 3 days (status = open)Risk owner, PMIn-app + EmailScheduled
Risk target_date passed (status = open)Risk owner, PM, AdminIn-app + EmailDaily
Risk owner removed from project (risk unowned)PMIn-app + EmailImmediate
Risk status changed to Mitigated/ClosedPM, all project membersIn-appImmediate
3.9 Sub-Module: Project Reports
3.9.1 Functional Description

The Project Reports module provides a comprehensive, data-driven reporting layer across all PMS sub-modules. Reports are available on-demand and can be scheduled for recurring delivery. Each report supports role-based access control, rich filter sets, interactive charts, and export in both PDF and Excel formats. All reports use the same underlying data pipeline ensuring consistency between in-app charts and exported documents.

Common Features across all reports:
  • Date range filter (custom, last sprint, last month, last quarter, custom)
  • Export to PDF (formatted A4 landscape, company branding)
  • Export to Excel (.xlsx with multiple worksheets where applicable)
  • Schedule for recurring delivery via email (daily/weekly/sprint-end)
  • Shareable link (role-restricted, token-based URL with 7-day expiry)
  • All monetary amounts shown in INR (₹) with comma formatting
Report 3.9.1 — Sprint Report

Purpose: End-of-sprint summary showing what was committed vs. completed, team velocity, and items carried over.

Data Points:
  • Sprint name, goal, start/end dates, duration (days)
  • Total story points committed vs. completed vs. carried over
  • Sprint velocity (completed SPs)
  • List of completed items (ID, title, assignee, story points, type)
  • List of incomplete items with reason (if noted)
  • Defects raised vs. resolved during sprint
  • Scope creep count (items added after sprint started)
  • Sprint completion % (completed SPs / committed SPs)
Filters:
  • Project, Sprint (multi-select), Date range
Charts Included:
  • Burndown chart (ideal vs. actual)
  • Story point breakdown by item type (pie/donut)
  • Defect severity distribution (bar)
Roles: PM Tech Lead Stakeholder Admin
Export: PDF, Excel
Report 3.9.2 — Burndown Chart Report

Purpose: Visual and tabular daily burndown data to assess sprint execution health.

Data Points:
  • Day-by-day remaining story points (actual vs. ideal)
  • Daily scope change (items added/removed)
  • Percentage deviation from ideal burndown per day
  • Days when team is ahead/behind ideal pace
  • Raw data table: date, sprint_day, ideal_remaining, actual_remaining, delta
Filters:
  • Project, Sprint (single select)
Charts: Burndown line chart (interactive, zoomable)
Roles: PM Tech Lead Admin
Export: PDF (chart + data table), Excel (raw data)
Report 3.9.3 — Velocity Report

Purpose: Historical sprint velocity to support capacity planning and forecasting.

Data Points:
  • Per-sprint: sprint name, dates, committed SPs, completed SPs, velocity
  • Rolling average velocity (last 3 and last 6 sprints)
  • Velocity trend: % change sprint-over-sprint
  • Forecasted sprints to complete remaining backlog (based on avg velocity)
  • Story points carried over per sprint
Filters:
  • Project; number of sprints to include (last 3, 6, 10, all)
Charts: Grouped bar chart (committed vs. completed SPs per sprint) with average velocity line overlay
Roles: PM Tech Lead Admin
Export: PDF, Excel
Report 3.9.4 — Team Workload Report

Purpose: Per-member workload analysis for capacity planning and identifying over/under utilisation.

Data Points:
  • Per team member: name, role, available hours (leave-adjusted), allocated task hours, utilisation %
  • Tasks assigned per member (count, total estimated hours, total logged hours)
  • Cross-project allocation for each member
  • Unassigned tasks in the sprint (risk indicator)
  • Members with >100% utilisation (over-allocated)
Filters:
  • Project, Sprint/Date range, Role, Team member
Charts: Horizontal bar chart: utilisation % per member (colour-coded: green <80%, amber 80–100%, red >100%)
Roles: PM Admin
Export: PDF, Excel
Report 3.9.5 — Defect Density Report

Purpose: Quality metrics — defect counts normalised by features/story points to measure software quality over time.

Data Points:
  • Total defects by sprint, by environment, by severity
  • Defect density = (total defects / completed story points) per sprint
  • Defect reopen rate = (reopened / total resolved) × 100
  • Mean time to resolve (MTTR) per severity band
  • Defects by assignee (developer quality metrics — PM-only access)
  • Defect ageing: open > 7 / 14 / 30 days
  • Version-wise defect counts (found vs. fixed)
Filters:
  • Project, Sprint, Date range, Severity, Environment, Assignee, Version
Charts:
  • Defects per sprint (stacked bar by severity)
  • Defect density trend (line chart)
  • MTTR by severity (horizontal bar)
Roles: PM Tech Lead QA Admin
Export: PDF, Excel
Report 3.9.6 — Time Tracking Report

Purpose: Aggregated time logging data for project costing, timesheet verification, and estimation accuracy analysis.

Data Points:
  • Hours logged per employee per day/week/sprint
  • Total logged hours per task and per project
  • Estimated vs. actual hours variance per task (over/under estimation)
  • Hours logged by project and by epic/story
  • Cost calculation: hours_logged × member_hourly_rate = cost per member
  • Total project cost vs. budget utilisation
  • Unbilled hours (if applicable for client projects)
Filters:
  • Project, Employee, Date range, Task type, Epic
Charts:
  • Hours logged per day (area chart)
  • Estimated vs. actual hours per epic (grouped bar)
  • Cost breakdown by team member (pie)
Roles: PM Admin (employee can view own data only)
Export: PDF, Excel
Report 3.9.7 — Project Status Report

Purpose: Executive-level project health snapshot for stakeholder communication and governance reporting.

Data Points:
  • Project ID, name, PM, start/end dates, status, RAG status
  • Overall completion % (task-based)
  • Current sprint name, goal, and progress
  • Budget: approved vs. actual spend vs. % utilised
  • Milestone summary: on-track / at-risk / missed counts
  • Risk summary: open risks by severity
  • Open defect count by severity
  • Team size and composition
  • Key highlights (text fields: achievements, risks, decisions needed)
Filters:
  • Project (single), Report date
Charts:
  • RAG status indicator (traffic light)
  • Budget gauge (actual vs. approved)
  • Completion % donut chart
Roles: All authenticated users (data filtered by role)
Export: PDF (formatted with company branding), Excel
Note: This report is auto-generated weekly and emailed to stakeholders if configured in project_settings.notification_preferences.
Report 3.9.8 — Milestone Report

Purpose: Milestone delivery performance — on-time rate, at-risk milestones, and variance from target dates.

Data Points:
  • All milestones: ID, title, target date, actual date, status, completion %, owner
  • Milestone delivery variance: actual_date − target_date (+ = late, − = early)
  • On-time delivery rate: % of completed milestones delivered on or before target
  • At-risk milestones (target within 7 days, completion < 80%)
  • Missed milestones with root cause notes
  • Linked items per milestone and their completion status
  • Milestone dependency map (textual or visual)
Filters:
  • Project, Status (multi-select), Date range, Owner
Charts:
  • Milestone timeline (Gantt-style bars)
  • On-time vs. late vs. missed (donut)
  • Variance distribution (histogram)
Roles: PM Stakeholder Admin
Export: PDF, Excel

Share link is role-restricted (recipient must have project access); link has a 7-day expiry; PM can revoke the link; link generates a token stored in the DB; accessing an expired or revoked link returns a 403 page. Medium

3.9.4 Validation Rules
  • Report requests must include at least a project_id parameter; requests without valid project membership return HTTP 403
  • Date range filters: from must be before to; maximum range is 365 days for time tracking reports (performance protection)
  • Sprint report can only be generated for sprints with status 'active' or 'completed'; requesting for 'planning' sprints returns an informational message
  • PDF/Excel export: report_type must be one of the 8 defined report types; unsupported types return HTTP 400
  • Scheduled report: recipient_emails must be valid email addresses; at least one recipient required; maximum 20 recipients per schedule
  • Share link: expiry_hours must be 1–168 (1 hour to 7 days); default is 168 (7 days)
3.9.5 Business Rules
  1. BR-RPT-001: Report data is generated on-demand in real time from the live database. There is no pre-computed report cache for interactive reports; exports are generated asynchronously and delivered via a download link or email.
  2. BR-RPT-002: All monetary values in reports use INR (₹) formatting with comma separators. Hourly rates and cost calculations are visible only to Admin and PM roles; other roles see redacted versions.
  3. BR-RPT-003: The Time Tracking Report is the authoritative source for project cost calculation: total_cost = Σ(hours_logged × hourly_rate) per member. Discrepancies with Finance module data are flagged for reconciliation.
  4. BR-RPT-004: Shared report links enforce the requesting user's project access level at the time the link is accessed (not at creation time). If the link accessor's access is revoked, the link returns 403.
  5. BR-RPT-005: Large Excel exports (>10,000 rows) are generated asynchronously; user receives an in-app notification and email with a download link valid for 24 hours.
  6. BR-RPT-006: Report schedules are paused automatically when a project's status changes to 'on_hold', 'completed', or 'cancelled'. PM can manually resume them.
3.9.6 Notification Triggers

Report schedule paused (project on_hold/completed)PMIn-appImmediate
