3.10 Cross-Cutting Concerns & Integration Points
3.10.1 Authentication & Authorisation
  • All PMS API endpoints require a valid JWT bearer token issued by the central auth service
  • Role-based access control (RBAC) is enforced at both API gateway and service layer
  • Project-level access is enforced via project_members table; Admin role bypasses membership check
  • All sensitive field visibility (hourly_rate, budget, actual_cost) restricted to Admin and PM roles
  • Token expiry: 1 hour; refresh token: 7 days
3.10.2 Audit Logging
  • Every mutating API call (POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE) logs: user_id, action, entity_type, entity_id, payload_diff, timestamp, IP address
  • Audit logs are immutable and stored in a separate audit_log table
  • Retention: 5 years for compliance purposes
  • Audit log is exportable by Admin only
3.10.3 File Storage
  • All attachments stored in object storage (AWS S3 or compatible); file metadata in DB
  • Files accessed via pre-signed URLs (15-minute expiry) to prevent direct URL enumeration
  • Virus scan on all uploads (ClamAV or cloud-native scanner); infected files rejected
  • Maximum total storage per project: 5 GB (configurable by Admin)
3.10.4 Module Integrations
PMS ModuleIntegrated ModuleIntegration Type
Team ManagementHR — Employee MasterRead (employee list, department, skills)
Team ManagementHR — Leave ManagementRead (approved leaves for capacity calc)
Time TrackingHR — TimesheetWrite (daily sync of task time logs)
Project Budget/CostFinance — Cost CentreRead/Write (budget approval, actual cost)
All PMS modulesNotifications ServicePublish (events → in-app, email, SMS)
ReportsDocument ManagementWrite (archived reports stored centrally)
3.10.5 Performance Requirements
  • Project dashboard load time: < 3 seconds (P95)
  • Sprint board real-time update: < 2 seconds from action to all visible clients
  • Report generation (interactive): < 5 seconds
  • Report export (PDF/Excel): < 30 seconds; async for >10k rows
  • API response time (P95): < 500ms for read endpoints, < 1 second for write endpoints
  • Concurrent users per project: up to 50 with consistent performance
3.10.6 Data Retention & Archival
  • Active project data: retained indefinitely
  • Completed project data: retained for 7 years (configurable)
  • Cancelled project data: retained for 1 year, then archived to cold storage
  • Sprint burndown snapshots: retained for 3 years
  • Task history records: retained for the life of the project + 7 years

Module 4.1

Defect Tracker

ID Format: JL-DFT-XXXXX
3 DB Tables 8 User Stories 15 API Endpoints 6 Notif. Triggers
Overview

The Defect Tracker module provides a standalone operational system for reporting, triaging, and resolving production and UAT defects across Jeshan Labs. It operates independently of project-level defect tracking — capturing defects raised against live or staging environments and driving them through a structured lifecycle from initial report to verified closure. SLA timelines are automatically computed on defect creation using the configurable defect_sla_config matrix keyed to severity and priority. A scheduled background job monitors open defects against their escalation thresholds and triggers automatic escalation to the assignee's manager when breached. The module integrates with the central Notification Engine for real-time alerting and exposes a full reporting API for SLA compliance dashboards and management exports.

Database Schema
it_defects Primary defect record — one row per reported defect
ColumnTypeKeyNullDescription
idBIGSERIALPKNOInternal auto-increment identifier
defect_idVARCHAR(20)UQNOHuman-readable identifier — format JL-DFT-XXXXX; auto-generated on creation
titleVARCHAR(255)NOShort descriptive title of the defect (used in all listings and notifications)
descriptionTEXTNOFull detailed description of the observed defect, supports Markdown
module_affectedVARCHAR(100)YESApplication module or component where defect was observed
severityVARCHAR(20)NODefect severity: critical | high | medium | low
priorityVARCHAR(5)NOBusiness priority level: P1 | P2 | P3 | P4; validated against severity rules
statusVARCHAR(30)NOLifecycle status: open | in_progress | resolved | closed | rejected | duplicate
reported_byBIGINTFKNOReferences employees.id — employee who reported the defect
assigned_toBIGINTFKYESReferences employees.id — developer currently assigned to fix the defect
department_idBIGINTFKYESReferences departments.id — owning department for reporting purposes
environmentVARCHAR(20)NOEnvironment where defect was found: production | uat | staging
steps_to_reproduceTEXTYESStep-by-step numbered instructions to consistently reproduce the defect
expected_behaviorTEXTYESDescription of the expected correct system behavior
actual_behaviorTEXTYESDescription of the actual (incorrect) behavior observed
resolution_notesTEXTYESMandatory when transitioning to resolved or closed — describes fix applied
attachmentsJSONBYESArray of file metadata: [{"name":"...","url":"...","type":"...","size":0}]
due_dateTIMESTAMPTZYESSLA-computed resolution deadline; set on creation from defect_sla_config
resolved_atTIMESTAMPTZYESTimestamp when status was last set to resolved
created_atTIMESTAMPTZNORecord creation timestamp; auto-set by database default
updated_atTIMESTAMPTZNOLast modification timestamp; auto-updated via trigger
is_deletedBOOLEANNOSoft-delete flag; default FALSE; deleted records excluded from all standard queries
defect_sla_config SLA matrix — keyed to severity + priority combination
ColumnTypeKeyNullDescription
idBIGSERIALPKNOInternal identifier
severityVARCHAR(20)NOSeverity level this rule applies to (e.g. critical)
priorityVARCHAR(5)NOPriority level this rule applies to (e.g. P1)
response_hoursINTEGERNOMaximum hours for first acknowledgement of the defect by assignee
resolution_hoursINTEGERNOMaximum hours from creation to resolved status
escalation_hoursINTEGERNOHours elapsed before auto-escalation triggers; must be ≤ resolution_hours
is_activeBOOLEANNOInactive rules are ignored during SLA computation; default TRUE
created_atTIMESTAMPTZNORecord creation timestamp
defect_escalations Escalation log — one row per escalation event per defect
ColumnTypeKeyNullDescription
idBIGSERIALPKNOInternal identifier
defect_idBIGINTFKNOReferences it_defects.id — the defect being escalated
escalated_toBIGINTFKNOReferences employees.id — escalation recipient (typically manager)
escalation_reasonTEXTNOFree-text reason; auto-filled for system escalations (e.g. "SLA breach — 8h elapsed")
escalated_atTIMESTAMPTZNOTimestamp when escalation was triggered
resolved_atTIMESTAMPTZYESTimestamp when the escalated defect was ultimately resolved
User Stories
US-DFT-001
Report a New Defect
As a tester or business user, I want to report a defect by providing a title, description, environment, severity, steps to reproduce, expected vs. actual behavior, and optional screenshots, so that the development team has everything needed to investigate and reproduce the issue.
US-DFT-002
Triage and Assign Defect
As a QA lead or team lead, I want to review newly reported defects, adjust severity and priority if needed, and assign them to the appropriate developer or team, so that every defect has a responsible owner and the correct SLA applied.
US-DFT-003
Track SLA Compliance
As an IT manager, I want a real-time SLA dashboard showing response time, elapsed time, and breach status for every open defect, with visual indicators (green/amber/red), so that I can proactively intervene before SLAs are breached.
US-DFT-004
Resolve and Close Defect
As a developer, I want to mark a defect as resolved with mandatory resolution notes describing the root cause and fix applied, and as a reporter, I want to verify the fix and close the defect, so that the lifecycle is properly documented and stakeholders are informed.
US-DFT-005
View Defect Dashboard
As any portal user with access, I want a summary dashboard showing defect counts by status, severity, and environment, plus weekly trend charts and SLA compliance rate, so that I can quickly assess the current defect health of the system.
US-DFT-006
Escalate Overdue Defect
As a system scheduler, I want to automatically escalate defects that exceed their configured escalation threshold to the assignee's direct manager, and as a manager, I want to manually escalate critical defects at any time, so that high-impact issues are never silently stalled.
US-DFT-007
Filter and Search Defects
As a developer or tester, I want to filter defects by status, severity, priority, environment, module, assigned-to, and date range, and perform a full-text search across title and description, so that I can quickly locate relevant defects without scrolling through the full list.
US-DFT-008
Export Defect Reports
As a project manager or QA lead, I want to export the current filtered defect list to CSV or Excel with all columns including SLA metrics and resolution notes, so that I can share formal defect status reports with clients or senior management outside the portal.
Business Rules
BR-DFT-001 — SLA Auto-Computation: On defect creation, the API looks up the active defect_sla_config row matching the submitted severity + priority pair and sets due_date = created_at + resolution_hours. If no matching config row is found, the defect is created but flagged for manual SLA assignment.
BR-DFT-002 — Auto-Escalation Scheduler: A cron job running every 15 minutes checks for open/in-progress defects where created_at + escalation_hours ≤ NOW() and no active escalation record exists. It creates a defect_escalations row, notifies the assignee's reports_to manager, and logs the action in the audit trail.
BR-DFT-003 — Severity–Priority Alignment: A critical defect cannot be assigned a priority lower than P2. A high defect cannot be lower than P3. Both the API endpoint and the UI enforce this rule with clear error messaging.
BR-DFT-004 — Duplicate Detection: On defect creation, the system performs a trigram similarity check against the titles of all open, non-deleted defects in the same module. If any existing defect exceeds 85% similarity, a warning is displayed to the reporter with a link to the candidate duplicate. The reporter may proceed with creation or set the status directly to duplicate and link the parent defect.
BR-DFT-005 — Mandatory Resolution Notes: Any API call that transitions status to resolved or closed is rejected with HTTP 422 if resolution_notes is null, empty, or fewer than 20 characters. This rule cannot be bypassed by admin roles.
BR-DFT-006 — Reopen Window: A closed defect can be reopened only within 7 calendar days of resolved_at. After this window, users must raise a new defect. Reopening resets the SLA clock (due_date is recalculated from the reopen timestamp) and sends a notification to the original assignee.
BR-DFT-007 — Soft Delete Only: Defects are never physically deleted from the database. The DELETE /api/v1/defects/:id endpoint sets is_deleted = TRUE. Soft-deleted defects are excluded from all standard queries, SLA calculations, and dashboard counts. Only users with the ADMIN role can perform soft deletes.
API Endpoints
MethodEndpointDescriptionAuth

GET/api/v1/defects/:idRetrieve single defect record including escalation history and SLA elapsed timeJWT
PUT/api/v1/defects/:idFull update of defect fields; validates severity–priority alignment; recalculates due_date if severity/priority changedJWT + Role
PATCH/api/v1/defects/:id/statusStatus transition; enforces resolution notes rule for resolved/closed; reopen enforces 7-day windowJWT + Role

Notification Triggers
New Defect Assigned — Sent to assigned_to employee via in-app notification and email when a defect is newly assigned or reassigned. Payload includes defect ID, title, severity, priority, and computed due_date.
SLA Warning at 75% — Sent to the assignee when 75% of the resolution_hours window has elapsed without resolution. A second reminder fires at 90%.
SLA Breached — Sent to the assignee and their direct manager when the full resolution_hours window has elapsed. The defect card in the portal turns red.
Defect Escalated — Sent to the escalation recipient and the original reporter when an escalation record is created, whether via auto-scheduler or manual escalation by a lead.
Defect Resolved — Sent to the original reporter when a defect transitions to resolved, including a summary of resolution notes so the reporter can verify the fix.
Defect Reopened — Sent to the current assignee and the team lead when a closed/resolved defect is reopened, including the reason provided by the user triggering the reopen.
Module 4.2

IT Services / Help Desk

Ticket ID: JL-TKT-XXXXX  ·  Change Request ID: JL-CHG-XXXXX
7 DB Tables 10 User Stories 20 API Endpoints 7 Notif. Triggers
Overview

The IT Services / Help Desk module provides a full ITSM-aligned support ticketing system for Jeshan Labs employees. It covers all employee IT support needs — hardware issues, software requests, user access provisioning, and network problems — through a structured ticket lifecycle. The module includes a dynamic service catalog that drives category-based SLA assignment and auto-routing to the appropriate support group. A built-in Knowledge Base enables self-service deflection by surfacing relevant articles before ticket submission. Scheduled maintenance windows and formal change request management round out the module, giving IT teams complete control over both reactive support and proactive change management. Satisfaction surveys are automatically dispatched after ticket closure to measure support quality over time.

User Stories
US-TKT-001
Raise an IT Support Ticket
As an employee, I want to raise an IT support ticket by selecting a service catalog category, providing a description, and optionally attaching screenshots, so that the IT team can address my issue with the appropriate SLA applied automatically.
US-TKT-002
Track My Ticket Status
As an employee, I want to see a real-time status view of all my open and recent tickets — including current status, assigned agent, and due date — so that I always know the progress of my requests without needing to contact IT directly.
US-TKT-003
Comment on a Ticket
As an employee or IT agent, I want to add public comments to a ticket for conversation with the other party, and as an IT agent, I also want to add internal-only notes visible only to the support team, so that all ticket context is preserved in one place.
US-TKT-004
Auto-Assign Ticket by Category
As an IT manager, I want tickets to be automatically routed to the correct IT support group and agent pool based on the selected service catalog category, so that new tickets never sit unassigned and response time SLA begins immediately.
US-TKT-005
Search Knowledge Base
As an employee, I want to search the Knowledge Base for self-help articles before and during ticket submission, so that I can resolve common issues independently without raising a ticket, reducing IT support workload.
US-TKT-006
Rate Ticket Resolution
As an employee, I want to receive a satisfaction survey via notification after my ticket is closed, rating the resolution quality from 1 to 5, so that IT management can track service quality trends over time.
US-TKT-007
Escalate an Open Ticket
As an employee or IT manager, I want to escalate a ticket that is approaching or has breached its SLA to a senior IT agent or IT manager, so that critical or stalled requests receive the priority they require.
US-TKT-008
View Scheduled Maintenance Windows
As any employee, I want to see a calendar of upcoming scheduled IT maintenance windows with affected systems listed, so that I can plan work around expected downtimes and set appropriate expectations with clients.
US-TKT-009
Submit a Change Request
As an IT agent or system owner, I want to formally document an IT change request with impact assessment, risk level, and planned timing, submit it for approval, and track its lifecycle through to implementation, so that all system changes follow the defined change management process.
US-TKT-010
View IT SLA and Performance Dashboard
As an IT manager, I want a consolidated dashboard showing ticket volumes by category, SLA compliance rates, average resolution time, first-response rates, satisfaction scores, and KB deflection rates, so that I can continuously improve IT service delivery.
Business Rules
BR-TKT-001 — SLA by Service Catalog: On ticket creation, the SLA is computed directly from service_catalog.sla_hours for the selected catalog item. due_date = created_at + sla_hours. If no catalog entry is matched, a default SLA of 24 hours is applied and the ticket is flagged for manual SLA review.
BR-TKT-002 — Auto-Assignment Rules: On ticket creation, the system sets assigned_group from service_catalog.assignee_group. Individual assigned_to is determined by round-robin within the group, weighted by current open ticket count. If the group has no active agents, the ticket remains group-assigned with an escalation alert to the IT manager.
BR-TKT-003 — First Response Tracking: first_response_at is set automatically when the assigned agent either (a) posts a public comment, or (b) changes the ticket status away from open. The first-response SLA window is 20% of sla_hours (minimum 1 hour). Breach of first-response SLA triggers an immediate alert to the IT manager.
BR-TKT-004 — Satisfaction Survey: When a ticket transitions to closed, the system automatically dispatches a satisfaction survey notification to the requester after a 2-hour delay (to allow resolution review). Surveys expire after 72 hours. Only one survey is sent per ticket regardless of close/reopen cycles.
BR-TKT-005 — KB Deflection Tracking: When an employee views a KB article during the ticket creation flow and clicks "This resolved my issue — no ticket needed", the article's helpful_count is incremented and the event is logged for deflection rate reporting. Deflection rate is tracked as a KPI on the IT dashboard.
BR-TKT-006 — Change Request Approval: A change request with risk_level of high or critical requires a second approver in addition to the primary approver_id. Emergency changes bypass the standard approval queue and auto-notify the IT Director. All approved changes are linked to a scheduled_maintenance record.
BR-TKT-007 — Pending User Timeout: If a ticket is set to pending_user (awaiting information from the requester) and the requester does not respond within 48 hours, the ticket is automatically resolved with a standard "No response from requester" resolution note. The requester is notified 24 hours before auto-closure.

Notification Triggers
Ticket Raised — Sent to the auto-assigned IT agent and group lead when a new ticket is created. Includes ticket ID, subject, category, priority, and SLA due date.
Ticket Assigned — Sent to the newly assigned agent when a ticket is manually assigned or reassigned. Previous assignee is also notified of the change.
New Comment Added — Sent to the other party (requester receives agent public comments; agent receives requester comments). Internal comments do not trigger notifications to the requester.
SLA Breach Alert — Sent at 75% and 100% of the SLA window elapsed. The 100% breach notification is copied to the IT manager and group lead.
Ticket Resolved — Sent to the requester when status changes to resolved, including resolution notes summary. Followed by the satisfaction survey after 2 hours.
Maintenance Window Notice — Sent to all employees 24 hours before a scheduled maintenance window, listing affected systems and the planned duration.
Change Request Approval — Sent to the designated approver when a change request is submitted for approval. Approval/rejection decision triggers a notification back to the change requester.
Module 4.3

Asset Management

Asset ID: JL-AST-XXXXX  ·  License ID: JL-LIC-XXXXX
7 DB Tables 8 User Stories 18 API Endpoints 5 Notif. Triggers
Overview

The Asset Management module provides full lifecycle management of all IT hardware and software assets owned by Jeshan Labs — from initial procurement registration through assignment, maintenance scheduling, and eventual disposal. Every physical asset is assigned a unique identifier and a QR code for rapid lookup and audit purposes. Assignment history is fully tracked, allowing the organization to know which employee held any asset at any point in time. Software license tracking ensures seat counts are monitored, preventing over-deployment of licensed software. The disposal workflow enforces approval gates to ensure assets are retired responsibly with salvage value recorded. Automated alerts notify relevant stakeholders of maintenance due dates, warranty expirations, and license renewal deadlines.

User Stories
US-AST-001
Register a New Asset

US-AST-002
Assign Asset to Employee
As an IT admin, I want to assign an available asset to a specific employee, recording the condition at assignment and the assigning admin, so that the asset status updates to assigned and the employee is formally responsible for the device.
US-AST-003
Track Asset Location and Status
As an IT manager, I want to see the current location and status of any asset at any time, including who it's assigned to, its physical location when unassigned, and its condition history, so that no asset is unaccounted for.
US-AST-004
Schedule Asset Maintenance
As an IT admin, I want to schedule preventive or corrective maintenance for an asset, specifying the type, description, date, and vendor if external, so that the asset's maintenance history is recorded and upcoming maintenance is visible in the calendar view.
US-AST-005
Track Software License Utilization
As an IT manager, I want to view software license records showing total seats, used seats, expiry dates, and cost, and receive alerts before licenses expire, so that we are never non-compliant and renewals are planned ahead of time.
US-AST-006
Initiate Asset Disposal
As an IT admin, I want to raise a disposal request for an end-of-life or damaged asset specifying disposal reason and method, route it for approval, and upon approval record the salvage value and disposal date, so that retired assets are properly de-registered and Finance is informed of salvage value.
US-AST-007
Conduct Asset Audit / Inventory Check
As an IT auditor, I want to scan asset QR codes using a mobile device to confirm physical presence and record the audit result against the asset record, so that annual physical inventories can be conducted efficiently with a full digital audit trail.
US-AST-008
QR Code Asset Lookup
As any IT team member, I want to scan an asset's QR code label and instantly see its current status, assignment, maintenance history, and warranty details, so that quick field verification requires no manual search in the portal.
Business Rules
BR-AST-001 — Unique Serial Numbers: The serial_number column has a unique constraint across all non-deleted assets. Any attempt to register an asset with a duplicate serial number is rejected with a descriptive error that links to the existing asset record.
BR-AST-002 — Assignment Constraints: An asset with status other than available cannot be assigned. Attempting to assign a disposed, under_maintenance, or lost asset returns HTTP 422. An employee may have multiple assets assigned simultaneously across different categories.
BR-AST-003 — Assignment History Integrity: Assets are never "re-assigned" by overwriting an existing assignment record. Each assignment event creates a new asset_assignments row. The previous row's returned_at is set automatically when a new assignment is created for the same asset.
BR-AST-004 — Maintenance Status Lock: When a maintenance record with status in_progress exists for an asset, the asset's status is automatically updated to under_maintenance and assignment is blocked until the maintenance record reaches completed or cancelled.
BR-AST-005 — License Seat Cap Enforcement: The API prevents used_seats from exceeding total_seats at all times. Allocation of a software license to a new employee is rejected if seats are exhausted. The threshold warning notification fires when used_seats / total_seats ≥ 90%.
BR-AST-006 — Disposal Approval Workflow: A disposal request must be approved by an IT Manager (or above) before the asset status can be set to disposed. Emergency disposals (theft/loss) may bypass approval but require the IT Director to be notified automatically. Approved disposal records are immutable.
BR-AST-007 — QR Code Generation: A QR code is automatically generated for each asset on creation, encoding the asset's asset_id as the payload. QR codes are regenerated if an asset ID is ever changed (admin-only operation). The QR code endpoint is publicly accessible (no auth) for field scanning purposes but returns only limited non-sensitive asset metadata.
API Endpoints

Notification Triggers
Asset Assigned — Sent to the employee when an asset is assigned to them, including asset details (ID, name, category) and a reminder of their responsibility for the device. Also sent to the IT admin as confirmation.
Asset Returned — Sent to the IT admin and asset manager when an employee returns an asset, including the recorded condition at return to flag any damage for follow-up.
Maintenance Due in 7 Days — Sent to the IT admin responsible for the asset when a scheduled maintenance event is 7 days away, including asset name, type, and maintenance description.
Warranty Expiry in 30 Days — Sent to the IT manager when an asset's warranty_expiry is within 30 calendar days. A second notification fires at 7 days.
License Expiry / Seat Threshold Alert — Sent to the IT manager 60 days and 30 days before a software license expires. Also sent when used_seats / total_seats ≥ 90%, recommending procurement of additional seats.
Module 4.4

Workflow Automation

Workflow ID: JL-WKF-XXXXX  ·  Execution ID: JL-WEX-XXXXX
6 DB Tables 7 User Stories 15 API Endpoints 5 Notif. Triggers
Overview

The Workflow Automation module provides a no-code, configurable engine for automating multi-step approval processes throughout the Jeshan Labs portal. Rather than embedding hard-coded approval logic in each business module (leave requests, expense claims, purchase orders, asset disposals), this engine allows administrators to define reusable workflow definitions with configurable approval chains, conditional branching, timeout escalation policies, and delegation rules. Each workflow definition is version-controlled, allowing changes to be deployed without disrupting in-flight executions. A library of pre-built system templates covers the most common business processes out of the box. The pending approvals inbox surfaces all outstanding approval tasks across all workflows in a single unified view for each approver.

Database Schema
workflow_definitions Versioned workflow blueprint — one row per workflow version
ColumnTypeKeyNullDescription
idBIGSERIALPKNOInternal identifier
workflow_idVARCHAR(20)UQNOHuman-readable workflow ID — format JL-WKF-XXXXX
nameVARCHAR(200)NODescriptive workflow name (e.g. "Leave Approval — Manager + HR")
descriptionTEXTYESPurpose and scope of the workflow; shown to admins in the workflow builder
trigger_moduleVARCHAR(100)NOModule that triggers this workflow (e.g. leave, expense, asset_disposal)
trigger_eventVARCHAR(100)NOSpecific event within the module (e.g. leave.submitted, expense.submitted)
stepsJSONBNOOrdered step definitions snapshot; denormalized copy for execution; authoritative source is workflow_steps
is_activeBOOLEANNOOnly one active version per workflow family may exist at any time
versionINTEGERNOMonotonically increasing version number; new publish increments version
created_byBIGINTFKNOReferences employees.id — admin who created or published this version
created_atTIMESTAMPTZNOVersion publish timestamp
workflow_steps Individual steps within a workflow definition
ColumnTypeKeyNullDescription
idBIGSERIALPKNOInternal identifier

step_typeVARCHAR(30)NOapproval | notification | condition | action
titleVARCHAR(200)NOHuman-readable step label displayed in the workflow builder and execution view
approver_typeVARCHAR(30)YESrole | user | manager | department_head; applicable when step_type = approval

actionsJSONBYESActions to perform on step execution: update field, send notification, call webhook

reminder_sent_atTIMESTAMPTZYESTimestamp of last reminder notification; prevents duplicate reminders

ColumnTypeKeyNullDescription

User Stories
US-WKF-001
Create a Workflow Definition
As an IT admin or HR admin, I want to create a new workflow by defining the trigger event, configuring an ordered sequence of steps (approval, notification, condition, action), and setting timeout policies per step, so that multi-step approval processes are automated without custom code.
US-WKF-002
Define Approval Chain
As an admin, I want to configure each approval step with a dynamic approver type (role, named user, direct manager, or department head), set conditional branching rules based on request values (e.g. amount, duration), and define timeout behavior (escalate, auto-approve, auto-reject), so that approvals reflect real business hierarchies.
US-WKF-003
Test Workflow Before Publishing
As an admin, I want to test a workflow in a sandbox mode by submitting a simulated trigger payload and stepping through the execution logic, so that configuration errors are caught before the workflow is activated for real business records.
US-WKF-004
Monitor Active Executions
As an IT admin or manager, I want to view a live execution monitor showing all running workflow instances — their current step, time elapsed, and any at-risk (near-timeout) tasks — so that I can intervene before a bottleneck causes a business delay.
US-WKF-005
Delegate an Approval Task
As an approver who is unavailable (e.g. on leave), I want to delegate my pending approval tasks to a colleague so that workflows are not stalled during my absence. Delegated approvals are actioned by the delegate but attributed to the original approver in the audit log.
US-WKF-006
Auto-Escalate on Timeout
As a system process, I want to automatically handle step timeouts per the configured policy — escalate to the approver's manager, auto-approve, or auto-reject — and notify all relevant parties, so that workflow execution never stalls indefinitely on an unresponsive approver.
US-WKF-007
View Pending Approvals Inbox
As any employee with pending approval tasks, I want a unified inbox showing all my outstanding approvals across all workflow types — with the request details, due date, and one-click approve/reject actions — so that I never miss an approval task regardless of which module originated it.
Business Rules
BR-WKF-001 — Step Ordering: step_order values within a workflow must be unique and contiguous. The engine executes steps in ascending order. Steps of type condition evaluate their JSONB condition rules against the execution context and may skip subsequent steps or redirect to a named step.
BR-WKF-002 — Circular Approval Prevention: The workflow engine validates at execution time that no approver in the chain would be approving their own request. If the computed approver resolves to the same employee as trigger_record.created_by, the engine automatically escalates to the next level in the hierarchy.
BR-WKF-003 — Version Control: Publishing a new version of a workflow definition increments version and deactivates the previous version. All in-flight executions on the old version continue to completion on the version they started with. New triggers always use the latest active version.
BR-WKF-004 — Timeout Escalation Chain: When on_timeout = escalate, the engine finds the approver's reports_to manager and creates a new workflow_approvals row for them. The escalation chain terminates at the CEO level (no further reports_to) where it falls back to auto_approve to prevent permanent stalling.
BR-WKF-005 — Delegation Limits: Delegation is limited to one level — a delegate cannot re-delegate. Delegation automatically expires when the original approver's out-of-office status is lifted (HR module integration) or after a maximum of 30 days. The audit log always records both the original approver and the delegate.
BR-WKF-006 — Rejection Cascade: If any approval step returns a rejected decision, the entire workflow execution moves to completed status with the final outcome set to rejected. The triggering business record is updated accordingly, and the submitter is immediately notified with the rejection reason.
BR-WKF-007 — Test Mode Isolation: Workflows executed in test/sandbox mode (via the ?dry_run=true flag) create temporary workflow_executions rows marked with a test flag. These records are automatically purged after 24 hours and do not trigger any real notifications or mutations to business records.
API Endpoints
MethodEndpointDescriptionAuth
GET/api/v1/workflowsList workflow definitions with filter by trigger_module and active status; includes step count and execution statsJWT + Admin
POST/api/v1/workflowsCreate new workflow definition; auto-generates workflow_id; created in draft state (version 1, inactive)JWT + Admin
GET/api/v1/workflows/:idRetrieve workflow definition with all steps; supports ?version=N for historical version retrievalJWT + Admin
PUT/api/v1/workflows/:idUpdate workflow definition and steps (draft only); publishes as new version if currently activeJWT + Admin
POST/api/v1/workflows/:id/publishActivate the workflow; deactivates the previous version; validates no duplicate active trigger existsJWT + Admin
POST/api/v1/workflows/:id/executeTrigger a workflow execution; ?dry_run=true for sandbox mode; returns execution_idJWT (System)
GET/api/v1/workflow-executionsList all executions with filter by workflow, status, module, date; used by admin monitor dashboardJWT + Admin
GET/api/v1/workflow-executions/:idRetrieve execution detail: all step outcomes, decisions, comments, and timelineJWT + Role
POST/api/v1/workflow-executions/:id/cancelCancel a running execution; only permitted for the originating module admin; notifies submitterJWT + Admin
GET/api/v1/workflow-approvals/pendingMy pending approvals inbox: all pending approval tasks assigned to the authenticated user across all workflowsJWT
POST/api/v1/workflow-approvals/:id/decideSubmit approval decision (approved / rejected) with optional comments; advances or terminates executionJWT
POST/api/v1/workflow-approvals/:id/delegateDelegate approval task to another employee; validates no circular delegation; records original approverJWT
GET/api/v1/workflow-templatesList all system and custom workflow templates; filtered by categoryJWT + Admin
POST/api/v1/workflow-templates/:id/useCreate a new workflow definition pre-populated from this template; increments usage_countJWT + Admin
GET/api/v1/workflows/stats/dashboardWorkflow engine KPIs: active executions, avg completion time, timeout rate, approval rate by workflow typeJWT + Admin
Notification Triggers
Approval Requested — Sent to each approver_id when a new workflow_approvals task is created for them. Includes the request subject, submitter name, context summary, and a deep link to the approval inbox. Also delivered as an in-app badge notification.
Approval Reminder — Sent at 50% and 80% of the step timeout_hours window if the approval task remains pending. Reminder throttled by reminder_sent_at to prevent duplicate sends.
Step Escalated — Sent to the escalation target (the approver's manager) when a step times out with policy escalate. The original approver also receives a notification that the task has been escalated.
Workflow Approved / Completed — Sent to the original submitter when the full workflow execution reaches completed status with all steps approved, including the final decision and any approver comments.
Workflow Rejected / Failed — Sent to the original submitter when any approval step returns rejected or when execution status reaches failed, including the step that was rejected and the reason provided by the approver.
Section 4.10

Cross-Module Integration Map

How Operations modules interact with the wider Jeshan Labs portal platform
Integration Overview

The four Operations modules (Defect Tracker, IT Services / Help Desk, Asset Management, Workflow Automation) do not operate as silos. They rely on shared platform services and bidirectionally integrate with the HR, Projects, Finance, Notifications, and Audit Log platform modules. The table below enumerates each Operations module, the external modules it consumes data from, the data it publishes to those modules, and the specific integration points.

Module Integration Matrix
Operations Module
Integration Points & Data Flows
4.1
Defect Tracker
← HR Module: Reads employees and departments for reported_by, assigned_to, department_id lookups and manager escalation chains. employees.id employees.reports_to departments.id
← Projects Module: Defects can be optionally linked to a project task via project_task_id; the Projects module reads defect counts for its task health indicators. project_tasks.id defect count by task
→ Notifications Engine: Publishes assignment, SLA breach, escalation, and resolution events to the Notification Engine for delivery via in-app and email channels. notification.send() recipient: assigned_to recipient: manager
→ Audit Log: All defect status changes, assignments, and escalations are written to the central audit_events table with entity type defect. audit_events entity_type: defect
4.2
IT Help Desk
← HR Module: Reads employee records for requester and agent identity. Auto-assignment logic reads the agent's group membership from HR role/team tables. employees.id employee.team employee.roles
→ Workflow Automation: Change requests (JL-CHG-XXXXX) trigger the "IT Change Approval" workflow; the change request status is updated based on the workflow execution outcome. trigger_event: cr.submitted workflow: JL-WKF-ITCHG
← / → Asset Module: Tickets of category Hardware can reference an asset_id; asset status is updated to under_maintenance when a hardware ticket opens and reverted on resolution. assets.asset_id assets.status
→ Notifications Engine / Audit Log: Ticket lifecycle events and change request approvals route through the Notifications Engine. All ticket CRUD actions write to audit_events with entity type ticket. notification.send() audit_events entity_type: ticket
4.3
Asset Management
← HR Module: Reads employees for assignment records. On employee offboarding (HR event employee.offboarded), the Asset module auto-triggers a return request for all assets assigned to the departing employee. employees.id event: employee.offboarded
→ Finance Module: Asset procurement cost, maintenance cost, and disposal salvage value are published to the Finance module for Fixed Asset Register maintenance and depreciation calculation. purchase_cost maintenance.cost disposal.salvage_value depreciation_rate
→ Workflow Automation: Asset disposal requests trigger the "Asset Disposal Approval" workflow (JL-WKF-ASTDSP), requiring IT Manager approval before assets.status can transition to disposed. trigger_event: asset_disposal.requested workflow: JL-WKF-ASTDSP
→ Notifications Engine / Audit Log: Warranty and license expiry alerts are routed via the Notifications Engine. All asset state changes create immutable rows in asset_audit_logs and also write to the central audit_events table. notification.send() audit_events entity_type: asset
4.4
Workflow Engine
← HR Module: Resolves dynamic approver types at execution time — manager resolves via employees.reports_to, department_head resolves via departments.head_id, role resolves via employee_roles. Also reads leave calendar to detect approver absence for delegation logic. employees.reports_to departments.head_id employee_roles leave.is_on_leave
→ All Triggering Modules: On workflow completion, the engine calls back into the triggering module's API to update the record status (e.g. sets leave request to approved, expense claim to approved, change request to approved). leave_requests.status expense_claims.status change_requests.status asset_disposal.status
→ Notifications Engine: All workflow approval requests, reminders, escalations, and outcomes are delivered via the central Notifications Engine. The workflow engine does not send notifications directly. notification.send() channel: in_app channel: email
→ Audit Log: Every step decision, delegation, timeout, and escalation event is written to the central audit_events table with entity type workflow_execution and sub-entity references to the triggering record. audit_events entity_type: workflow_execution
Shared Platform Services Consumed by All Operations Modules
HR Module
Consumed data: Employee directory, department hierarchy, reporting lines, roles & permissions, team membership, leave calendar, onboarding/offboarding events.
Access pattern: Read-only via internal HR API service; no direct DB joins across module boundaries.
Projects Module
Consumed data: Project and task identifiers for defect-to-task linking.
Published data: Defect counts per task feed the task health indicator widget in the Projects module.
Access pattern: Bidirectional via REST API callbacks.
Finance Module
Published data: Asset costs (purchase, maintenance, disposal salvage) feed the Fixed Asset Register. Vendor payments from maintenance records are linked to Finance purchase orders.
Access pattern: Event-driven publish via internal message queue on asset lifecycle events.
Notifications Engine
Published events: All Operations modules publish notification events (assignment, SLA breach, approval request, etc.) to the central Notification Engine.
Channels supported: In-app bell, email, (future) SMS / push.
Access pattern: Fire-and-forget publish; delivery guarantees handled by the Engine.
Central Audit Log
Published data: All create, update, status change, and delete operations from all Operations modules write immutable records to the central audit_events table, including actor ID, timestamp, entity type, entity ID, and diff payload.
Access pattern: Synchronous write on every mutation; never bypassed.
Auth & RBAC
Consumed service: All Operations API endpoints enforce JWT authentication and role-based access control via the central Auth service. Role checks determine view, create, update, approve, and admin permissions per module.
Access pattern: Middleware-level enforcement on every request.

Module 5.1

Training & Learning Management System (LMS)

Internal employee skill development, mandatory compliance training, and certification tracking
Doc ID: JL-FSD-005-01
Status: Active
● 5.1.1   Module Overview

The Training LMS module provides a comprehensive platform for managing the full lifecycle of employee learning — from course authoring and publishing through enrollment, progress tracking, quizzing, certification issuance, and compliance reporting. It supports both self-directed and mandatory training programmes and integrates with the HR module for role-based auto-assignment.

● 5.1.4   Business Rules
BR-001
Mandatory Auto-Enrollment: When an employee is onboarded (or role changes), the system evaluates all active mandatory_training_rules and creates course_enrollments with enrollment_type = mandatory and due_date = joining_date + due_days_from_joining.
BR-002
Prerequisite Enforcement: The system must prevent enrollment in a course if the learner has not completed all courses listed in its prerequisites array with a status of completed.
BR-003
Passing Score: An enrollment is only marked completed after the employee scores ≥ passing_score in the final assessment AND completes all mandatory modules.
BR-004
Max Attempts: Once attempts_used = max_attempts, the employee is blocked from retaking the quiz and the enrollment status is set to failed. HR admin can reset attempts.
BR-005
Certificate Expiry: A scheduled job runs daily; certificates approaching expiry within 30 days trigger re-enrollment into the same course. On expiry, is_valid is set to false.
BR-006
Minimum Module Completion: Course completion requires that all mandatory modules (course_modules.is_mandatory = true) have progress_percent = 100 before the final quiz is unlocked.
BR-007
Recurrence: Courses with recurrence_months set auto-create a new enrollment after recurrence_months from completed_at. The previous enrollment and certificate are archived but not deleted.
BR-008
Published Only: Employees can only enroll in courses with status = published. Draft or archived courses are invisible to learners.
BR-009
Duplicate Enrollment Prevention: A learner cannot have two active (non-expired, non-completed) enrollments for the same course simultaneously.
BR-010
Training Request Approval Chain: A training request requires sequential approval: Line Manager first, then HR. Rejection at either stage closes the request. Budget approval requires HR-level sign-off.

● 5.2.1   Module Overview

The Invoices & Billing module manages the complete outbound invoicing lifecycle for Jeshan Labs — from client onboarding, invoice creation, and dispatch through payment recording, partial-payment tracking, credit note issuance, and automated overdue management. ● 5.2.4   Business Rules
BR-001
Invoice Number Sequence: Invoice IDs are system-generated in strictly sequential format JL-INV-XXXXX (zero-padded 5-digit sequence per financial year). Manual overriding of invoice IDs is prohibited. The sequence resets each financial year (1 April).
BR-002
Overdue Auto-Status: A scheduled job runs nightly. Any invoice with status = sent or partially_paid whose due_date < CURRENT_DATE is automatically transitioned to overdue status.
BR-003
Credit Note Balance Reduction: When a credit note with status = applied is linked to an invoice, the invoice's balance_due is reduced by the credit note amount. If balance_due reaches ≤ 0, the invoice status transitions to paid.
BR-004
Partial Payment Tracking: Each payment entry increments amount_paid. When amount_paid > 0 but balance_due > 0, status is partially_paid. When balance_due = 0, status transitions to paid and paid_at is recorded.
BR-005
GST / Tax Calculation: Tax is calculated at line-item level using the associated tax_rates entry active at invoice_date. tax_amount at header level is the sum of all line-level taxes. Tax rates cannot be retroactively changed on issued invoices.
BR-006
Recurring Invoice Auto-Generation: The scheduler generates a new invoice from the configured template on next_invoice_date, increments next_invoice_date by the frequency period, and notifies the Finance team. Errors are logged and Finance is alerted.
BR-007
Invoice Lock After Sending: Once status = sent, line items, subtotal, and tax amounts are locked. Only notes, status, and payment-related fields can be updated. A correction requires cancellation and re-issue with a credit note.
BR-008
Credit Limit Check: When creating an invoice, the system checks whether the client's total outstanding balance (open invoices) plus the new invoice total would exceed credit_limit. If so, a warning is displayed; Finance manager approval is required to proceed.

Module 5.3

Payroll Management

End-to-end monthly payroll — salary structures, computation, approval workflow, statutory compliance & Form 16
Doc ID: JL-FSD-005-03
Status: Active
● 5.3.1   Module Overview

The Payroll Management module automates the full monthly payroll run for Jeshan Labs — from salary structure assignment and payroll cycle initiation through multi-level approval (HR → Finance → CFO), bank file generation, payslip distribution, and statutory compliance reporting. It handles PF, ESI, TDS (Section 192), and Professional Tax, and produces Form 16 annually. It integrates with the Leave module for LOP deductions and the HR module for new joiner / exit (FFS) processing.

JL-PCY
Payroll Cycle
JL-PSL
Payslip
JL-SSL
Salary Structure
JL-RMB
Reimbursement
JL-FFS
Full & Final Sett.
● 5.3.2   Payroll Approval Workflow
Draft
→
Processing
HR Initiates
→
Review
HR Review
→
Approved
Finance + CFO
→
Paid
Bank File
→
Locked
Rejection path: Finance or CFO may reject at the Approved stage, returning the cycle to Review with mandatory rejection remarks. HR must resolve corrections and resubmit. A cycle rejected after bank-file generation is blocked — CFO must authorise a reversal before resubmission.

US-PAY-009Line ManagerApprove or reject my direct reports' reimbursement claims before payroll cutoffOnly legitimate claims are included in the payroll disbursementShould Have
US-PAY-010HR / FinanceGenerate a Full & Final Settlement calculation for a departing employee including gratuity, encashment, and recoveriesExit settlements are accurate, auditable, and processed without delayMust Have
● 5.3.5   Business Rules
BR-001
One Cycle Per Month: A UNIQUE constraint on (month, year) in payroll_cycles prevents duplicate cycles. A new cycle cannot be initiated if the current month's cycle is in any active state.
BR-002
Payroll Lock: Once the bank file is generated (bank_file_generated_at is set), the cycle is locked. No edits to payroll_records are permitted. Any corrections require a supplementary next-cycle adjustment.
BR-003
LOP Integration: Approved leave-without-pay days are fetched from the Leave module at payroll initiation time and stored in payroll_records.lop_days. Unapproved pending leaves are excluded.
BR-004
PF Wage Ceiling: Employee and employer PF contributions are calculated at 12% of the PF wage. PF wage is capped at ₹15,000/month (i.e., max employee PF = ₹1,800/month).
BR-005
ESI Wage Ceiling: ESI (Employee: 0.75%, Employer: 3.25%) applies only when the employee's gross monthly salary is ≤ ₹21,000. Employees above this threshold are not covered.
BR-006
TDS Section 192: Monthly TDS is computed as (annual projected taxable income − deductions under Chapter VI-A) / 12, applying the applicable income-tax slabs. TDS is recalculated monthly as declarations change.
BR-007
Salary Structure Effective Date: Salary revisions take effect on the 1st of the configured month. Mid-month revisions are not permitted; any revision entered with a mid-month date is automatically adjusted to the 1st of the following month.
BR-008
Approval Sequence: The cycle must follow the sequence HR Submit → Finance Approve → CFO Approve strictly. Finance cannot bypass HR submission; CFO cannot approve before Finance.
BR-009
Reimbursement Cutoff: Reimbursements must be submitted and approved by the 20th of the month to be included in the same month's payroll. Claims approved after the cutoff are held to the following cycle.
BR-010
Gratuity: Gratuity is payable only if the employee has completed ≥ 5 years of continuous service. Formula: (last drawn basic × 15/26) × years of service. Fractional service above 6 months is rounded up to a full year.

Module 5.4

Analytics Dashboard

Cross-module analytics, configurable dashboards, custom report builder, scheduled reports & ML-based predictive insights
Doc ID: JL-FSD-005-04
Status: Active
● 5.4.1   Module Overview

The Analytics Dashboard module provides a unified data-intelligence layer across all Jeshan Labs portal modules — HR, Finance, Projects, IT, Learning, and Operations. It exposes a configurable drag-and-drop dashboard builder, a custom ad-hoc report builder, scheduled report delivery, and ML-powered predictive insights (attrition risk, project delay prediction). Data is served from a read replica refreshed every 15 minutes to keep production transactional performance unaffected.

● 5.4.2   Technical Architecture
📄 Data Sources
  • HR Module (employees, leaves, exits)
  • Payroll Module (cycles, payslips, TDS)
  • Finance / Invoicing (invoices, revenue)
  • Projects Module (timelines, milestones)
  • IT Module (tickets, SLA)
  • LMS (completions, compliance)
⚡ Processing Layer
  • PostgreSQL streaming replication → Read Replica
  • 15-minute materialized view refresh scheduler
  • ML scoring service (weekly re-train)
  • Async report generation queue (BullMQ)
  • PDF/Excel/CSV export engine
🌎 Delivery Layer
  • REST API with role-based data scoping
  • WebSocket push for dashboard widget refresh
  • Scheduled report email delivery (SMTP)
  • In-App notifications for async exports
  • 90-day signed URL file storage

● 5.4.6   Business Rules
BR-001
15-Minute Read Replica Refresh: All analytics queries are routed to the read replica. Materialized views for computed KPIs are refreshed every 15 minutes via a scheduler. Live transactional data is never queried directly.
BR-002
Role-Based Data Scoping: All API responses are filtered to data the requesting user's role is authorised to see (e.g., a Manager sees only their team's data; Finance sees company-wide financials; Employees see only their own data).
BR-003
Dashboard Ownership: A user may only edit or delete dashboards they created. Shared dashboards are read-only for non-owners. System-default dashboards (is_default = true) cannot be edited or deleted by any user — only Analytics Admins.
BR-004
Scheduled Report Retry: Failed scheduled report executions are retried 3 times. After 3 failures the schedule is auto-paused and the owner and Analytics Admin receive an email alert. Manual re-activation is required.
BR-005
Export Row Limits: CSV/Excel exports are capped at 100,000 rows. PDF table exports are capped at 1,000 rows. Requests exceeding these limits trigger an async export job; the user is notified via In-App notification when ready.
BR-006
Weekly ML Model Re-scoring: The attrition risk and project delay risk ML models re-score their respective entity populations every Monday at 02:00. Scores are stored and compared against prior week; significant movement triggers an alert.
BR-007
Widget Refresh Rate Minimum: Dashboard widgets cannot be configured with a refresh interval below 5 minutes. Attempting to set a lower interval returns a validation error. Widget-level refresh frequency must not exceed the underlying materialized view refresh rate.
BR-008
Report File 90-Day Retention: All generated export files (scheduled and on-demand) are stored in object storage with a 90-day lifecycle policy. After 90 days, files are automatically deleted and the file_url is nulled. Users must re-run the report to regenerate.
BR-009
Report Filter Limits: A saved report cannot have more than 20 filter clauses or more than 50 output columns. These limits prevent runaway queries on the read replica. Requests exceeding limits are rejected at the API validation layer.
BR-010
Analytics Replica Failure Escalation: If the read replica health check fails for more than 5 consecutive minutes, an automated CRITICAL alert is sent to the Platform Engineering team and the Analytics Admin. All dashboard KPI endpoints return a graceful "Data temporarily unavailable — refreshing" response.

Section 6

Platform Services

Core platform capabilities — user identity, access control, master data governance, document library, audit integrity, notifications, and leave management — underpinning every business module of the Jeshan Labs Internal Company Portal.

6.1 User Management

Complete user lifecycle management — creation, authentication, session control, MFA, and account security

ID: JL-USR-XXXXX

Overview

The User Management module manages the complete lifecycle of portal user accounts. It provides secure authentication (username/password + optional MFA), session token management, account lockout, password policies, and administrative tools including bulk import and user impersonation. Every user is assigned a system-generated unique identifier in the format JL-USR-XXXXX (sequential, zero-padded to 5 digits). Users may be linked to an employee_id for HR integration but system/admin accounts may exist without an employee record.

6.1.3 Business Rules

Authentication & Security
  • Email is unique, lowercase-normalised, immutable after creation (admin can override with approval workflow)
  • Password hashed with bcrypt cost factor 12; minimum 8 characters, at least 1 uppercase, 1 digit, 1 special character
  • 5 consecutive failed attempts → account locked for 30 minutes; 10 attempts → locked until admin unlock
  • JWT access token: 8-hour expiry; refresh token: 7-day expiry (rotation on each refresh)
  • CSRF token required on every mutating HTTP request (POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE)
  • MFA backup codes: 8 codes per user — each single-use, bcrypt-hashed
  • Max 3 concurrent active sessions per user; oldest session invalidated on 4th login
Password Policies & Data
  • Password history: last 5 passwords cannot be reused (stored as bcrypt hashes)
  • Password expiry: 90 days; warning email sent at 7 days before expiry
  • Password reset OTP expires in 15 minutes and is single-use
  • New login from unknown device/IP triggers a new device alert email
  • Soft-delete only: is_deleted=TRUE set; data never purged from database
  • Impersonation sessions are flagged (is_impersonated=TRUE in session) and every action is double-audit-logged
  • Bulk CSV import: maximum 500 rows per file; duplicate emails skipped with error report

6.2 Role-Based Access Control (RBAC) & Permissions

Hierarchical role and permission management with user-level overrides, temporary assignments, and Row-Level Security (RLS) policies

Access Control

Overview

The RBAC module implements a fine-grained, cascaded permission model. Permissions are defined at the module × action level (e.g., EMP:CREATE, LEAVE:APPROVE). They are grouped into Roles, which are assigned to Users. Individual user-level overrides (grant or deny) take precedence over role permissions. All permission checks are enforced via API middleware on every authenticated request. PostgreSQL Row-Level Security (RLS) policies, driven by the data_access_policies table, ensure data scoping at the database layer.

6.2.1 Database Schema

Table roles Core
ColumnData TypeConstraints / Notes
idBIGSERIALPRIMARY KEY
role_codeVARCHAR(50)UNIQUE NOT NULL — e.g., SUPER_ADMIN, IT_ADMIN, HR_ADMIN, FINANCE_ADMIN, MANAGER, EMPLOYEE, AUDITOR; custom roles also allowed
role_nameVARCHAR(100)NOT NULL — display name
descriptionTEXTNULLABLE
is_systemBOOLEANDEFAULT FALSE — system roles cannot be deleted or have role_code modified
is_activeBOOLEANDEFAULT TRUE
created_byBIGINTFK → users(id) NULLABLE
created_atTIMESTAMPTZDEFAULT NOW()
updated_atTIMESTAMPTZDEFAULT NOW()
System Roles (seeded): SUPER_ADMIN IT_ADMIN HR_ADMIN FINANCE_ADMIN MANAGER EMPLOYEE AUDITOR
Table permissions Core
ColumnData TypeConstraints / Notes
idBIGSERIALPRIMARY KEY
perm_codeVARCHAR(100)UNIQUE NOT NULL — format MODULE:ACTION, e.g., EMP:CREATE, LEAVE:APPROVE, PAYROLL:EXPORT
module_codeVARCHAR(50)NOT NULL — e.g., USR, EMP, LEAVE, PAYROLL, ASSET
sub_module_codeVARCHAR(50)NULLABLE — for granular sub-module permissions
action_typeVARCHAR(20)CHECK IN ('CREATE','READ','UPDATE','DELETE','EXPORT','IMPORT','APPROVE') NOT NULL
descriptionTEXTNULLABLE — human-readable description
is_activeBOOLEANDEFAULT TRUE
Approximately 300 permission records seeded at installation (module × action matrix). Inactive permissions block all assignments.
Table role_permissions Junction
ColumnData TypeConstraints / Notes
idBIGSERIALPRIMARY KEY
role_idBIGINTFK → roles(id) ON DELETE CASCADE NOT NULL
perm_idBIGINTFK → permissions(id) ON DELETE CASCADE NOT NULL
granted_byBIGINTFK → users(id) NOT NULL — who assigned this permission to the role
created_atTIMESTAMPTZDEFAULT NOW()
Unique Constraint: UNIQUE(role_id, perm_id) — prevents duplicate role-permission mappings.
Table user_roles Junction
ColumnData TypeConstraints / Notes
idBIGSERIALPRIMARY KEY
user_idBIGINTFK → users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE NOT NULL
role_idBIGINTFK → roles(id) ON DELETE CASCADE NOT NULL
assigned_byBIGINTFK → users(id) NOT NULL
valid_fromTIMESTAMPTZNOT NULL — DEFAULT NOW(); supports future-dated assignments
valid_toTIMESTAMPTZNULLABLE — NULL = permanent; set for temporary role assignments
is_activeBOOLEANDEFAULT TRUE — auto-set FALSE by nightly job when valid_to passed
created_atTIMESTAMPTZDEFAULT NOW()
Table user_permission_overrides
ColumnData TypeConstraints / Notes
idBIGSERIALPRIMARY KEY
user_idBIGINTFK → users(id) NOT NULL
perm_idBIGINTFK → permissions(id) NOT NULL
override_typeVARCHAR(10)CHECK IN ('grant', 'deny') NOT NULL
reasonTEXTNOT NULL — mandatory justification for override
assigned_byBIGINTFK → users(id) NOT NULL
valid_untilTIMESTAMPTZNULLABLE — overrides auto-expire
created_atTIMESTAMPTZDEFAULT NOW()
Table permission_groups
ColumnData TypeConstraints / Notes
idBIGSERIALPRIMARY KEY
group_nameVARCHAR(100)UNIQUE NOT NULL
descriptionTEXTNULLABLE
permissionsJSONBNOT NULL — array of perm_codes e.g. ["EMP:CREATE","EMP:READ","EMP:UPDATE"]
is_activeBOOLEANDEFAULT TRUE
created_atTIMESTAMPTZDEFAULT NOW()
Permission groups are convenience bundles used in the admin UI for faster role configuration; they do not replace role_permissions rows.
Table data_access_policies RLS
ColumnData TypeConstraints / Notes
idBIGSERIALPRIMARY KEY
role_idBIGINTFK → roles(id) NOT NULL
module_codeVARCHAR(50)NOT NULL
scopeVARCHAR(20)CHECK IN ('own', 'team', 'department', 'all') NOT NULL
conditionsJSONBNULLABLE — additional filter conditions applied to RLS policy
is_activeBOOLEANDEFAULT TRUE
Drives PostgreSQL Row-Level Security policies on sensitive tables. A DB function get_user_scope(user_id, module_code) resolves the effective scope at query time using the current DB session's app.current_user_id setting.
Table ip_restrictions
ColumnData TypeConstraints / Notes
idBIGSERIALPRIMARY KEY
role_idBIGINTFK → roles(id) ON DELETE CASCADE NOT NULL
allowed_ipsJSONBNOT NULL — array of CIDR notations, e.g. ["192.168.1.0/24","10.0.0.5/32"]
is_activeBOOLEANDEFAULT TRUE
When an active IP restriction exists for a user's role, all requests from disallowed IPs receive 403 Forbidden. Multiple roles = union of allowed CIDRs.

6.2.2 User Stories

#As a…I want to…Acceptance Criteria (Key)
US-6.2.1IT AdminCreate a new custom role with a unique role_code and descriptionRole appears in role assignment UI; is_system = FALSE; deletable
US-6.2.2IT AdminAssign or remove specific permissions from a roleChanges take effect within 60 seconds (cache TTL); audit log written for each change
US-6.2.3IT AdminAssign a role to a userUser immediately gains role permissions; email notification sent; role visible in user profile
US-6.2.4IT AdminAssign a role temporarily with a valid_to dateRole auto-expires at valid_to (nightly job + real-time middleware check); expiry notification sent
US-6.2.5IT AdminGrant or deny an individual permission to a specific userOverride visible in effective-permissions resolver; mandatory reason captured; deny overrides any role grant
US-6.2.6User / AdminView my effective permissions — the net result after roles and overridesAPI returns flat permission list; clearly labels source (role vs. override) and expiry if applicable
US-6.2.7IT AdminConfigure data access scope (own/team/department/all) per role per moduleRLS policy updated within 5 min; incorrect scoping tested via integration tests
US-6.2.8IT AdminRestrict portal access for a role to specific IP ranges (CIDR)Login/API calls from outside CIDR return 403; restriction editable; supports IPv4 and IPv6

6.2.3 Business Rules

Permission Resolution (Cascaded)
  1. User-level DENY override — immediately blocks access, regardless of roles
  2. User-level GRANT override — grants access even if no role covers it
  3. Role permissions — union of all active, non-expired role permissions assigned to the user
  4. Default DENY — if no grant found at any level
  • SUPER_ADMIN bypasses all permission checks; RLS scope is always 'all'
  • System roles (is_system=TRUE) cannot be deleted or have their role_code changed
  • Inactive permissions block new assignments; existing assignments treated as denied
Enforcement & Expiry
  • Permission check on every API call via Express/NestJS middleware (after JWT validation)
  • Effective permissions are cached in Redis with 60-second TTL; invalidated on any role or override change
  • Temporary roles are evaluated real-time in middleware (checking valid_to); a nightly scheduled job also sets is_active=FALSE
  • User-level overrides expire automatically (valid_until) and are re-evaluated per request
  • RLS is enforced at the database layer, not just application layer — cannot be bypassed by direct DB queries from app_rw DB role
  • All role/permission changes are audit-logged with before/after values

6.3 Master Data

Centralised reference data management — categories, leave types, holiday calendars, system configurations, vendors, and notification templates

Reference Data

Overview

The Master Data module manages all reference/lookup data that drives dropdowns, validations, and configurations across the portal. It follows a category-value model (master_categories → master_values) for general-purpose reference data, with dedicated tables for domain-specific entities (leave types, holidays, system configs, vendors). System-seeded values are protected from deletion; all values support i18n display names via JSONB.

6.3.1 Database Schema

display_nameVARCHAR(150)NOT NULL
descriptionTEXTNULLABLE

is_activeBOOLEANDEFAULT TRUE
Seeded categories (~20): DEPARTMENT_TYPE, EMPLOYMENT_TYPE, GRADE_BAND, JOB_CATEGORY, GENDER, MARITAL_STATUS, BLOOD_GROUP, COUNTRY, STATE, CURRENCY, TICKET_CATEGORY, ASSET_CATEGORY, DOCUMENT_TYPE, VENDOR_CATEGORY, SKILL_CATEGORY, PAY_COMPONENT, BANK_ACCOUNT_TYPE, RELATION_TYPE, ID_PROOF_TYPE, LANGUAGE

6.3.2 User Stories

#As a…I want to…Acceptance Criteria (Key)
US-6.3.1IT/HR AdminAdd or edit master values under a categorySystem values cannot be deleted; value_code unique within category; sort_order editable
US-6.3.2IT AdminImport master values from CSV for bulk populationDuplicate value_codes skipped with error; validation report returned; system values not overwritten
US-6.3.3HR AdminCreate and manage leave types with their rulesAt least one active leave type required; changing max_days creates a leave_policy version; auto-recalculate balances on rule change
US-6.3.4HR AdminManage the holiday calendar for a given yearImport from CSV or manual entry; national/state/company holiday types; used in leave day-count calculation
US-6.3.5IT AdminUpdate system configuration values via the admin panelNon-editable configs blocked; value type-validated before save; change audit-logged; Redis cache refreshed within 30 sec
US-6.3.6Finance AdminOnboard a new vendor through an approval workflowStatus starts as pending_approval; approver notified; GST/PAN validated format; blacklisted vendors blocked from future transactions

6.3.3 Business Rules

  • System-protected values: is_system=TRUE records cannot be deleted; deactivation only
  • i18n support: display_name_i18n JSONB provides locale-based labels; fallback to display_name if locale not found
  • Holiday calendar: Maintained per calendar year; new year calendar must be loaded before fiscal year start; optional holidays not auto-excluded from leave calculations
  • System configs: All values type-validated on save; number types validated as finite positive integers/decimals; boolean must be "true"/"false" string
  • Vendor approval workflow: Finance Admin is the designated approver; approval logs captured; blacklisted vendors blocked from PO/invoice creation in all modules
  • Leave type immutability: Changing max_days_per_year or is_carry_forward on an active leave type creates a versioned leave_policy entry; existing balances for the current year are not retroactively altered

6.4 Document Library

Centralised corporate knowledge base — policies, SOPs, handbooks, forms, announcements — with version control, acknowledgment tracking, and full-text search

ID: JL-LIB-XXXXX

Overview

The Document Library provides a structured repository for all organisational documents. Documents progress through a draft → published → archived/expired lifecycle. Access control is role- and department-based. Mandatory acknowledgment requirements are tracked per employee per version, with automatic re-acknowledgment prompts when a document is updated. Full-text search is powered by a PostgreSQL tsvector index updated via trigger within 5 minutes of content change.

6.4.1 Database Schema

Table library_categories

review_dateDATENULLABLE — reminder triggered 30 days before
expiry_dateDATENULLABLE — status auto-set to expired by scheduler
requires_acknowledgmentBOOLEANDEFAULT FALSE

target_departmentsJSONBNULLABLE — array of department_ids; NULL = all departments

6.4.2 User Stories

#As a…I want to…Acceptance Criteria (Key)
US-6.4.1HR/IT AdminUpload a document and publish it to target roles/departmentsDraft created on upload; published only by authorised user; file max 25MB; restricted types only; acknowledgment flag configurable

US-6.4.7HR AdminSet a review date and be reminded to review the documentEmail reminder 30 days before review_date; document auto-flagged as needing review; no auto-archival without admin action

6.4.3 Business Rules

  • Lifecycle: Draft → Published → Archived (manual) or Expired (auto on expiry_date). No hard delete — archive only.
  • Acknowledgment gate: Employee must open and scroll to end of document before the "Acknowledge" button activates (tracked via frontend event; verified server-side via views_count increment)
  • Version re-acknowledgment: When a new version is published, all prior acknowledgments for the same doc are superseded; all target users receive re-acknowledgment prompt within 24 hours
  • File restrictions: Allowed types: PDF, DOCX, XLSX, PPTX, TXT. Maximum file size: 25 MB. Files stored in S3-compatible object storage; access via time-limited pre-signed URLs (15 min TTL)
  • Search index: Updated asynchronously within 5 minutes via a BullMQ job; weighted ranking (title > description > body text)
  • Category access_roles: NULL = all authenticated roles can access. Non-NULL = only listed roles. Documents inherit category restrictions (plus their own target_roles filter).

6.5 Audit Logs

Tamper-proof, append-only audit trail with SHA-256 hash-chain integrity, monthly partitioning, and compliance-grade retention policies

ID: JL-AUD-XXXXX

Overview

The Audit Log system provides an immutable, chronological record of all create, update, delete, login, and operational actions performed in the portal. Integrity is enforced via a SHA-256 hash chain — each log entry includes the SHA-256 hash of the previous entry, making retroactive modification detectable. Records are append-only: the app_rw database role is explicitly denied UPDATE and DELETE on audit_logs. Monthly range partitioning ensures query performance across large data volumes. Sensitive field values are [REDACTED] before being written to old_values/new_values.

Design Principles
Append-Only No UPDATE/DELETE on audit_logs — enforced at DB-role level
Hash-Chain SHA-256 chain across ordered records — tampering breaks chain
Partitioned Monthly range partitions on created_at for query efficiency
Synchronous Written in same DB transaction as the triggering operation
Field Masking PII/sensitive fields replaced with [REDACTED] in old/new values
Retention Module-specific policies; financial records retained 7 years

6.5.1 Database Schema

Table audit_logs Append-Only Partitioned Monthly
ColumnData TypeConstraints / Notes
idBIGSERIALPRIMARY KEY (composite with partition key)
log_idVARCHAR(20)UNIQUE NOT NULL — JL-AUD-XXXXX
user_idBIGINTFK → users(id) NULLABLE — NULL for system-generated events
user_emailVARCHAR(255)NOT NULL — denormalized; preserved if user later deleted
user_nameVARCHAR(200)NOT NULL — denormalized display name at time of action
session_idVARCHAR(255)NULLABLE — session token hash reference
action_typeVARCHAR(20)CHECK IN ('CREATE','UPDATE','DELETE','LOGIN','LOGOUT','EXPORT','IMPORT','APPROVE','REJECT') NOT NULL
module_codeVARCHAR(50)NOT NULL — e.g., USR, EMP, PAYROLL, LEAVE
sub_module_codeVARCHAR(50)NULLABLE
record_idVARCHAR(50)NULLABLE — business key of the affected record (e.g., JL-EMP-00042)
record_typeVARCHAR(100)NULLABLE — table name or entity type
old_valuesJSONBNULLABLE — previous state; sensitive fields replaced with "[REDACTED]"
new_valuesJSONBNULLABLE — new state after change; same masking rules
changed_fieldsJSONBNULLABLE — array of field names that changed, for UPDATE events
ip_addressINETNULLABLE
user_agentTEXTNULLABLE
request_idUUIDNOT NULL — correlates with application request trace ID
statusVARCHAR(20)CHECK IN ('success','failure') NOT NULL
error_messageTEXTNULLABLE — for status=failure events
additional_contextJSONBNULLABLE — module-specific context (e.g., approval decision reason)
hashVARCHAR(64)NOT NULL — SHA-256 of canonicalized row data including previous_hash
previous_hashVARCHAR(64)NULLABLE — hash of preceding audit record (NULL for first record)
created_atTIMESTAMPTZNOT NULL — partition key (RANGE partitioning by month)
Indexes: idx_audit_user_id_created, idx_audit_module_code, idx_audit_action_type, idx_audit_record_id, idx_audit_request_id, GIN on changed_fields
DB Constraint: GRANT INSERT ON audit_logs TO app_rw; REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE ON audit_logs FROM app_rw;
Tableaudit_log_archive
ColumnNotes
Identical schema to audit_logs. Records are moved here from audit_logs partitions after archive_after_months has elapsed. Archived records remain queryable via the audit log API (transparent union). Hash-chain integrity is maintained across the move.
Tableaudit_retention_policies
ColumnType / Notes
idBIGSERIAL PK
module_codeVARCHAR(50) UNIQUE NOT NULL
retention_monthsSMALLINT NOT NULL — PAYROLL/EXPENSE: 84 (7yr); HR: 36 (3yr); default: 24 (2yr)
archive_after_monthsSMALLINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 12 — move from hot to archive partition after N months
is_activeBOOLEAN DEFAULT TRUE

6.5.2 User Stories

#As a…I want to…Acceptance Criteria (Key)
US-6.5.1Auditor / AdminFilter audit logs by module, action type, user, date range, and statusDate range is mandatory (max 90-day window per query); pagination; results within 3 sec for 12-month range
US-6.5.2AuditorExport filtered audit logs to PDF or ExcelExport includes all filtered columns; file generated async for >5,000 rows; download link emailed; requires AUDIT:EXPORT permission
US-6.5.3Auditor / AdminView before/after diff for any UPDATE eventSide-by-side comparison of old_values vs new_values; changed_fields highlighted; masked fields shown as [REDACTED]
US-6.5.4IT AdminConfigure retention policy per moduleCannot set retention_months below legal minimum (PAYROLL: 84); changes audit-logged; next archival job respects new policy
US-6.5.5AuditorRun an integrity verification report to confirm hash-chain has not been tampered withReport re-computes hashes for a date range and flags any broken links; completion within 5 min for 1-year range; result exportable
US-6.5.6Compliance OfficerGenerate a compliance summary report for a module and date rangeShows action counts by type, top users, failure rates; PDF export; date range mandatory; requires AUDIT:READ permission

6.5.3 Business Rules

  • Immutability: No UPDATE or DELETE statements may be issued against audit_logs by any application code or app_rw DB role. Enforced at PostgreSQL level via role permissions.
  • Synchronous writes: Audit log entries are written within the same database transaction as the operation being audited. If the audit write fails, the parent operation is rolled back.
  • Sensitive field masking: Fields password_hash, mfa_secret, token_hash, card_number, account_number, salary and others defined in the masking config are replaced with "[REDACTED]" before JSONB serialisation.
  • Financial records: PAYROLL and EXPENSE module audit records retained for a minimum of 84 months (7 years) per Indian Companies Act requirements.
  • Date range mandatory: All audit log queries via API must include from_date and to_date filter (max 90-day window). No unbounded full-table scans via API.
  • Access control: AUDIT:READ permission required to view any audit logs. Users without this permission cannot access any audit data, even their own actions.

6.5.4 API Endpoints

MethodPathDescriptionPermission
GET/api/v1/audit-logsList audit logs (paginated, date-range mandatory)AUDIT:READ
GET/api/v1/audit-logs/:logIdGet single audit log entry with before/after diffAUDIT:READ
POST/api/v1/audit-logs/exportAsync export of filtered logs to PDF or ExcelAUDIT:EXPORT
POST/api/v1/audit-logs/compliance-reportGenerate compliance summary report for module + date rangeAUDIT:READ
POST/api/v1/audit-logs/integrity-checkRun hash-chain integrity verification for a date rangeAUDIT:READ + IT_ADMIN
GET/api/v1/audit/retention-policiesList retention policies for all modulesAUDIT:READ
