6.6 Notifications System

Real-time in-app, email, and SMS notifications with per-user preferences and daily digest support

ID: JL-NTF-XXXXX

Overview

The Notifications system delivers contextual alerts to portal users across three channels: in-app (real-time via WebSocket/SSE), email (transactional HTML), and SMS (critical security events only). Users control their notification preferences per notification type and channel. A daily digest bundles low-priority notifications for users who prefer reduced interruptions. In-app notifications are pruned after 90 days. Unread count is surfaced in the portal's bell icon via a lightweight polling endpoint or WebSocket push.

🔔
In-App

Real-time via WebSocket / SSE. Bell icon counter. 90-day retention. Soft-delete (mark read).

📧
Email

Branded HTML + plain text. Delivered via SES/SMTP. Supports daily digest batching.

📱
SMS

Critical security events only (OTP, account lockout). Sent via Twilio/SNS. Cannot be disabled for security events.

6.6.1 Database Schema

Table notifications Partitioned Monthly
ColumnData TypeConstraints / Notes
idBIGSERIALPRIMARY KEY (composite with partition key)
notif_idVARCHAR(20)UNIQUE NOT NULL — JL-NTF-XXXXX
recipient_idBIGINTFK → users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE NOT NULL
sender_idBIGINTFK → users(id) NULLABLE — NULL for system-generated notifications
notification_typeVARCHAR(100)NOT NULL — e.g., LEAVE_APPROVED, ACCOUNT_LOCKED, DOC_ACKNOWLEDGE_REQUIRED
titleVARCHAR(255)NOT NULL — short notification heading
messageTEXTNOT NULL — notification body
action_urlTEXTNULLABLE — deep link URL for the relevant portal page
module_codeVARCHAR(50)NULLABLE — source module
record_idVARCHAR(50)NULLABLE — related record business key
is_readBOOLEANDEFAULT FALSE
read_atTIMESTAMPTZNULLABLE — set when user opens or explicitly marks read
created_atTIMESTAMPTZNOT NULL DEFAULT NOW() — partition key
Records older than 90 days purged by nightly cleanup job. Indexes: idx_notif_recipient_is_read (composite), idx_notif_created_at, idx_notif_notification_type
Table notification_preferences
ColumnData TypeConstraints / Notes
idBIGSERIALPRIMARY KEY
user_idBIGINTFK → users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE NOT NULL
notification_typeVARCHAR(100)NOT NULL
channelVARCHAR(20)CHECK IN ('in_app','email','sms') NOT NULL
is_enabledBOOLEANDEFAULT TRUE — users can disable email/in_app; SMS security events cannot be disabled
digest_frequencyVARCHAR(20)CHECK IN ('immediate','daily','weekly') DEFAULT 'immediate'
Unique Constraint: UNIQUE(user_id, notification_type, channel). Default preferences are auto-created on user registration based on a system defaults template.

6.6.2 Business Rules

  • Security SMS cannot be disabled: MFA OTPs, account lockout, and password reset SMS are always delivered regardless of user preferences
  • Daily digest: Notifications with digest_frequency=daily are batched and delivered at 08:00 IST via a scheduled job; immediate ones are delivered in real-time
  • 90-day pruning: In-app notifications older than 90 days are permanently deleted by nightly batch; read/unread status not preserved post-pruning
  • Real-time delivery: WebSocket (authenticated persistent connection) for active sessions; SSE as fallback for clients that do not support WebSocket
  • Unread count caching: Unread count per user cached in Redis; invalidated on mark-read events

6.7 Leave Management

Employee leave application, 2-level approval workflow, balance tracking, compensatory off, and annual carry-forward management

ID: JL-LEV-XXXXX

Overview

The Leave Management module allows employees to apply for leave, track their balances by leave type, view a leave calendar, and manage compensatory-off credits. Managers approve leaves at L1 (direct manager). Earned Leave applications exceeding 3 days require additional L2 approval (HR). LOP (Loss of Pay) is auto-triggered when a requested leave type has zero balance. Annual balance refresh credits opening balances, applies carry-forward rules, and lapses expired credits. Holiday calendars from Master Data are used to correctly compute working-day counts.

Employee: Apply → L1 Manager: Approve / Reject → (EL > 3 days) L2 HR: Approve / Reject → Approved
Cancellation before start date: employee self-service | After start: manager approval required

6.7.1 Database Schema

Table leave_applications Core
ColumnData TypeConstraints / Notes
idBIGSERIALPRIMARY KEY
leave_idVARCHAR(20)UNIQUE NOT NULL — JL-LEV-XXXXX

leave_type_idBIGINTFK → leave_types(id) NOT NULL
from_dateDATENOT NULL
to_dateDATENOT NULL — CHECK(to_date >= from_date)
total_daysDECIMAL(4,1)NOT NULL — computed (excludes holidays + weekends per leave policy)
reasonTEXTNULLABLE — mandatory for ML (maternity), LOP, and backdated applications
statusVARCHAR(20)CHECK IN ('draft','submitted','approved','rejected','cancelled','revoked') DEFAULT 'submitted'
approver_l1_idBIGINTFK → users(id) NOT NULL — direct manager auto-resolved at submission
approver_l2_idBIGINTFK → users(id) NULLABLE — assigned for EL > 3 days

l2_commentsTEXTNULLABLE — mandatory when l2_decision = rejected

Indexes: idx_leave_app_employee_id_status, idx_leave_app_from_date_to_date, idx_leave_app_approver_l1_id, idx_leave_app_approver_l2_id
Table leave_balances Core
ColumnData TypeConstraints / Notes
idBIGSERIALPRIMARY KEY
employee_idBIGINTFK → employees(id) NOT NULL
leave_type_idBIGINTFK → leave_types(id) NOT NULL
yearSMALLINTNOT NULL — calendar year
opening_balanceDECIMAL(5,1)DEFAULT 0 NOT NULL

pendingDECIMAL(5,1)DEFAULT 0 NOT NULL — submitted but not yet approved applications

Unique Constraint: UNIQUE(employee_id, leave_type_id, year). Indexes: idx_leave_bal_emp_type_year (UNIQUE), idx_leave_bal_employee_id
Table comp_off_records
ColumnData TypeConstraints / Notes
idBIGSERIALPRIMARY KEY
employee_idBIGINTFK → employees(id) NOT NULL
worked_dateDATENOT NULL — the holiday/weekend the employee worked
reasonTEXTNOT NULL — project/task description for working on holiday
hours_workedDECIMAL(4,1)NOT NULL
comp_off_daysDECIMAL(3,1)NOT NULL — calculated: 0.5 if hours_worked < 4 else 1.0
expiry_dateDATENOT NULL — worked_date + 60 days; CO credits auto-expire if unused
statusVARCHAR(20)CHECK IN ('pending','approved','used','expired')
approved_byBIGINTFK → users(id) NULLABLE — manager who approved the comp-off credit
On approval, a corresponding credit is added to leave_balances for leave_type CO for the current year.

6.7.2 User Stories

#As a…I want to…Acceptance Criteria (Key)
US-6.7.1EmployeeApply for leave specifying type, dates, and reasonBalance checked before submission; overlapping leave detected; backdating limited to 3 days; submission triggers L1 notification
US-6.7.2Manager (L1)Approve or reject a leave application with commentsComment mandatory on rejection; decision updates leave_balances immediately; employee notified; EL > 3 days forwarded to L2
US-6.7.3EmployeeView my leave balances by type for the current yearShows opening, credited, used, pending, closing; downloadable statement
US-6.7.4Employee / ManagerView team leave calendar (monthly/weekly view)Shows approved leaves colour-coded by type; manager sees full team; employee sees own + team's approved leaves only
US-6.7.5EmployeeCancel a leave application before it startsIf submitted/approved: cancellation request sent to manager if from_date ≤ today; auto-cancelled if future; balance restored on cancellation
US-6.7.6EmployeeApply for compensatory off for working on a holiday/weekendworked_date must be a holiday or weekend; manager approves; comp_off_days credited to CO balance; expiry 60 days from worked_date
US-6.7.7HR AdminPerform annual balance refresh for all employees at year startCredits leave entitlements per leave_policy; applies carry-forward rules; lapses excess; generates refresh summary report; bulk job async
US-6.7.8HR AdminOverride an employee's leave balance (add/subtract days)Mandatory reason; override logged to audit; reflected in balance immediately; notification sent to employee

6.7.3 Business Rules

Application Rules
  • Balance check performed at submission time; pending balance is reserved but not yet deducted
  • Backdate maximum 3 calendar days from submission date
  • Overlapping leave applications (same employee, overlapping dates) are rejected at validation
  • Half-day leave allowed for leave types with min_days ≤ 0.5 (from_date = to_date + half_day_type flag)
  • 2-level approval triggered for EL > 3 days; all other types single-level
  • When balance is zero and leave is applied: system auto-converts to LOP (with employee consent prompt)
Computation & Year-End Rules
  • Day calculation: Holidays (from holiday_calendar) and configured non-working days (weekends) are excluded from total_days count
  • Comp-off expiry: CO credits expire 60 days from worked_date; nightly scheduler marks expired records and adjusts CO balance
  • Annual refresh: Run as async bulk job on or after Jan 1 (or fiscal year start); creates leave_balances for the new year; carry-forward respects max_carry_forward_days per leave type
  • Cancellation: Before from_date: employee can self-cancel; after from_date (or on same day): manager approval required for revocation
  • Balance lapse: Unused CL and CO lapse at year end; EL can be carried forward up to max_carry_forward_days

6.9 Seed Data

Initial data required for a functional system at first deployment — roles, permissions, leave types, holidays, configs, and reference data

Installation Required

All seed data is delivered as part of the initial Flyway/Liquibase migration scripts located in /db/migrations/V001__seed_initial_data.sql and subsequent version-specific seed scripts. Seed data is idempotent — re-running migrations does not duplicate records (uses INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING patterns).

6.10 Database Design Principles

Architectural standards and conventions applied consistently across all 38 tables — PostgreSQL 15+, naming, indexing, partitioning, RLS, and migration management

PostgreSQL 15+
Database Technology
  • Database: PostgreSQL 15+ (leverages JSONB, tsvector, RLS, declarative partitioning, logical replication)
  • Primary Keys: BIGSERIAL (auto-incrementing 64-bit integer) on all tables
  • Business Keys: UUID-style VARCHAR in JL-{MODULE}-XXXXX format as secondary unique identifiers exposed in APIs (internal PK never exposed externally)
  • Timestamps: All timestamps use TIMESTAMPTZ (UTC stored, displayed in IST via application layer)
  • Decimals: Monetary/day-count values use DECIMAL(p,s) — never FLOAT
  • Booleans: Native BOOLEAN type; never 0/1 integers
  • JSONB: Used for flexible schema (device_info, permissions arrays, i18n, metadata, config values); GIN indexed where queried
Naming Conventions
  • Tables: snake_case, plural nouns — e.g., leave_applications, user_sessions
  • Columns: snake_case, descriptive — e.g., failed_login_attempts, is_carry_forward
  • Indexes: idx_{table}_{column(s)} — e.g., idx_users_email, idx_leave_app_employee_id_status
  • Foreign Keys: fk_{table}_{referenced_table} — e.g., fk_user_roles_users
  • Unique Constraints: uq_{table}_{column(s)} — e.g., uq_users_email
  • Check Constraints: ck_{table}_{column} — e.g., ck_users_status
  • Sequences: seq_{table}_id (auto-managed by BIGSERIAL)
Standard Column Sets (Applied to All Tables)
Timestamps (ALL tables): created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW() NOT NULL
updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW() NOT NULL
updated_at maintained by a shared trigger function set_updated_at()
Audit Columns (CUD tables — tables with CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE operations): created_by BIGINT FK → users(id)
updated_by BIGINT FK → users(id)
Soft-Delete Columns (tables where data must not be hard-deleted): is_deleted BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE NOT NULL
deleted_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULLABLE
deleted_by BIGINT FK → users(id) NULLABLE
Partial index on is_deleted=FALSE for all filtered queries
Indexing Strategy
  • Every FK column indexed — prevents sequential scans on parent table deletion checks
  • Business key UNIQUE indexes — all JL-XXX-XXXXX columns have a UNIQUE constraint + index
  • Composite indexes for common filter combinations — e.g., (employee_id, status) on leave_applications, (employee_id, leave_type_id, year) on leave_balances
  • Partial indexes on is_deleted=FALSE and is_active=TRUE columns to minimise index bloat on soft-deleted data
  • GIN indexes on all queried JSONB columns — display_name_i18n, changed_fields, access_roles
  • GIN index on document_search_index.search_text (tsvector) for full-text search
  • BRIN indexes on created_at columns of partitioned tables (within each partition)
Partitioning

Strategy: PostgreSQL RANGE partitioning on created_at by calendar month.

Partitioned Tables:

  • audit_logs — append-only, high volume; monthly partitions retained per module retention policy
  • user_login_history — login events; retained 24 months; older partitions detached
  • notifications — in-app notifications; 90-day pruning job drops old partitions

Partition Management: New month partitions are pre-created by a scheduled job on the 25th of each month. Detached partitions are compressed to read-only tablespace before permanent deletion.

Row-Level Security (RLS)
  • Enabled on: HR-sensitive tables (employees, leave_applications, leave_balances) and financial tables (payroll_records, expense_claims)
  • Policy driver: data_access_policies table (Section 6.2) defines scope (own/team/dept/all) per role per module
  • DB function: get_user_scope(p_user_id BIGINT, p_module_code VARCHAR) — returns effective scope for the current DB session
  • Session variable: Application sets SET LOCAL app.current_user_id = '...' at the start of each DB transaction; RLS policies consume this
  • DB role bypass: db_admin role has RLS bypassed (BYPASSRLS privilege) for migrations only; app_rw and app_ro are subject to all RLS policies
Database Roles & Permissions
DB RolePrivilegesUsed By
app_rwSELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE on all tables except audit_logs (INSERT only) and audit_log_archive (INSERT only)Application server (API)
app_roSELECT only on all tables; subject to RLSReporting service, analytics
db_adminSuperuser equivalent for migrations; BYPASSRLS; never used at runtimeFlyway/Liquibase migrations only
Sensitive Data & Connection Pooling
Sensitive Column Encryption:
  • users.mfa_secret — AES-256-GCM encrypted by application layer before INSERT; decrypted only at authentication service layer; encryption key in AWS Secrets Manager / Vault
  • No other columns are application-level encrypted; TLS in transit + AES-256 at-rest (disk encryption) handles remaining data
Connection Pooling:
  • PgBouncer in transaction mode — pool per DB role (app_rw, app_ro)
  • Pool size: 20 connections per app instance (API typically 3 instances = 60 max to PgBouncer)
  • PgBouncer max server connections: 100 total per pool
  • Idle timeout: 600 seconds; server lifetime: 3600 seconds
Migration Management
Tool: Flyway (preferred) or Liquibase — versioned SQL migration scripts.

Location: /db/migrations/ within the monorepo

Naming: V{version}__{description}.sql — e.g., V001__create_users_table.sql, V042__add_comp_off_records.sql

Rollback: Each forward migration paired with a rollback script in /db/rollbacks/U{version}__{description}.sql
Principles:
  • Migrations are idempotent where possible (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS, etc.)
  • Schema changes are backwards-compatible by default — new columns nullable or with defaults; column drops in separate post-deployment migration
  • Large table changes (ADD INDEX, ALTER COLUMN) use pg_try_advisory_xact_lock to avoid table locks in production
  • Seed data delivered in migration scripts using INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING
  • Migration scripts reviewed by senior engineer before deployment; executed by CI/CD pipeline (not manual DBA runs)
Design Principle Summary
PostgreSQL 15+ Full JSONB, tsvector, RLS, partitioning
BIGSERIAL PKs JL-XXX-XXXXX business keys in API
TIMESTAMPTZ All timestamps UTC + IST in app layer
Soft-Delete is_deleted flag; no hard deletes
RLS Enforced HR & financial tables at DB layer
Flyway Migrations Versioned, idempotent, CI/CD deployed
3 DB Roles app_rw / app_ro / db_admin (migration only)
PgBouncer Transaction mode, 20 conn/app instance
AES-256-GCM mfa_secret encrypted at app layer
